   rename that fails stops the sequence; the state is re-derived (Leftover files if a non-empty journal remains) and
   shown.
6. The lock file stays and **stays held**: the process can then create a new Library (LIB-07) without releasing the
   lock, so no other process can slip in between set-aside and creation. End the lease.

Order matters: a journal left without its main file is never opened at the Library path (creation refuses non-empty
leftovers), whereas a main file left at the path without its hot journal would expose uncommitted data as valid
[E-4]. A crash between renames leaves the Leftover files state, whose action finishes the job under a new stem; the
help text says to keep every `library.damaged-*` file together. Set-aside files are never opened again by
StorageInventory. **"Keep a copy" is removed from 1.1.0** (set-aside already preserves every member; REV-H02).

### 6.9 What may delete or rename StorageInventory-owned data (LIB-12)

| Operation | Trigger | Scope | Safeguards |
|---|---|---|---|
| Delete a snapshot (REQ-11) | User, with confirmation | The rows of one **published** snapshot | One `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction (T-DELETE, `FULL`) inside a **Delete** mutation lease: `UPDATE snapshot SET state = 3 WHERE snapshot_id = ? AND state = 2` must change exactly one row; delete that snapshot's `file_obs`, `folder_obs`, `scan_error` and `snapshot_extension_total` rows; delete the snapshot row; commit. Refused, with its reason, unless the interlock is Idle (OBS-07, OBS-11). Other snapshots are never touched. The attempt row stays |
| Record an attempt outcome | Automatic | One `scan_attempt` row of this session | `UPDATE … WHERE attempt_id = ? AND session_token = ? AND outcome = 1` |
| Recovery at open | Automatic, under the writer lock, inside the Open lease | `scan_attempt` rows with `outcome = InProgress` from other sessions | Only the outcome and end time change (CONC-08) |
| Set aside a Library (LIB-13) | User | The Library's member files | Renames only, `overwrite: false`; only under the writer lock and a SetAside lease, after every connection of the process is closed |
| **Never** | — | Deleting any Library file; purging rows of another capture; automatic retention; deleting on incompatibility or corruption; deleting reports or spools of past runs; `VACUUM`, backup or migration in 1.1.0 | — |

Unreferenced `name` and `folder_path` rows left by deletions stay until Compact (SREQ-09, 1.1.x). Their size is
counted in the Library size display.

### 6.10 Concurrency (LIB-10, D-36, replacing D-23)

`c0c031f` used a `Local\` named mutex, which excludes nothing across logon sessions, and verified the snapshot outside
the publish transaction, so a second session's open-time recovery could purge a live capture's rows between
verification and publication (REV-H03). D-36 enforces correctness in the store itself and uses the lock only to give
a clear "In use" state.

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| CONC-01 | **Writer lock.** The process that owns the Library holds `library.lock` open with `FileShare.None` from its first acquisition (at open, LIB-08 step 2, or at creation or set-aside, LIB-07 step 3, LIB-13 step 2) until it exits; it is never released and re-acquired in between. It is acquired **before** any SQL runs and before any Library member is inspected, created or renamed. File sharing modes are enforced by the filesystem for every opener, whatever its process, logon session or account, so the lock excludes other processes in other sessions too. No named kernel object is used (A-15) |
| CONC-02 | **Second instance.** A process that cannot acquire the lock shows **In use**, opens no connection to the database at all, never creates, renames or deletes a Library member, and runs scans without history. Different Windows accounts have different `%LOCALAPPDATA%` and so different Libraries; there is no cross-account sharing. **Limitation L-OBS1:** the interlock (OBS-07) is per process, so a scan run by the second instance can observe the Library while the owning instance mutates it. No snapshot is affected (only the owner publishes, and its own captures are protected by OBS-07 and INV-19); the second instance's reports may record a changing Library folder, as v1's reports can record another instance's report folder today. The In use message says so. An informational change note in the second instance's results is not adopted: it could only rest on listing metadata, which is unreliable while the owner holds files open (G0-M04) |
| CONC-03 | **One writer connection, only inside a mutation lease.** Only the lock holder opens a `Mode=ReadWrite` connection, and only while it holds a mutation lease (OBS-12); the connection is closed before the lease ends. Every write is a `BEGIN IMMEDIATE … COMMIT` transaction with `synchronous = FULL`. SQLite's own file locks (`RESERVED`, then `EXCLUSIVE` at spill and commit) exclude any other writer in any process while it runs |
| CONC-04 | **Publication is one transaction.** Import, verification (§11.8, IMP-05) and publication are one transaction (T-IMPORT). Nothing of the new snapshot is visible to any connection before its `COMMIT`; there is no committed intermediate state to purge, so no other process can make a partially imported snapshot valid. Verification cannot be interleaved with another writer because the transaction holds the database write lock |
| CONC-05 | **Readers.** UI pages use separate `Mode=ReadOnly` connections in the same process. SQLite isolation means they see only committed data. A read-only connection never rolls back a journal (recovery happens at open, LIB-08) |
| CONC-06 | **In-process gate.** `LibrarySession` owns an asynchronous reader/writer gate. A writer acquires it before `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`: new reads wait, and long in-flight reads (a comparison) are cancelled through `SqliteCommand.Cancel` and re-run by their page afterwards. `busy_timeout` is only a backstop |
| CONC-07 | **One capture at a time.** v1 already runs one scan at a time. A capture holds the interlock from its Prepare lease (T0) through its Observing state to the end of its Save lease (outcome recorded), with atomic hand-offs (OBS-10), so a second scan or capture cannot start until the first capture's outcome has been recorded (or its recording has failed, G0-O06) |
| CONC-08 | **Recovery only under the lock.** Open-time recovery runs only while this process holds the writer lock, and only changes `InProgress` attempts whose `session_token` differs from the current session's. Since the lock proves no other live session exists, such attempts belong to dead processes |
| CONC-09 | **Maintenance lockout is the interlock.** Every Library mutation (OBS-08: creation, T0, import, outcome, deletion, open and recovery, set-aside, and any later migration, backup or compaction) runs only inside a mutation lease, and a lease is granted only when the interlock is Idle (or by a capture's own hand-off, OBS-10). So no mutation starts while a window is open or another mutation runs, and no window opens while a mutation runs (OBS-07) |
| CONC-10 | **External writers.** Another program editing the database directly (with the app closed, or without StorageInventory's lock) is outside StorageInventory's control. It still cannot make a partial snapshot valid: publication happens only inside CONC-04's transaction, tampering is detected at open (LIB-08 steps 4 and 6), and every comparison verifies its inputs against their sealed totals (D-47) |

---

## 7. Storage and source identity

This section claims only what Windows evidence supports. Where evidence cannot tell two things apart, it says so, and
the product says so to the user (D-42, superseding D-21).

### 7.1 Concepts (kept separate)

| Concept | Meaning | Is it identity? | Stored in |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Volume identity** | Evidence that two observations were of the same filesystem volume: filesystem type plus serial numbers, with a confidence level | Yes, as strong as its confidence (§7.3) | `volume` |
| **Source identity** | The persistent thing snapshots belong to: a volume identity **plus the root's exact location inside the volume** (`\` for a whole volume, `\Media\Music` for a folder); for network sources, the canonical share root plus the path | Yes. Comparisons are allowed only within one source | `source` |
| **Root path within the volume** | The exact, case-preserving path of the source root below the volume's mount point | Part of source identity, compared **exactly** (ID-02). Its comparison key (NAME-03) is used only to *suggest* a match | `source.root_in_volume` |
| **Observed location** | The drive letter or mount point, and the path as entered, at scan time | **No.** Recorded per snapshot for audit and display | `snapshot` |
| **Display label** | The volume label or a user-given name | **No** (labels change) | `volume`, `source` |
| **Identity confidence and basis** | How strong the evidence was (Strong, Moderate, PathOnly) and what the attachment rested on (Evidence, UserAsserted, Location) | Describes identity; it is not identity | `source`, `snapshot` |

### 7.2 Evidence captured at each scan (read-only)

The evidence comes from a directory handle opened on **the path the scan enumerates** (v1's
`validation.RootFullPath`, the normalised path as entered, `InventoryScanner.cs:50`), like v1's `NativeMethods`
(`desiredAccess = 0`, `FILE_FLAG_BACKUP_SEMANTICS`, share mode read, write and delete). Opening that path resolves any
drive letter, SUBST or mapped drive exactly as the scanner's own listings do, so the handle designates the directory
object the scan starts observing; the canonical root is read **from that handle** (`GetFinalPathNameByHandleW`). For a
capture, that handle is **held for the whole observation window** (ID-10).

| Call | Returns | Probe result (NTFS `C:\`, `c0c031f` task) |
|---|---|---|
| `GetVolumeInformationByHandleW` | Filesystem name, 32-bit serial, label, filesystem flags | `NTFS`, `0xFE561879`, flags `0x03E72EFF`; works with **0 access** |
| `GetFileInformationByHandleEx(FileIdInfo)` | `FILE_ID_INFO`: 64-bit volume serial and 128-bit file ID of the root directory | `VolumeSerialNumber = 0x1AFE5644FE561879`, whose low 32 bits equal the 32-bit serial; works with **0 access** |
| `GetVolumePathNameW` | Mount point of the volume that contains the root | `C:\` |
| `GetDiskFreeSpaceExW` | Capacity and free bytes | (not probed) |
| `GetFinalPathNameByHandleW` on the same handle (v1's existing function) | Canonical root of the opened object: SUBST and mapped drives resolved, UNC as `\\server\share\...` | v1 behaviour |

**Not used for identity:** `GetVolumeNameForVolumeMountPointW` (the `\\?\Volume{GUID}\` name, assigned per machine),
and the root directory's file ID for **whole-volume** sources: on NTFS the root directory is MFT record 5 on every
volume (review [DOC]), so it cannot tell volumes apart (Q-13 records the 128-bit values in C3). For **subfolder**
sources the root folder's file ID is recorded and compared only to say "the source folder was recreated" (§7.6).

### 7.3 Identity confidence (ID-01)

| Level | When | Automatic matching | What it cannot tell apart |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Strong** | NTFS or ReFS, local, with a non-zero 64-bit serial from `FileIdInfo` | Yes, on (filesystem type, 64-bit serial) plus the exact root | **A disk clone or copied VHD that is never mounted at the same time as its original.** Clones copy the serial; nothing StorageInventory reads distinguishes them. Documented limitation L-ID1 (§7.5 ID-05) |
| **Moderate** | FAT, FAT32 or exFAT, local, with a non-zero 32-bit serial | Only if the filesystem type, 32-bit serial, **label and capacity (±1%)** all match exactly one volume. Otherwise the user is asked (ID-04) | Two volumes whose serial, label and capacity all collide (rare; the prompt covers the rest) |
| **PathOnly** | Network sources; UDF or optical media; unknown filesystems; serial unavailable or 0; any evidence call failed | Network: by canonical location (§7.4). Local: **never automatic**; always a new source unless the user explicitly picks an existing one (basis UserAsserted) | Anything served at the same location |

Confidence is keyed by the **filesystem name**, so a FAT volume can never reach Strong even if `FileIdInfo` succeeds on
it (Q-02 records what it returns). The level and basis are stored with every snapshot and shown (ID-09).

### 7.4 Source keys (ID-02)

- **Local sources:** `(volume_id, root_in_volume)`, with `root_in_volume` stored and compared **exactly** (UTF-16 code
  units, NAME-01), with no trailing separator except for the volume root `\`. In a case-sensitive parent, `\Media` and
  `\media` are two folders and so two sources (REV-M11). If a new root differs from an existing source's root only
  under the comparison key (NAME-03), the matching step **suggests** that source ("A saved source has this path apart
  from letter case: …") and the user decides (ID-04); nothing merges automatically.
- **Network sources:** `(network_root_key, root_in_volume)`. `network_root_key` is the comparison key of the canonical
  `\\server\share`: server and share names are case-insensitive in SMB, so `\\NAS\Media` and `\\nas\media` are one
  source. **Different server spellings are different sources** (`\\nas`, `\\nas.local`, `\\192.168.1.5`):
  StorageInventory does not resolve names (INV-05). The path inside the share is exact.

### 7.5 Matching algorithm (ID-03 to ID-06)

For every scan with "Save to History" on, at step P0 of §10.2 (read-only; no Library write):

1. Collect the evidence E0 (§7.2) through the path as entered. If collection fails, the confidence is `PathOnly`, with
   the reason recorded; if an item ID-13 requires is missing, Save to History is unavailable for this source (O24).
2. **Strong:** find volumes with the same filesystem type and 64-bit serial.
   - Exactly one → use it.
   - None → a new volume (created in T-IMPORT, not before).
   - **More than one** (clones that were both saved before) → ask (ID-04).
3. **Moderate:** find volumes with the same filesystem type and 32-bit serial. Label and capacity also match exactly
   one → use it; otherwise ask (ID-04).
4. **PathOnly:** network sources match on `(network_root_key, root_in_volume)`. Local PathOnly sources are always new
   unless the user picks an existing source (basis `UserAsserted`).
5. Find the source with the same `(volume, root_in_volume)`; if none, a new source (created in T-IMPORT). If a source
   exists whose root differs only under the comparison key, suggest it (ID-04).
6. The decision (existing IDs or "new") is held in memory and applied inside T-IMPORT, which re-reads the chosen rows
   under the write lock; a chosen row that no longer exists fails the import (`SourceChanged`).

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| ID-03 | A drive letter or mount point is never identity, and never a path identity on its own. Local PathOnly sources never match automatically |
| ID-04 | **The identity prompt.** When evidence is ambiguous (several candidate volumes), Moderate corroboration fails, a PathOnly local source could belong to an existing source, or a case-variant root suggests one, the user is asked **before the scan starts**: "Is this the drive last seen as *E: 'Backup'* on *date*?" with **Yes, the same** (basis `UserAsserted` if the evidence alone was not enough), **No, a different drive or folder** (a new source) and **Don't save this scan** (the scan runs with reports only; no attempt is recorded). The answer never changes a stored row until T-IMPORT |
| ID-05 | **Clones.** If two currently mounted volumes report the same Strong or Moderate identity, StorageInventory asks (ID-04) and never assigns silently. A clone that is **not** mounted together with its original cannot be distinguished and is attached to the same source: limitation L-ID1, stated in the identity help and in the Sources card's "How is this drive recognised?" text. The `c0c031f` heuristic "different root file ID and capacity" is removed for volume roots because it never fires on NTFS (§7.2) |
| ID-06 | **Never silent merges on weak evidence.** A snapshot is attached to an existing source only on Strong evidence, Moderate evidence with full corroboration, a network location, or an explicit user answer (recorded as `UserAsserted`). Low-confidence evidence never merges two sources silently. "Treat as a new source" is always available |
| ID-09 | **How the user is informed.** The preflight shows "Save to History: <source name> (recognised by <basis>, confidence <level>)". The Sources card shows the level. A comparison shows the lower level of its two snapshots; below Strong, or with basis `UserAsserted` or `Location`, a banner says "Compared on the basis of location only" or "Compared on the basis of your confirmation", and Missing reads "missing at this location". Network sources (basis `Location`) report no Missing or Added at all (L-DIFF3); their comparisons carry the L-DIFF3 banner as well (§14.4) |
| ID-10 | **End-of-capture re-verification (REV-H04, G0-M03).** The question it answers is: **did the scan finish observing the same source namespace it began observing, through the path it enumerated?**, not "what does this path point to now?". Three readings of the identity items of ID-13 are taken: **E1** at P2, through the handle opened on the enumerated path (§7.2), which is then held; **E2** at P4, through that same held handle: is the object the window began with still reachable and still on the same volume, at the same canonical position?; **E3** at P4, through a **fresh open of the enumerated path** (never of the canonical path, which a re-pointed letter does not change): does the path the scanner used still lead to that object? The capture is eligible only if E0 (preflight, §7.5), E1, E2 and E3 all provide every required item and are equal on each. An invalidated handle (for example after a surprise removal), a required call that fails, or any difference makes the capture `NotEligible (IdentityChangedDuringScan)`: the reports stay valid, nothing is published, an attempt is recorded. This catches: a volume swapped at the same letter (A→B, and A→B→A through the invalidated handle); a drive letter moved away from the volume; a SUBST or mapped letter re-pointed and still re-pointed at the end; the source folder renamed, moved or deleted during the scan. It cannot catch a letter re-pointed **away and back** between P2 and P4 while the original volume stays mounted: limitation **L-ID2**, stated in the identity help (TEST-U1) |
| ID-13 | **Minimum re-verification evidence per source type.** Identity items compared by ID-10: (1) the canonical path of the opened object, from `GetFinalPathNameByHandleW`, required for **every** source; (2) for local volumes, the filesystem name and 32-bit serial from `GetVolumeInformationByHandleW`, required; (3) when `FileIdInfo` succeeds at E0, the 64-bit volume serial and the 128-bit file ID of the opened root directory, then required at E1, E2 and E3; (4) for network sources, the filesystem name and serial when the provider returns them at E0, then required again. Label, capacity and free space are recorded but are not identity items (they change legitimately). If item (1), or item (2) for a local volume, cannot be obtained at preflight (E0), Windows cannot provide enough evidence to re-verify this source, and **Save to History is unavailable for it** before the scan starts, with the reason ("This location can't be re-identified at the end of a scan, so it can't be saved to History"); the scan still runs with reports only (O24). A capture never falls back to trusting the path. Q-02 records which source types provide which items |
| ID-11 | **Permitted comparisons.** Only two published snapshots of the same source (DIFF-01). Cross-source comparison is refused. Snapshots of a source always share its basis, because a later capture with weaker evidence never attaches to it silently (ID-12) |
| ID-12 | A source's confidence and basis are fixed when it is created. A later capture whose evidence is weaker than the source's is never attached automatically: it goes through ID-04 |

### 7.6 Edge cases

| Case | Outcome |
|---|---|
| Drive letter changes (`E:` → `F:`) | Same Strong or Moderate identity, so the **same source**. The observed location records the new letter |
| Offline removable drive | The source is shown **Offline** (when checked) or **Not checked**, with when and where it was last seen. History and browsing work from the Library (REQ-08) |
| Drive reformatted | A new serial means a **new volume and a new source**. The history is not merged; merging is deferred. If the label matches, the UI shows "Possibly the same drive (reformatted?)" as information only |
| Drive cloned; clone saved while the original is not mounted | Same source (L-ID1). The UI's identity help says clones cannot be told apart unless both are connected |
| Volume resized | Identity unchanged. The capacity difference is noted in the snapshot |
| Volume label changed | Identity unchanged; the display name is updated unless the user renamed it |
| Subfolder source deleted and recreated (NTFS) | Identity unchanged: the exact path within the volume identifies the source. The root folder's file ID differs, so the comparison says "The source folder was recreated since the older snapshot" (information, not a class change) |
| Subfolder source renamed only in letter case | A different exact root, so not attached automatically; ID-04 suggests the existing source |
| SUBST or mapped drive | The identity handle is opened on the path as entered and resolves to the target object; the canonical root and volume evidence come from that object (§7.2). Re-pointing the letter during a scan is caught by ID-10's E3 when the letter is still re-pointed at P4; away and back is L-ID2 |
| Mapped network drive re-pointed during the scan (`X:` → another server or share) | E3's canonical `\\server\share` differs from E1's → `NotEligible (IdentityChangedDuringScan)` |
| Removable medium replaced during the scan (same letter) | The held handle is invalidated by the removal, so E2 fails; E3 reads another serial → NotEligible (Q-19 confirms on media) |
| Drive letter reassigned during the scan (`E:` moved to another volume or removed) | E2 still reads the original volume; E3 fails or reads another volume → NotEligible. A letter change **between** scans is harmless (first row) |
| Source folder renamed, moved or deleted during the scan | E2's canonical path differs, or E3's open fails → NotEligible |
| Letter re-pointed away and back within one scan, original volume still mounted | Not detectable by start and end evidence: **L-ID2**, documented. The captured listings may mix two namespaces |
| A source type whose provider does not return the canonical path, or a local volume without filesystem name and serial | Save to History unavailable for it (ID-13); reports only |
| Folder-mounted volume (`C:\Mounts\X`) | **Not a supported source path.** `PathPolicy` blocks any source whose path passes through a reparse point (`SourceThroughLink`), and a mount folder is one. Scanning `C:\` records the mount folder as `ReparsePointSkipped`. The mounted volume can be scanned through a drive letter of its own (X-7) |
| Identity calls fail (for example on some network providers) | `PathOnly`, with the reason stored |
| Optical media (UDF) | `PathOnly`; discs are rarely compared, and serials are per disc |
| Volume swapped at the same letter during the scan | `NotEligible (IdentityChangedDuringScan)` (ID-10) |
| Network share now served by a different machine at the same name | Same source (location identity); comparisons carry the location banner (ID-09). DFS paths can change their canonical target; the canonical root recorded per snapshot shows it |

### 7.7 Online detection for the Sources view (ID-07, D-32)

- **When (1.1.0):** only **on demand**: when the user opens a source card, presses "Check drives", or starts a scan.
  Nothing is probed automatically at start-up (REV-M12). An automatic sweep is 1.1.x.
- **Which drives:** only mounted **local fixed and removable** drives (`DriveType.Fixed` or `Removable`, and
  `IsReady`), each with a 2 s timeout.
- **Never probed:** network and optical drives. Their sources show "Not checked" until the user scans them.
- **Test:** TEST-I7 drives the service with a fake drive list and asserts that network and optical drives are never
  probed and that nothing is probed without a user action.

### 7.8 New native API surface (ID-08, SEC-12a)

| Function | Access | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `kernel32!GetVolumeInformationByHandleW` | On a 0-access directory handle | Filesystem type, 32-bit serial, label, flags |
| `kernel32!GetFileInformationByHandleEx` (class `FileIdInfo` = 18 **only**) | On a 0-access directory handle | 64-bit volume serial; root directory file ID |
| `kernel32!GetVolumePathNameW` | No handle | Mount point |
| `kernel32!GetDiskFreeSpaceExW` | No handle | Capacity and free space |

All four are read-only queries. The 0-access requirement was verified on NTFS. FAT, FAT32, exFAT, UDF and SMB behaviour
is verified on real media in C3 (Q-02); holding the root handle for a whole scan is checked for side effects in C3
(Q-19).

---

## 8. File identity and name representation

### 8.1 Facts

| Topic | Fact | Source |
|---|---|---|
| NTFS | "A file keeps the same file ID until it is deleted." IDs survive renames and moves within the volume | Microsoft Learn, `BY_HANDLE_FILE_INFORMATION` remarks |
| Reuse | "File IDs are not guaranteed to be unique over time, because file systems are free to reuse them" | same |
| FAT | "The file ID is generated from the first cluster of the containing directory and the byte offset within the directory of the entry for the file." It can change after defragmentation or a rename to a longer name | same |
| ReFS | 128-bit identifiers; "the 64-bit identifier … is not guaranteed to be unique on ReFS" | same |
| Copies | A copy is a new file with a new ID. Restore-from-backup and sync tools usually create new files | (general filesystem behaviour) |
| Hard links | All links to a file share one ID. v1 counts each link as a file (documented limitation) | Microsoft Learn; `README.md` |
| Network (SMB) | IDs come from the server's filesystem, or are synthesised. There is no portable guarantee | *(not verified)* |
| Collection cost | `FileSystemEnumerable` (v1's enumerator) **does not return file IDs**. Collecting them needs a per-file open or a different enumeration core, which affects PowerShell parity | v1 code; Win32 documentation |
| Listing staleness | "In rare cases or on a heavily loaded system, file attribute information on NTFS file systems may not be current" when listed (`FindFirstFileW` remarks). v1's listing metadata can lag the file | review [DOC] (REV-O06) |
| Name equivalence | NTFS, ReFS, FAT, FAT32 and exFAT compare names case-insensitively by default (per code unit through an up-case table, with no Unicode normalisation); an NTFS directory can be marked case-sensitive; SMB servers define their own rules and some (for example macOS) also ignore normalisation differences. The published reproductions of the Windows NTFS up-case tables (XP to 8) and the exFAT default table map U+0345 to itself ([G0 R2]) | Win32 documentation of case sensitivity; [G0 R2]; *(the tables in use are verified by Q-14)* |

### 8.2 Decision (D-15, D-16)

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| FID-01 | **Move and rename detection: DEFERRED.** It cannot be proven portably (FAT and network IDs are unreliable, IDs are reused, copies get new IDs), and collecting IDs needs a reviewed enumeration design. Path-based comparison reports **Missing + Added** for a move. That is honest; a guessed "Moved" is not |
| FID-02 | **No per-file ID column in schema 1.** A later schema adds it through a migration. v1.1 records only the root folder's file ID per snapshot, as information (§7.2) |
| FID-03 | **Hard links (D-17):** each link path is its own observation, as in v1. A comparison classifies each path independently; sizes are per link and are not deduplicated |

### 8.3 Name representation and comparison key (D-30, D-38)

Five things are kept apart, and only the last one decides Missing or Added:

1. the **stored exact name** (NAME-01, NAME-02): what the listing returned, never normalised or folded;
2. the **filesystem's own name matching** (NAME-04): what the filesystem or server treats as one name. StorageInventory
   does not reproduce it and claims only what Q-14 verifies about it, per filesystem class (its *matching assurance*);
3. the **listing visibility** (DIFF-14): whether a successful listing shows every entry of the folder. Network servers
   can hide entries from an account while the listing succeeds (access-based enumeration, DFS and NAS policies), so
   visibility is claimed only for local filesystem classes that Q-23 verifies;
4. the **comparison candidate key** `K` (NAME-03, NAME-09): a deterministic, table-driven key used to find every entry
   that *could* occupy the same namespace position; equal keys never prove sameness, and unequal keys prove difference
   only where the assurance is `Verified`;
5. the **final proof rule** (DIFF-12): Missing or Added only when the absence is proven under the comparison's
   capability, which both snapshots determine (DIFF-14); whenever visibility or matching is uncertain, the result is
   Unknown.

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| NAME-01 | **Exact stored representation.** Every filesystem-derived name and path segment is stored as the exact UTF-16LE code units the listing returned, in a `BLOB` (`name.utf16`, `source.root_in_volume`, observed paths), including unpaired surrogates. Nothing is trimmed, normalised or case-folded. Full relative paths are rebuilt from the `folder_path` parent chain |
| NAME-02 | **Identity within a snapshot** is the exact name, compared by code units. Two siblings that differ only in case (possible in a case-sensitive directory) are two items; no two stored siblings can collide, because a source's names are `UNIQUE (source_id, utf16)` on exact bytes and `folder_path` / `file_obs` keys use the integer ID (§9.2, D-52) |
| NAME-03 | **Comparison candidate key `K`** (used only to find candidates for the same namespace position in the comparison, §12, and to *suggest* identity matches, §7.4; it never proves anything by itself). `K(n) = R(NFD(R(n)))` when `n` is well-formed UTF-16; otherwise (it contains an unpaired surrogate) `K(n) = R(n)`, with each unpaired surrogate mapped to itself. `R` maps each code point to the representative of its **case class**: the connected component of the graph that joins each code point to its simple uppercase, simple lowercase and simple titlecase mappings (`UnicodeData.txt`) and to its common and simple case foldings (`CaseFolding.txt`, statuses C and S); the representative is the class's smallest code point. `NFD` is canonical decomposition, Hangul included, followed by canonical ordering. **Fold, normalise, fold:** folding before normalising is what makes the per-unit argument of NAME-04 hold; normalising first reorders U+0345, the one case class member whose combining class differs from its partner's (U+0399), so `α U+0345 U+0301` and `α U+0399 U+0301` got different keys ([G0 R1], [E-9] 1 to 3). **Tables, not APIs:** `R`, the canonical decompositions and the combining classes are generated from the **Unicode 16.0.0** data files (SHA-256 in [E-9]) into a table checked into `StorageInventory.History`, regenerated only by a reviewed change. No operating-system or .NET casing or normalisation function (`ToUpperInvariant`, `Rune.ToUpperInvariant`, `string.Normalize` and the like) contributes to `K`, so `K` is identical on every Windows build and .NET servicing release (TEST-D6). **Not a canonical-equivalence key:** for 34 code points, the Greek iota letters with diacritics (U+038A, U+0390, U+03AA, U+03AF, U+03CA, U+1F30 to U+1F3F, U+1F76, U+1F77, U+1FD0 to U+1FD3, U+1FD6 to U+1FDB) and U+0130, `K(c) ≠ K(NFD(c))`, in Unicode 16.0.0 and on every plane ([G0F] §2, [E-9] addendum): the iota class's representative U+0345 is a combining mark that `NFD` reorders in one spelling and not the other, and `R` maps U+0130 to `I` before its dot is decomposed. So some canonically equivalent spellings get different keys. Soundness does not depend on it: `Verified` classes do not normalise (NAME-04), so on them those spellings are different names, and elsewhere unequal keys prove nothing; the cost is at most a missed spelling-only match. `K` is therefore never described as folding normalisation. **Versioned:** this is key version 1. `K` is never stored, with one exception: `source.network_root_key` (§7.4, §9.2); changing the tables is a schema change that recomputes those values in a migration (SCH-09) |
| NAME-04 | **The filesystem's name matching and its assurance.** The comparison needs one containment fact about a source's filesystem: every pair of names it treats as the same name has equal `K`. For a filesystem that matches names through a **per-code-unit fold** `U` (an up-case table, no normalisation), containment follows from the **per-unit condition**: for every code unit `u`, `U(u)` is a surrogate exactly when `u` is, `U(u) = u` for surrogates, and `R(u) = R(U(u))`; then fold-equal names are equal after `R`, code point by code point, and so have equal `K` ([E-9] 3: 0 failures in 100,000 random fold-equal pairs, against 147 for the `456b233` key). A filesystem class is *verified* only if it is on the release's checked-in *verified-class list*, which C7 fills from Q-14's evidence (the class matches names per code unit; the per-unit condition holds on every table Q-14 checks; the multi-code-point sequence census finds no collision outside `K`). A comparison's **matching assurance** is `Verified` only when both of its snapshots come from one verified class (below); otherwise it is **`Unverified`**, as for every network source (SMB servers define their own matching, and some ignore normalisation or apply full case folding), UDF, unknown local filesystems, and any class whose census has not passed. Before Q-14 passes for a class, that class is `Unverified` (the safe default). Assurance is a property of a **comparison**, not of a drive: it is derived at comparison time from **both** snapshots' recorded capture kind and filesystem name (`snapshot.capture_kind`, `snapshot.fs_name`, §9.2), never from the source row, the current drive or the newer snapshot alone, and is `Verified` only when both snapshots recorded the same class and that class is verified (DIFF-14). It is never stored, so a later release that verifies more classes applies to existing snapshots. The filesystem name is the one the driver reports (`GetVolumeInformationByHandleW`): a driver that reports a verified class's name while matching names differently is outside the model, as for any metadata a driver misreports. **Scope of `Verified`, stated exactly:** the tables Q-14 checks. A future up-case table that equates two code points assigned in Unicode 16.0.0 but in different case classes would break it; none has been seen, and Q-14 re-runs whenever the verified-class list changes. Characters assigned after 16.0.0 are handled by NAME-09. The proof rule that uses assurance is DIFF-12 |
| NAME-09 | **Verified domain.** A name is *in the verified domain* when every code point in it is assigned in Unicode 16.0.0 (general category other than `Cn`) or is an unpaired surrogate. A case-fold match (DIFF-11 step 2.2, on **every** comparison, so that alignment never depends on the assurance level) and a key-based proof of absence (DIFF-12, on a `Verified` comparison) are used only when the name concerned and **every same-type sibling** in the listing concerned are in the verified domain; otherwise the item is matched only exactly and is Missing or Added only through the name-independent proof, else Unknown (`NameMatchingUnverified`). Reason: a filesystem table newer than the pinned Unicode version can equate only characters `R` does not know, and such a character must occur in one of the two names for them to collide. Names outside the domain are rare (characters added to Unicode after 16.0.0), so the cost is a few Unknown items, never a false Missing |
| NAME-05 | **Display.** The exact spelling from the snapshot being shown, decoded to a .NET string (WPF renders an unpaired surrogate as a replacement glyph). A matched case-only rename shows both spellings. Display strings are never used as filesystem paths (SEC-15) |
| NAME-06 | **Search representation.** Name and path filters decode names in .NET and use `OrdinalIgnoreCase` substring matching (§13.5). Search is a finding aid, not a proof, so it does not use `K` |
| NAME-07 | **Sorting.** Display sorting happens in .NET with an ordinal comparer. SQL never orders by a name `BLOB` (little-endian bytes are not code-unit order) (A-05) |
| NAME-08 | **Why a BLOB** (D-30, reworded per REV-O10). Microsoft.Data.Sqlite binds .NET strings as UTF-8, which cannot represent an unpaired surrogate (it becomes U+FFFD), so two distinct NTFS names could collide on `UNIQUE` and fail a capture, or be merged. SQLite can store UTF-16 `TEXT`, but only through `sqlite3_bind_text16`, which the provider does not use; mixing `TEXT` and `BLOB` in one column breaks `STRICT`; WTF-8 gains nothing in .NET. Cost: joins use integer IDs, and decoding happens only for display and search (measured 0.56 s names-first search over 2M files, §15.1) |

**Capability table (NAME-04, DIFF-14).** A comparison takes the capability of the weaker of its two snapshots on each
axis, and `Verified` matching also needs both snapshots to have recorded the same class.

| Source capability | The filesystem's own name matching | Listing visibility | Matching assurance | Proof of absence (DIFF-12) | Why that is safe |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| NTFS, ReFS (local), default directories | Case-insensitive per code unit through the volume's up-case table; no normalisation | `Complete` once Q-23 passes for the class; `Unverified` until then | `Verified` once Q-14 passes for the class (NTFS in CI; ReFS on a Dev Drive, manually); `Unverified` until then | Name-independent, or key-based within the verified domain | Per-unit condition verified; `K` is at least as wide as the per-unit fold (it also joins wider case classes, which only widens it); a local listing of a readable folder shows every entry (Q-23) |
| NTFS directory marked case-sensitive | Exact | as its volume | as its volume | as its volume | Exact matching is contained in any per-unit fold; the rule never needs to know the flag, even when it changes between scans (T41) |
| FAT, FAT32, exFAT | Case-insensitive through an up-case table (exFAT: the volume's table; FAT: the system's); no normalisation | `Complete` once Q-23 passes (manual) | `Verified` once Q-14 passes (manual census); `Unverified` until then | as NTFS | as NTFS |
| UDF | Unknown | `Complete` once Q-23 passes (manual) | `Unverified` | Name-independent only: an exactly aligned, Listed folder holding nothing of that type | No assumption about the filesystem's matching is needed |
| Unknown local filesystems (any other reported name, for example third-party drivers that may be backed by a server) | Unknown | `Unverified` | `Unverified` | **None:** never Added or Missing (L-DIFF3) | Nothing about their listings is verified |
| SMB network shares (any server, any mapped letter) | Server-defined: case-sensitive or not; some ignore normalisation or use other folds | **`Unverified`**: access-based enumeration, DFS and NAS policies, and account or ACL changes between scans can hide entries while the listing succeeds (G0F-H01) | `Unverified` | **None:** never Added or Missing; only exact paths seen in both scans are compared (L-DIFF3) | No claim depends on what the server chose to show |
| Two snapshots with different recorded classes (for example an NTFS source and a later UDF capture attached by the user, ID-04) | Differs between the snapshots | the weaker of the two | `Unverified` (G0F-M04) | as the visibility allows: name-independent at most | Neither side's matching is used as authority for the other's namespace |

---

## 9. Persistent schema

### 9.1 Principles (SCH-01 to SCH-06, SCH-10)

- **SCH-01 Raw values only.**
  - Sizes are bytes.
  - Timestamps are **UTC .NET ticks** (100 ns since 0001-01-01; `NULL` means invalid or unrepresentable, as in v1
    records).
  - Attributes are the raw `FileAttributes` integer.
  - Status, type and error values are stable integer codes (§9.4).
  - **No CSV columns** (no KB/MB units, local times, percentages, categories or formula guard).
- **SCH-02 Identity never depends on a drive letter.** Paths are root-relative. The observed location is stored
  separately (§7.1).
- **SCH-03 Full snapshots.** Every published snapshot contains every file and folder observation. No deltas (D-07).
- **SCH-04 Shared dictionaries, per source (D-52).** Names (`name`) and folder paths (`folder_path`) are interned per
  source. Snapshots of a source share name and path IDs, so comparisons, which only ever pair snapshots of one source
  (DIFF-01), join on integers. A name seen by two sources is stored once for each: the cost is small (a source's
  distinct names, not its observations), and in exchange a new source's names occupy a key range that no existing page
  holds, so its import modifies no other source's dictionary pages and journals none of them (PERF-15 (a)). This is not
  delta storage: each snapshot still has one row per observed item.
- **SCH-05 Versioned from day one:** `PRAGMA user_version = 1` and `application_id`.
- **SCH-06 Invariants can be checked in the database** (§9.5), inside the import transaction and by tests.
- **SCH-10 Filesystem-derived strings are stored as exact UTF-16LE `BLOB`s** (NAME-01, NAME-08): names, paths, volume
  labels, and messages that can embed a path. **Exempt** (stored as `TEXT`, where lossy rendering of an unpaired
  surrogate is harmless because the value is never used for identity or matching): user-editable display names,
  attempt messages written by StorageInventory, version strings and the filesystem type name.
- **SCH-11 Capture order is the snapshot ID.** `snapshot_id` is assigned from `library_info.next_snapshot_id` inside
  T-IMPORT and is never reused, even after deletion. "Older" and "newer" mean smaller and larger `snapshot_id`
  (D-44).
- **SCH-12 Discovery ordinals.** `folder_obs.discovery_index` (v1's folder index) and `file_obs.seq` (v1's file
  emission sequence) are stored so that browsing reproduces v1's tie order exactly (D-48, REV-M09).

### 9.2 Tables (schema version 1)

```sql
PRAGMA application_id = 1397313110;          -- 0x53494E56 'SINV'
PRAGMA user_version  = 1;

CREATE TABLE library_info (
  singleton               INTEGER PRIMARY KEY CHECK (singleton = 1),
  library_id              TEXT    NOT NULL,   -- random GUID, fixed for the life of the Library
  created_utc             INTEGER NOT NULL,
  created_app_version     TEXT    NOT NULL,
  last_opened_app_version TEXT    NOT NULL,
  next_snapshot_id        INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (next_snapshot_id >= 1)   -- capture sequence (SCH-11)
) STRICT;

CREATE TABLE volume (
  volume_id            INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
  fs_type              TEXT    NOT NULL,      -- as reported: 'NTFS', 'ReFS', 'exFAT', 'FAT32', 'FAT', 'UDF', ...
  serial64             INTEGER,               -- FILE_ID_INFO.VolumeSerialNumber (bit pattern stored as signed 64-bit)
  serial32             INTEGER,               -- GetVolumeInformationByHandleW serial
  confidence           INTEGER NOT NULL,      -- IdentityConfidence (§9.4)
  display_name         TEXT    NOT NULL,      -- user-editable (SCH-10 exempt)
  last_label           BLOB,                  -- UTF-16 (SCH-10)
  last_capacity_bytes  INTEGER,
  first_seen_utc       INTEGER NOT NULL,
  last_seen_utc        INTEGER NOT NULL
) STRICT;
CREATE INDEX volume_by_serial ON volume (fs_type, serial64, serial32);   -- deliberately NOT unique (clones, ID-05)

CREATE TABLE source (
  source_id            INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
  kind                 INTEGER NOT NULL,      -- SourceKind: 1 LocalVolume, 2 Network
  volume_id            INTEGER REFERENCES volume (volume_id),
  network_root         BLOB,                  -- UTF-16 exact canonical '\\server\share' (display)
  network_root_key     BLOB,                  -- UTF-16 of K(network_root), key version 1 (§7.4, NAME-03)
  root_in_volume       BLOB    NOT NULL,      -- UTF-16, EXACT: '\' or '\Media\Music' (ID-02, NAME-01)
  identity_confidence  INTEGER NOT NULL,      -- fixed at creation (ID-12)
  identity_basis       INTEGER NOT NULL,      -- IdentityBasis (§9.4)
  display_name         TEXT    NOT NULL,
  created_utc          INTEGER NOT NULL,
  CHECK ((kind = 1 AND volume_id IS NOT NULL AND network_root_key IS NULL)
      OR (kind = 2 AND volume_id IS NULL     AND network_root_key IS NOT NULL))
) STRICT;
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX source_local_key   ON source (volume_id, root_in_volume)        WHERE kind = 1;
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX source_network_key ON source (network_root_key, root_in_volume) WHERE kind = 2;

CREATE TABLE scan_attempt (
  attempt_id           INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
  session_token        BLOB    NOT NULL,   -- 16 random bytes: the LibrarySession that inserted it (CONC-08)
  capture_token        BLOB    NOT NULL,   -- 16 random bytes: this capture; also written into the spool header
  source_id            INTEGER REFERENCES source (source_id),   -- NULL until the source exists (T-IMPORT)
  root_path_as_entered BLOB    NOT NULL,
  report_folder        BLOB,               -- display only (a stale-spool message); never used as a path (SEC-15)
  run_id               TEXT,
  started_utc          INTEGER NOT NULL,
  ended_utc            INTEGER,
  outcome              INTEGER NOT NULL,   -- AttemptOutcome (§9.4)
  failure_kind         INTEGER,            -- CaptureFailureKind (§9.4)
  message              TEXT                -- StorageInventory's own words (SCH-10 exempt)
) STRICT;
CREATE INDEX scan_attempt_by_source ON scan_attempt (source_id, attempt_id);

CREATE TABLE snapshot (
  snapshot_id                INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,   -- capture sequence; never reused (SCH-11)
  source_id                  INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES source (source_id),
  attempt_id                 INTEGER NOT NULL UNIQUE REFERENCES scan_attempt (attempt_id),
  state                      INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (state IN (1, 2, 3)),  -- SnapshotState; only 2 is ever committed
  completeness               INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (completeness IN (0, 1)),
  run_id                     TEXT    NOT NULL,   -- StorageScanResult.RunId: links to that scan's CSV reports
  started_utc                INTEGER NOT NULL,
  finished_utc               INTEGER NOT NULL,
  published_utc              INTEGER,            -- set by the last statement of T-IMPORT
  -- observed location: audit and display only, never identity
  root_path_as_entered       BLOB    NOT NULL,   -- UTF-16 (SCH-10)
  canonical_root             BLOB    NOT NULL,
  mount_point                BLOB,
  volume_label               BLOB,
  capacity_bytes             INTEGER,
  free_bytes                 INTEGER,
  root_dir_file_id           BLOB,               -- 16 bytes (FILE_ID_128) when available; subfolder sources only (§7.2)
  identity_confidence        INTEGER NOT NULL,
  identity_basis             INTEGER NOT NULL,
  identity_note              TEXT,               -- why confidence is reduced, when it is
  -- what this capture itself observed through (comparison capability, DIFF-14; never taken from the source row)
  capture_kind               INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (capture_kind IN (1, 2)),  -- SourceKind of the enumerated path at E0
  fs_name                    TEXT,               -- filesystem name reported at E0 (equal at E1 to E3, ID-10); NULL if none
  library_inside_source      INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (library_inside_source IN (0, 1, 2)),
  library_rel_path           BLOB,
  -- provenance
  app_version                TEXT    NOT NULL,
  scanner_contract           INTEGER NOT NULL,   -- 1 = v1.0 metadata semantics (§9.4)
  spool_format               INTEGER NOT NULL,   -- 1 (§11.7)
  schema_version             INTEGER NOT NULL,
  sqlite_version             TEXT    NOT NULL,
  -- sealed totals (from ScanTotals; checked against the data inside T-IMPORT and at every use, D-47)
  files INTEGER NOT NULL, folders INTEGER NOT NULL, bytes INTEGER NOT NULL, scan_errors INTEGER NOT NULL,
  reparse_points_skipped INTEGER NOT NULL, file_reparse_points INTEGER NOT NULL,
  locally_incomplete_folders INTEGER NOT NULL, affected_ancestor_folders INTEGER NOT NULL,
  CHECK (state <> 2 OR published_utc IS NOT NULL)
) STRICT;
CREATE INDEX snapshot_by_source ON snapshot (source_id, snapshot_id);

CREATE TABLE name (
  name_id   INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
  source_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES source (source_id),   -- names are interned per source (SCH-04, D-52)
  utf16     BLOB    NOT NULL,              -- the exact name as UTF-16LE code units (NAME-01); compared bytewise
  UNIQUE (source_id, utf16)
) STRICT;

CREATE TABLE folder_path (
  path_id        INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
  source_id      INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES source (source_id),
  parent_path_id INTEGER REFERENCES folder_path (path_id),   -- NULL only for the root
  name_id        INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES name (name_id),  -- the empty name for the root
  depth          INTEGER NOT NULL
) STRICT;
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX folder_path_child ON folder_path (source_id, parent_path_id, name_id);
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX folder_path_root  ON folder_path (source_id) WHERE parent_path_id IS NULL;

CREATE TABLE folder_obs (
  snapshot_id                  INTEGER NOT NULL,
  path_id                      INTEGER NOT NULL,
  discovery_index              INTEGER NOT NULL,   -- v1 folder index (SCH-12); 0 = root
  status                       INTEGER NOT NULL,   -- FolderScanStatus code
  status_reason                INTEGER,            -- ScanErrorType code
  subtree_complete             INTEGER NOT NULL,
  attributes                   INTEGER NOT NULL,
  created_utc                  INTEGER,
  modified_utc                 INTEGER,
  direct_bytes INTEGER NOT NULL, total_bytes INTEGER NOT NULL,
  direct_files INTEGER NOT NULL, total_files INTEGER NOT NULL,
  direct_subfolders INTEGER NOT NULL, total_subfolders INTEGER NOT NULL,
  largest_file_bytes           INTEGER,            -- size only: v1's browsing never shows the largest file's path
  PRIMARY KEY (snapshot_id, path_id)
) STRICT, WITHOUT ROWID;

CREATE TABLE file_obs (
  snapshot_id    INTEGER NOT NULL,
  folder_path_id INTEGER NOT NULL,
  name_id        INTEGER NOT NULL,
  seq            INTEGER NOT NULL,   -- v1 emission sequence (SCH-12)
  size_bytes     INTEGER NOT NULL,
  modified_utc   INTEGER,
  created_utc    INTEGER,
  accessed_utc   INTEGER,
  attributes     INTEGER NOT NULL,
  PRIMARY KEY (snapshot_id, folder_path_id, name_id)
) STRICT, WITHOUT ROWID;

CREATE TABLE scan_error (
  snapshot_id INTEGER NOT NULL,
  seq         INTEGER NOT NULL,       -- order of emission
  rel_path    BLOB    NOT NULL,       -- UTF-16, root-relative ('.' = root); computed from ScanErrorRecord.Path at import
  error_type  INTEGER NOT NULL,       -- ScanErrorType code (includes the informational reparse rows)
  message     BLOB    NOT NULL,       -- UTF-16; v1's default English text (it can embed a path)
  PRIMARY KEY (snapshot_id, seq)
) STRICT, WITHOUT ROWID;

CREATE TABLE snapshot_extension_total (
  snapshot_id   INTEGER NOT NULL,
  extension_key BLOB    NOT NULL,     -- UTF-16 of ScanEngine.ExtensionOf(name), lower-invariant; empty = none
  files         INTEGER NOT NULL,
  bytes         INTEGER NOT NULL,
  PRIMARY KEY (snapshot_id, extension_key)
) STRICT, WITHOUT ROWID;
```

**Foreign keys** are declared on the catalogue and dictionary tables. They are deliberately **not** declared on
`file_obs`, `folder_obs`, `scan_error` and `snapshot_extension_total`: the measured insert rates (§15) had none. Their
integrity does not depend on foreign keys: the §9.5 invariants, including name and parent closure, are verified inside
the import transaction before commit (IMP-05) and in tests. C4 measures the cost of declaring them (Q-07); if it is
under 10%, they are declared as well.

### 9.3 Indexes (conceptual justification)

| Index | Serves | Measured (§15.1) |
|---|---|---|
| `file_obs` PK `(snapshot_id, folder_path_id, name_id)` | Ordered comparison merge, per-folder browsing, key-ordered import (IMP-03) | 2M × 2 merge in 3.3 s (proxy engine) |
| `folder_obs` PK `(snapshot_id, path_id)` | Folder alignment, folder growth | — |
| `snapshot_by_source`, `scan_attempt_by_source` | History timeline | trivial |
| `name (source_id, utf16) UNIQUE` | Interning, per source (D-52): a new source's names form a new key range, appended; a later snapshot's new names land only in its own source's range. Names-first search scans the source's distinct names in .NET | 0.56 s substring search over 2M files (proxy, a Library-wide dictionary); per-source journal and throughput measured in the C4 design review |
| **Not added in 1.1:** `(snapshot_id, size_bytes)` or `(snapshot_id, name_id)` on `file_obs` | Largest-N uses a bounded top-N sort (`ORDER BY size_bytes DESC, seq LIMIT n`, A-24): 0.43 s measured | Revisit only if PERF budgets fail |

### 9.4 Stable codes (SCH-07)

Codes are **explicit integers mapped in code**, never enum ordinals. They are append-only. A test asserts the mapping
tables (TEST-S2), so reordering a C# enum can never change stored meaning.

| Type | Codes |
|---|---|
| `SnapshotState` | 1 Importing, 2 Published, 3 Deleting. Only 2 is ever committed: 1 and 3 exist only inside T-IMPORT and T-DELETE |
| `ScanCompletionState` (stored) | 0 Complete, 1 Incomplete. Cancelled and Failed are never stored on a snapshot |
| `FolderScanStatus` | 0 Ok, 1 Unreadable, 2 Partial, 3 ReparsePointSkipped |
| `ScanErrorType` | 1 AccessDenied, 2 NotFound, 3 PathTooLong, 4 IOError, 5 InvalidPath, 6 InvalidTimestamp, 7 UnexpectedError, 8 ReparsePointSkipped, 9 ReparsePointFile |
| `IdentityConfidence` | 1 Strong, 2 Moderate, 3 PathOnly |
| `IdentityBasis` | 1 Evidence, 2 UserAsserted, 3 Location |
| `SourceKind` | 1 LocalVolume, 2 Network (also `snapshot.capture_kind`) |
| `AttemptOutcome` | 1 InProgress, 2 Published, 3 Cancelled (the scan was cancelled), 4 ScanFailed, 5 NotEligible (the scan finished but the capture was not eligible), 6 SnapshotFailed (the import failed), 7 SaveCancelled (cancelled while saving), 8 Interrupted (found InProgress at a later open) |
| `CaptureFailureKind` | With ScanFailed: 1 InvalidPaths, 2 OutputError, 3 OutputInsideScannedTree, 4 InternalConsistency, 5 Unexpected. With NotEligible: 101 SpoolWriteFailed, 102 SpoolInvalid, 103 IdentityChangedDuringScan, 104 LibraryChangedDuringScan, 105 ReportVolumeNearlyFull, 106 ReportVolumeSpaceUnknown (SPOOL-24). With SnapshotFailed: 201 LibraryFull, 202 LibraryIoError, 203 LibraryBusy, 204 InvariantViolation, 205 SourceChanged, 206 LibraryUnavailable. **207 Catastrophic is reserved and never written by 1.1.0:** after a class C failure the process writes nothing more to the Library, and the attempt becomes Interrupted at the next open (OBS-13) |
| `LibraryInsideSource` | 0 No, 1 Yes, 2 Unknown |
| `scanner_contract` | 1 = v1.0 traversal and metadata semantics (NeverFollow, the v1 completeness rules, UTC ticks) |
| `spool_format` | 1 (§11.7) |

### 9.5 Database invariants for published snapshots (SCH-08)

For every published snapshot S of source X:

0. Every committed `snapshot` row has `state = 2` (checked at every open, LIB-08 step 7).
1. There is exactly one `folder_obs` row for the root `folder_path` of X, and its `discovery_index` is 0.
2. Every `folder_obs.path_id` and every `file_obs.folder_path_id` refers to a `folder_path` row of source X that has a
   `folder_obs` row in S.
3. **Parent closure:** every non-root `folder_obs` row has a `folder_obs` row in S for its parent path, the parent's
   status is Ok or Partial (a child is observed only if its parent was listed), and the parent's `discovery_index` is
   smaller.
4. **Name closure:** every `file_obs.name_id` and `folder_path.name_id` of S exists in `name` **with `source_id` = X**, the
   snapshot's own source (REV-M06, D-52).
5. For every folder: `direct_files` equals its `file_obs` count, `direct_bytes` their summed size, and
   `direct_subfolders` its child `folder_obs` count.
6. S's sealed totals match the data: `files` = `count(file_obs)`; `bytes` = `sum(size_bytes)`; `folders` =
   `count(folder_obs)`; the root's `total_*` values equal the snapshot totals.
7. Completeness matches the root: `completeness = Complete` ⇔ the root's `subtree_complete = 1`, mirroring
   `StorageScanResult.ForFinishedScan`.
8. No `folder_obs` with status `ReparsePointSkipped` or `Unreadable` has child folders or files in S.
9. A folder with status `Unreadable` or `Partial` has `subtree_complete = 0`, and so does every ancestor.
10. `scan_errors` equals the count of `scan_error` rows whose type is not informational.
11. `file_obs.seq` takes each value `0 … files−1` exactly once; `folder_obs.discovery_index` takes each value
    `0 … folders−1` exactly once.
12. `snapshot_extension_total` sums to `files` and `bytes`.
13. `snapshot.attempt_id` refers to an attempt with `outcome = Published` and the same `run_id`.

These are the Library-side mirror of `FolderAggregator.Verify`. T-IMPORT evaluates invariants 1 to 12 for S with
aggregate SQL **before** its final statements and rolls back on any failure (IMP-05, `InvariantViolation`); invariants 0
and 13 are established by those final statements (IMP-06) and re-checked by TEST-S3 and at every open (LIB-08).

### 9.6 Versioning and migration rules (SCH-09)

v1.1.0 ships **schema 1 only**, so no migration, backup or `VACUUM INTO` exists in 1.1.0. These rules bind the first
release that adds schema 2:

1. `user_version` is the schema version, and `snapshot.schema_version` records the version each snapshot was written
   under.
2. An app supports opening schema versions `1..N` (its own is N). Older Libraries are **migrated forward** only after
   an explicit prompt (declining leaves the Library read-only in that app), after a **pre-migration backup**, and in
   **one transaction** per version step with `synchronous=FULL`.
3. The backup target is created by `LibraryStore` with `FileMode.CreateNew` under a `.partial` name, filled with
   `VACUUM INTO` that existing empty file, verified, and renamed to its final name with `overwrite: false`. Its
   `-journal` joins the owned set. The temporary-storage and disk-space design for `VACUUM` is re-reviewed then
   (REV-M01, REV-M02).
4. A migration failure rolls back. The backup and the original remain, and the Library is reported "Incompatible
   (migration failed)" with the backup's path.
5. **A newer schema is never opened**, and there is never a downgrade (§6.8).
6. Migrations only **add** (tables, nullable columns, codes). A destructive change needs a new major version with an
   export path.
7. Each schema version has a frozen fingerprint test (LIB-08 step 6) and, from schema 2, a migration test from every
   earlier version's fixture database (TEST-M1).

---

## 10. Snapshot lifecycle

### 10.1 Phases and the validity point

```
 interlock (OBS-07)   capture step
 ──────────────────   ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 Mutating(Open)       App start: the interlock is CONSTRUCTED holding the start-up Open lease (OBS-14); LIB-08 runs
   │ clean end of the start-up open (OBS-13) → Idle: only now do Scan and the Library actions become available
 Idle                 P0 preflight ── read-only: path rules, Library state, identity evidence E0 and matching, ID-04
   │ TryBeginMutation(Prepare r): refused unless Idle (O28)
 Mutating(Prepare r)  P1 T-CREATE if needed, T0 ── Library writes BEFORE observation; writer closed at the end (OBS-12)
   │ atomic hand-off (OBS-10)
 Observing(r)         P2 window opens: epoch M1, commit generation D1; root handle opened on the enumerated path; E1
   │                     ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │                  P3 │ enumeration → aggregation → v1 reports; capture data → spool in REPORT folder;         │
   │                     │ spool folder section and trailer ("Scanning", then "Writing reports")                 │
   │                     │ Library: reads allowed; NO mutation of any kind can be granted (INV-17)               │
   │                     └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
   │                  P4 scan returned: M2, D2; E2 (held handle), E3 (fresh open of the enumerated path); handle closed
   │ atomic hand-off (OBS-10): capture → Mutating(Save r); Save off, or no attempt → Idle (clean end, OBS-13)
 Mutating(Save r)     P5 eligibility: Finished ∧ spool sealed and verified (V, SPOOL-23) ∧ M1 = M2 ∧ D1 = D2
   │                     ∧ E0 = E1 = E2 = E3 ?   ("Saving to History"; results already shown)
   │                     no ──▶ T-OUTCOME (Cancelled | ScanFailed | NotEligible); spool closed and deleted
   │                     yes
   │                  P6 T-IMPORT: one BEGIN IMMEDIATE transaction: rows + in-transaction verification + publish
   │                     failure or Stop saving ──▶ ROLLBACK ──▶ T-OUTCOME (SnapshotFailed | SaveCancelled); spool closed
   │                     COMMIT  ◀── the validity point (LIFE-01)
   │                  P7 writer closed; spool closed and deleted; "Saved to History"
   │ EndMutation (always, in finally): a clean end (OBS-13) → Idle
 Idle                 the next scan may start (UI-13)

 class C exception anywhere, a lease whose writer, spool or root handle cannot be released, or a non-current lease
 (OBS-15) ──▶ Faulted: TERMINAL. No lease of either kind again in this process; resources closed best effort; no
              further Library write; "Restart StorageInventory"; closing exits at once (OBS-13)

 crash anywhere ──▶ next open (Mutating(Open)): SQLite rolls back a hot journal and deletes it; T-RECOVER marks the
                    InProgress attempt Interrupted; the OS deleted the spool when the process died (it remains only
                    after power loss or an OS crash)
```

**LIFE-01 (validity point):** the `COMMIT` of T-IMPORT. **LIFE-02:** before it, nothing of the snapshot is visible to
any connection (SQLite isolation, CONC-04), and every service API accepts only `PublishedSnapshotId`, a type that only
the catalogue constructs from committed rows with `state = 2`.

### 10.2 Capture steps

| # | Step | Library access | Filesystem writes | Transaction |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| P0 | Preflight: v1 path rules plus LIB-05/LIB-06; Library state (§6.8); identity evidence E0 and matching (§7.5); the ID-04 prompt if needed; `InventoryScanner.PrepareCapture` validates the paths and names the run (its `RunId`) without creating anything (SINK-07) | read-only | none | none |
| P1 | Inside the capture's **Prepare** lease (OBS-08, OBS-10): creation (LIB-07) if the Library state is Not created (LIB-07 step 4 re-derives it under the lock and opens a Library that appeared meanwhile instead of creating), then **T0**: insert the `scan_attempt` row (`InProgress`, session and capture tokens, run ID, source if already known, root as entered, report folder for display); then close the writer (OBS-12). If creation or T0 fails, the scan may still run with reports only, and the UI says why nothing will be saved | write | Library only | T-CREATE (if needed), T0 (`FULL`) |
| P2 | Observation window opens (OBS-01): the interlock hands the Prepare lease over to *Observing(r)* atomically (OBS-10) and records the mutation epoch M1; the commit generation D1 is read (OBS-04); the root handle is opened on the enumerated path and held; evidence E1 read through it (ID-10). For a Save-off scan the interlock goes from Idle to *Observing(r)* directly | read-only | none | none |
| P3 | The v1 scan runs, with the CSV report sink (critical) and the spool writer (isolated) as observers: enumeration, aggregation, finalised folders to the spool, v1 reports, `StorageScanResult` | **reads only** (UI pages) | report folder only (reports and spool) | none |
| P4 | The scan has returned: epoch M2 and commit generation D2; evidence E2 through the held handle and E3 through a fresh open of the enumerated path; the handle is closed; the interlock hands *Observing(r)* over to the capture's **Save** lease atomically (OBS-10), or returns to Idle for a Save-off scan (on a clean end; otherwise Faulted, OBS-13) | read-only | none | none |
| P5 | Eligibility (inside the Save lease): `StorageScanResult.Finished` (LIFE-08); the spool is sealed, passes the verification pass V (SPOOL-23) and its trailer matches the result's totals (SPOOL-12); M1 = M2, the interlock stayed in *Observing(r)*, and D1 = D2 (INV-19, OBS-04); E0 = E1 = E2 = E3 (ID-10, ID-13) | none | report folder (spool read) | none |
| P6 | **T-IMPORT** (§11.8), after IMP-11's space preflight and under its guard: resolve or create the volume and source; insert the snapshot row (`state = 1`); stream the spool into dictionary rows (interned run by run in the source's dictionary, IMP-03) and observation rows in key order; verify §9.5 and the sealed totals (checking the save token as CAN-01d states); set `state = 2`, `published_utc`, the attempt's outcome `Published`, and advance `next_snapshot_id` | write | Library only | **T-IMPORT** (one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, `FULL`) |
| P7 | Close the writer (OBS-12) and the spool (it is deleted on close, SPOOL-14); end the Save lease (on a clean end the interlock returns to Idle; otherwise Faulted, OBS-13); show "Saved to History" | none | report folder (spool deletion) | none |

### 10.3 Transactions

| Name | When | Mutation lease (OBS-08) | What | Never |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T-CREATE | Library creation (LIB-07) | Prepare (first save) or Create | Schema, `application_id`, `user_version`, `library_info` | During an observation window; before the writer lock is held |
| T0 | P1 | Prepare | One `scan_attempt` insert | During an observation window |
| **T-IMPORT** | P6 | Save | The whole snapshot: dictionary rows, observation rows, snapshot row, attempt outcome, `next_snapshot_id`; with verification inside | Before the scan has returned; split into several commits (D-52 rejects a staged import) |
| T-OUTCOME | After any end other than publication | Save | `UPDATE scan_attempt … WHERE attempt_id = ? AND session_token = ? AND outcome = 1`. Best effort: if it fails, the attempt stays InProgress in this session (History shows "outcome not recorded yet", G0-O06) and the next open's T-RECOVER marks it Interrupted | During an observation window |
| T-DELETE | User deletes a snapshot (LIB-12) | Delete | One snapshot's rows | While any window is open or another mutation runs |
| T-RECOVER | Library open (LIB-08 step 8) | Open | `InProgress` attempts of other sessions → `Interrupted`; `library_info.last_opened_app_version` | Without the writer lock |

All are `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` on the one writer connection, with `synchronous = FULL` (CONC-03), and each runs only inside
the mutation lease named above. SQLite's hot-journal rollback at open runs inside the Open lease (LIB-08 step 5).

### 10.4 Outcome table (LIFE-03)

"Reports" is v1's own outcome, which the Save-to-History outcome never changes (LIFE-05).

| # | Situation | CSV reports (v1) | Snapshot | Attempt | User sees |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| O1 | Scan **Complete**, capture eligible, import commits | Valid | **Published** (`completeness = Complete`) | Published | "Saved to History" |
| O2 | Scan **Incomplete** (finished; some folders unreadable), eligible | Valid | **Published** (`Incomplete`), with the completeness evidence of LIFE-09 | Published | "Saved to History (incomplete: N folders could not be read)" |
| O3 | Cancelled during enumeration or during v1 report writing | Listed as incomplete (v1) | None (no import) | Cancelled | "Not saved: the scan was cancelled" |
| O4 | **Cancelled while saving** (P5 or P6) | **Valid** | Rolled back; none | SaveCancelled | "Reports saved. Saving to History was cancelled." |
| O5 | Failed: invalid paths | Nothing created (v1) | None; `PrepareCapture` failed before T0 (if the paths pass in P0 but fail when the scan re-validates them, the attempt records ScanFailed (InvalidPaths)) | none, or ScanFailed | v1 message |
| O6 | Failed: a report write during enumeration (critical observer) | Incomplete (v1) | None | ScanFailed (OutputError) | v1 message, plus "Not saved" |
| O7 | Failed: a report write after enumeration (Folders or sorted Files), **spool complete** | Incomplete (v1) | **None** (LIFE-08) | ScanFailed (OutputError) | v1 message, plus "Not saved" |
| O8 | Failed: tripwire (own file found inside the source) or internal consistency | Withheld (v1) | None | ScanFailed (OutputInsideScannedTree / InternalConsistency) | v1 message |
| O9 | **Spool write failure** (I/O error); or the spool gave up its space, or was never created, because the report folder's free space was below the reserve or could not be read (SPOOL-24); the scan still finishes | **Valid** (byte-identical to a Save-off run) | None | NotEligible (SpoolWriteFailed, ReportVolumeNearlyFull or ReportVolumeSpaceUnknown) | "Reports saved. Not saved to History: <reason>" ("the report folder's drive is nearly full; saving needed about N MB more" or "the free space of the report folder's drive could not be read") |
| O10 | Spool not sealed, or it fails the verification pass V (digest, structure, run index; SPOOL-23), or its trailer does not match the result | Valid | None | NotEligible (SpoolInvalid) | same |
| O11 | Identity evidence changed or became unavailable during the scan (ID-10: volume swapped, medium replaced, letter moved or re-pointed and still re-pointed, source folder renamed or deleted) | Valid | None | NotEligible (IdentityChangedDuringScan) | "Reports saved. Not saved to History: the drive or folder changed during the scan" |
| O12 | The mutation epoch or the commit generation changed during observation (INV-19, OBS-04) | Valid | None | NotEligible (LibraryChangedDuringScan) | "Reports saved. Not saved to History: the Library changed during the scan" |
| O13 | **Import fails or does not start for lack of space**: the disk holding the Library is full (`SQLITE_FULL`), or IMP-11's preflight at P5 finds too little free space (T-IMPORT does not start), or its guard rolls T-IMPORT back before the free space falls below the margin | **Valid** | Rolled back, or never begun; none | SnapshotFailed (LibraryFull) | "Reports saved. The scan could not be saved to History: the disk holding the Library is full" (or "…has N MB free; saving needs about M MB", or "…became nearly full while saving") |
| O14 | Import fails: I/O error, or the Library became unavailable | Valid | Rolled back | SnapshotFailed (LibraryIoError / LibraryUnavailable) | same pattern, with the reason |
| O15 | Import fails: an invariant or sealed total does not hold (IMP-05) | Valid | Rolled back | SnapshotFailed (InvariantViolation) | same, with "internal check failed" |
| O16 | Import fails: `SQLITE_BUSY` beyond the timeout | Valid | Rolled back | SnapshotFailed (LibraryBusy) | same |
| O17 | Import fails: the chosen source or volume row no longer exists | Valid | Rolled back | SnapshotFailed (SourceChanged) | same |
| O18 | Class C failure (CAT-03, for example out of memory) during the scan | v1: `Failed (Unexpected)`; files left as v1 leaves them | None | Not written; InProgress → Interrupted at the next open (Save on); none (Save off) | The interlock is **Faulted** (OBS-13): v1's message plus the restart instruction; nothing else is available |
| O19 | Class C failure during the saving phase (V, T-IMPORT) | Valid | Rolled back (by disposal, or by SQLite at the next open) | Not written; Interrupted at the next open | **Faulted**: "Reports saved at <folder>. StorageInventory ran out of resources while saving to History and must be restarted" |
| O20 | **App crash or process termination** during P1 to P5 | Whatever v1 produced (files left, as in v1) | None | InProgress → **Interrupted** at the next open | At the next start: "A scan of X on <date> was interrupted and not saved" |
| O21 | App crash or process termination **during the import** | Valid (written before P6) | Hot journal rolled back at the next open [E-4] | Interrupted | as O20 |
| O22 | Power loss or OS crash before the import commits | as O20/O21 | as O21 | Interrupted | as O20, plus "a temporary file History_<runId>.spool.tmp may remain in <report folder>; you can delete it" (SPOOL-17) |
| O23 | Power loss after the import commits | — | **Published and durable** (`TRUNCATE` + `FULL`, [E-5]) | Published | — |
| O24 | Library In use, Unavailable, Damaged, Incompatible, Not a Library, Leftover files, Missing or recovery pending at P0; or the source cannot provide the minimum re-verification evidence (ID-13); or the report folder's free space is already below the spool reserve, or cannot be read (SPOOL-24); or the Library's volume has less free space than IMP-11's P0 check requires, or it cannot be read | Unaffected | Not attempted | none | Preflight warning; "Save to History" disabled with its reason |
| O25 | "Don't save this scan" at the identity prompt | Unaffected | Not attempted | none | Reports only |
| O26 | App closed during P3 | v1 confirm-and-wait, then O3 | — | — | v1 flow |
| O27 | App closed during P5 to P7 | Valid | "Stop saving and close" → O4 then close; "Keep saving" waits | as chosen | UI-10 |
| O28 | **A scan (Start, New scan, or a scan started from another page) requested while a Library mutation runs** (saving, deleting, opening or recovering, creating, setting aside) | Not started: no report folder file, no attempt | — | none | The Scan command is unavailable with the running operation's name ("Saving to History…", "Deleting a snapshot…"); if a request races the state change, it is refused with the same words and nothing starts (OBS-11). The scan becomes available when the interlock returns to Idle (OBS-10) |
| O29 | **A Library mutation requested while a scan observes or another mutation runs** (Delete, Set aside, Create a new Library, Retry open) | Unaffected | Unaffected | Unaffected | The action is unavailable with its reason; a racing request is refused and changes nothing (OBS-11) |
| O30 | Class C failure (CAT-03) while any other lease is held (Open, including the start-up open; Create; Prepare; Delete; SetAside), or a lease that cannot release its writer, spool or root handle, or a non-current lease (OBS-15) | Valid if the scan had finished | Rolled back (by disposal, or by SQLite at the next open) | Not written; Interrupted at the next open | The interlock is **Faulted** (OBS-13): nothing that needs it is available; "Restart StorageInventory" (CAT-04) |

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| LIFE-04 | A capture never updates or deletes rows of any other snapshot. T-IMPORT inserts observation rows only for its own new `snapshot_id` and only inserts dictionary rows; T-DELETE touches one snapshot. **An older valid snapshot can never be lost because a newer attempt failed** (TEST-T3) |
| LIFE-05 | The Save-to-History outcome never changes `StorageScanResult`. The CSV reports keep v1's authority over their own outcome |
| LIFE-06 | A snapshot is published only if P5's eligibility holds **and** the §9.5 invariants and sealed totals hold inside T-IMPORT |
| LIFE-07 | No Library mutation overlaps any observation window, in either order (INV-17, OBS-07): no mutation lease is granted between P2 and P4, and no window opens while one is held. T0 happens before P2 and T-IMPORT after P4, joined to the window by atomic hand-offs (OBS-10) |
| LIFE-08 | **D-06, made explicit.** A scan whose result is not `Finished` never publishes, **even if its spool is complete** (O7). Reasons: v1's result is the authority over the run (LIFE-05); the failure kinds include the tripwire (our own output was found inside the source, so the observation may be polluted) and internal consistency (the folder records cannot be trusted), where discarding is essential; telling "benign" report failures apart would add a new decision surface. Reviewed and accepted for 1.1.0 (REV-O09); revisited with SREQ-04 in 1.2 |
| LIFE-09 | **Finished but incomplete scans are valid snapshots only with their completeness evidence:** every folder's status, status reason and `subtree_complete`; every child actually listed in a Partial folder; and the `scan_error` rows. That is all §12 needs to return Unknown instead of Missing (TEST-D2, TEST-D5) |
| LIFE-10 | Cancelled, failed and not-eligible captures leave **no** observation rows (the import never ran or rolled back). Their attempts are separate rows with no filesystem data |
| LIFE-11 | A crash during the import cannot make a partial snapshot queryable: SQLite rolls the hot journal back at the next open. If the journal was lost or separated, the committed-state check (LIB-08 step 7) and the sealed-totals check at use (D-47) turn the damage into an explicit error |

### 10.5 Attempts versus snapshots (D-12)

- **The two concepts are separate.** Every capture has exactly one `scan_attempt` row from T0 on. A published capture's
  attempt has outcome Published and is referenced by exactly one snapshot; every other outcome has no snapshot.
- **Attempts carry no filesystem data.** They reference no file or folder rows, so they cannot take part in comparisons.
- **The type system enforces this:** diff and history services take `PublishedSnapshotId`, and no path exists from an
  attempt to that type.
- **The History view shows attempts inline** in capture order, with a distinct style and wording per outcome
  ("Scan cancelled", "Not saved: the drive changed during the scan", "Interrupted"), so completeness history is honest.
- An attempt whose source was never created (a new source whose capture did not publish) is listed on Home under
  "Attempts needing attention" and never creates a source card.

---

## 11. Scanner, observers, spool and import

### 11.1 Goal and shape

**SINK-01:** one filesystem traversal feeds every consumer; there is no second scan for persistence. Nothing that runs
during the traversal can reach the Library.

```
            ┌──────────────── observation window: no Library write of any kind (INV-17) ────────────────┐
            │                                                                                            │
 InventoryScanner ─▶ ScanEngine ─▶ ObserverFanOut ─┬─▶ OutputGuard ─▶ CsvReportSink        (critical)    │
            │                                      └─▶ SpoolWriter ─▶ History_<runId>.spool.tmp          │
            │                                                          (isolated; in the REPORT folder)  │
            └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
 then, only after the scan has returned Finished and P5 holds (§10.2):

 InventoryRun ─▶ SpoolReader (same handle) ─▶ SnapshotImporter ─▶ LibraryDatabase: T-IMPORT, one transaction
```

The fan-out, the observers and the spool codec live in Core, which cannot reference the Library assembly (A-12). The
importer lives in `StorageInventory.Library` and runs only after the scan has returned.

### 11.2 Observer contract (SINK-02, internal)

```csharp
namespace StorageInventory.Core.Scanning;

internal interface IScanObserver
{
```
