```csharp
    void OnScanStarted(in ScanStartInfo start);            // once, first: run ID, canonical root, the run's own names
    void OnFile(in FileInventoryRecord file, int folderIndex);  // v1 record, unchanged; the n-th call has sequence n
    void OnError(ScanErrorRecord error);                   // v1 record, unchanged
    void OnFolderFinalised(int index, int parentIndex, FolderInventoryRecord folder);
                                                           // after aggregation and Verify: every folder once, ascending index
    void OnScanEnded(in ScanEndInfo end);                  // exactly once, last, on every path after OnScanStarted
}
```

- `IScanSink` (internal) is retired; its two methods are kept, unchanged, inside `IScanObserver`.
- **SINK-08 (no discovery callback; folder records are final).** v1.1 has no `OnFolderDiscovered`. During enumeration
  a folder's state lives only in `ScanEngine.Folders` and is **provisional**: its status can still become Partial,
  Unreadable (for example `NotFound` when it disappears before listing) or ReparsePointSkipped
  (`BecameReparsePoint`), and its attributes can change. The **first and only** emission of a folder is
  `OnFolderFinalised`, after `FolderAggregator.Aggregate` (including `Verify`) has computed its final totals,
  `Status`, `StatusReason` and `SubtreeComplete`. `OnFile` refers to a folder by index, a forward reference that the
  finalised records resolve. No observer can publish a folder before it is final, because none is told about it
  earlier. A future consumer that needs discovery-time events gets them in 1.2 with explicitly provisional semantics.
- `ScanEndInfo` carries the outcome (`Finished`, `Cancelled`, `Failed`) and, when Finished, the final `ScanTotals`.
- **Visibility (SINK-09, D-46).** `IScanObserver`, the fan-out, `ScanStartInfo`, `ScanEndInfo` and the spool types
  are `internal`. Core's `InternalsVisibleTo` list is exactly `StorageInventory.History`, `StorageInventory.Library`,
  `StorageInventory.Core.Tests`, `StorageInventory.History.Tests`, `StorageInventory.Library.Tests` and
  `StorageInventory.IntegrationTests` (A-13; v1's list is the first and last test assemblies). No
  public consumer API is frozen before the 1.2 integration design.

**Ordering guarantees (SINK-03).** Each is asserted by a recording observer on every fixture (TEST-O1):

1. If the scan fails before its run is named (invalid paths), no observer is called. Otherwise `OnScanStarted` comes
   first and `OnScanEnded` last, exactly once each.
2. `OnFile` and `OnError` happen only during enumeration. For each folder `i`, all `OnFile(…, i)` calls form **one
   contiguous run with respect to other folders' `OnFile` calls**; `OnError` calls may interleave with it. Runs
   appear in the order folders are listed (depth-first pop order), not in index order.
3. The `n`-th `OnFile` call (0-based) has v1 sequence `n`, the tie-breaker of the sorted Files report.
4. `OnFolderFinalised` is called exactly once per folder, in ascending index order, only if enumeration and
   aggregation (with `Verify`) completed, after the last `OnFile`/`OnError` and before the Folders report is written.
   `parentIndex < index` always holds, and the root has index 0 and parent −1.
5. The sequence and arguments of `OnFile` and `OnError` are identical to v1's `IScanSink` calls, so report bytes
   stay unchanged (INV-12, TEST-O2).

### 11.3 Fan-out and failure isolation (SINK-04, SINK-05, D-11)

| Observer class | Examples | If it throws a Class A exception (CAT-01) |
|---|---|---|
| **Critical** | `CsvReportSink` (v1 reports), wrapped by `OutputGuard` | The exception propagates and aborts the scan, exactly as v1 (`IOException`/`UnauthorizedAccessException` → `ReportWriteException` → `OutputError`) |
| **Isolated** | `SpoolWriter` | The fan-out catches it, marks the observer **faulted** (no further calls except `OnScanEnded`) and records it for the capture outcome (`SpoolWriteFailed`). The scan continues |

- **SINK-05:** `OutputGuard` wraps **only** the critical observer. An I/O error in an isolated observer can never be
  reported as the scan's `OutputError` (REV-M03, review §14A).
- **No stalls by construction.** The spool writer performs synchronous, buffered appends on the scanning thread, as
  v1's CSV writer does. There is no channel, no writer thread and no database I/O during traversal, so an isolated
  observer cannot hold the critical path hostage beyond one buffered write, and cancellation latency is v1's
  (REV-M03).

### 11.4 Exception classes (CAT-01 to CAT-05, D-45)

| ID | Class | Members | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| CAT-01 | **A: expected failures** | `IOException`, `UnauthorizedAccessException`, spool format errors, `SqliteException`, invariant failures, and any other exception that is not class B or C | Converted to outcomes at defined points only: a critical observer → `OutputError` (v1); an isolated observer → faulted; the import → rollback and `SnapshotFailed` |
| CAT-02 | **B: cancellation** | `OperationCanceledException` while the run's token is cancelled | Never isolated: it propagates from any observer, and the scan returns `Cancelled` (v1). An `OperationCanceledException` from an isolated observer while the token is **not** cancelled is class A for that observer |
| CAT-03 | **C: catastrophic but catchable** | `OutOfMemoryException`, `InsufficientExecutionStackException` | **Never isolated, never retried, never turned into a normal outcome by new code.** The fan-out rethrows it. v1's existing top-level handler in `InventoryScanner.Scan` is kept unchanged (behaviour preservation, X-6): it returns `Failed (Unexpected)`, which is never `Finished`, so no snapshot can follow; for `RunCapture` (internal) it also sets `CaptureScanOutcome.Catastrophic`, so the App learns that the failure was class C while the public `StorageScanResult` stays exactly v1's. The importer does not catch it: its `using` scopes dispose the transaction (a rollback) and the spool handle (a deletion), and the exception propagates to the App |
| CAT-04 | **App boundary for class C** | — | **One authoritative outcome: the interlock becomes Faulted (OBS-13), wherever the class C exception surfaced**: at `ScanSession`'s task boundary (a scan, a capture or its saving, including `CaptureScanOutcome.Catastrophic`), inside any lease holder (every mutation kind and every observation), or at the task boundary of an App service that runs work of its own (`ICompareService`). It does not retry and attempts no recovery: no further Library write (so no T-OUTCOME; the attempt becomes Interrupted at the next open), and no new scan. The message is the OBS-13 restart instruction, naming the report folder as plain text when the reports are valid |
| CAT-05 | **D: not catchable** | `StackOverflowException`, access violations (not delivered to managed handlers by default), `Environment.FailFast`, process termination, power loss | No handler. Correctness rests on durable state only: the spool is never importable except by the live process (SPOOL-06), T-IMPORT is atomic (LIFE-11), and the attempt becomes Interrupted (T-RECOVER) |

The rule behind CAT-03: catching is allowed only where every per-run resource has already been released and nothing of
the run can become valid; v1's scanner boundary satisfies this, and its documented "never faults for scan, output or
cancellation problems" contract is kept, not widened.

### 11.5 Cancellation contract by stage (CAN-01 to CAN-03)

| ID | Stage | How cancellation is observed | What remains valid | Snapshot | Attempt |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CAN-01a | Enumeration | v1: the token is checked between folders and every 256 entries | Nothing new: report files listed as incomplete (v1); the spool is closed and so deleted | None | Cancelled |
| CAN-01b | Spool writing | The spool writes inside observer callbacks on the scanning thread; the next token check stops the scan. A blocked write returns when the OS completes it, as v1's CSV writes do | as CAN-01a | None | Cancelled |
| CAN-01c | v1 report writing (Folders report, sorted Files report) | v1's token checks in both loops | as CAN-01a | None | Cancelled |
| CAN-01d | Post-scan saving (P5, P6) | The **save token** is checked every 64 MiB of the verification pass V; before T-IMPORT begins; at every IMP-11 check of T-IMPORT (`BEGIN`, every 4,096 inserted rows of any table, and the final check immediately before `COMMIT`); inside the in-transaction verification after at most 65,536 rows examined by any verification query (each query runs over bounded key ranges, or under the engine's progress callback, so that no stretch of verification goes longer without a check); and after the verification. Stretches that insert few rows have a read-based cadence too, as V has (C4DRR-O04): the error pass of IMP-03 (5) checks the token every 64 MiB of spool it reads, and the dictionary-footprint query before T-IMPORT runs over bounded key ranges or under the progress callback, like the verification. **No two consecutive checks are more than 0.5 s apart** in any TEST-P1 cell, and in C5's spool-reading parts in any TEST-P2 cell: the detection budget of CAN-03. The last check before `COMMIT` is IMP-11's final check; a cancellation after it is not observed (CAN-01e) | **The reports** (the scan is already Finished) | Rolled back; none | SaveCancelled |
| CAN-01e | After `COMMIT` | Ignored | Reports and the snapshot | Published | Published |

- **CAN-02:** the scan's token and the save token are separate. "Cancel" during P3 cancels the scan; during P5 and P6
  it cancels only the saving; the scan result is final by then. Closing the window while saving (P5 to P7) asks first
  (UI-10).
- **CAN-03 (Stop Saving: phases and budgets; D-53).** The user-visible time starts when the save token is cancelled
  ("Stop saving", or "Stop saving and close") and ends when the capture's Save lease has ended cleanly: the transaction
  is rolled back, T-OUTCOME has recorded SaveCancelled (best effort), the writer, the gate and the spool are released and
  the interlock is Idle, so that the next allowed transition (a scan, a deletion, closing) can start. Three phases:
  (1) **detection**, from the cancellation to the next token check (CAN-01d), at most **0.5 s**, in verification as
  everywhere else; (2) **rollback**, SQLite restoring the journalled pages and truncating (13 to 34 ms per MiB of journal
  measured, C4 design review and repair; a new source's journal is a few pages, a representative re-scan's about 30 MiB
  at 2M files, D-52); (3) **release**, T-OUTCOME, the writer and spool closes and the lease's end. **Budgets:** in the
  **representative** workload classes of §15.4 the whole stop takes at most **2 s** (PERF-16) and phases 1 and 2 at
  most **1.75 s** (PERF-15 (c)); in the **stress and worst-case** classes at most **5 s** and **4.75 s**. The 2 s promise
  is made for representative saves only; a save of high-novelty or adversarial names may take up to 5 s to stop, and
  UI-10 shows it. Appended pages are not journalled ([E-3], [E-5]), so the rollback cost is the journalled dictionary
  pages (G0-O03), which per-source dictionaries bound by the source (D-52).

### 11.6 The observation window and the Library activity interlock (OBS-01 to OBS-13, D-51)

The re-review found that the repaired design stopped writes from starting during a window but did not stop a window
from opening while a write was already running: a New scan of `C:\` could start while the previous capture was still
importing into `%LOCALAPPDATA%` (G0-B01). The rule is now a **lifecycle interlock**, not a disabled button: every
Library mutation and every observation window is a lease on one state machine, and the two kinds of lease exclude each
other in both directions.

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| OBS-01 | The window opens at P2, before `ScanEngine` starts enumerating, and closes at P4, after the scan has returned (enumeration, aggregation, the spool's folder section, v1's reports, and the spool's run index and trailer). It applies to **every** scan the process runs, Save on or off, whatever the source (scope: INV-17; another process: L-OBS1) |
| OBS-02 | While it is open, the interlock is *Observing(r)*: no mutation lease can be granted, so no T-CREATE, T0, T-OUTCOME, T-IMPORT, T-DELETE, T-RECOVER, Library open, creation or set-aside can start (OBS-07, OBS-11), and the corresponding actions are unavailable with their reason (UI-13) |
| OBS-03 | Reads are permitted: `Mode=ReadOnly` connections may open, read and close. In `TRUNCATE` mode a read transaction writes no file ([E-2], [E-8] 1), and a read-only connection that meets a hot journal fails with `SQLITE_READONLY_ROLLBACK` without writing ([E-8] 4). A read cannot commit, so a read that did write would not show in the epoch or the commit generation: the Windows confirmation that reads change no file's contents and create or delete no file is TEST-W1 and Q-21, a C4 stop condition. OBS-03 does not claim more than that |
| OBS-04 | **Library-change detection (INV-19).** (a) **Authoritative, for StorageInventory's own mutations:** the interlock's *mutation epoch*, a 64-bit counter incremented inside the interlock's monitor whenever a mutation lease is granted. M1 is recorded in the same monitor section as the hand-off into *Observing(r)*, M2 in the same section as the hand-off out of it, and the interlock records whether any other transition happened in between. Every mutation needs a lease (OBS-08, A-25), so M1 = M2 with no other transition proves that StorageInventory mutated nothing during the window, with no filesystem metadata involved. (b) **Secondary, for commits by any connection or process:** the *commit generation* D = (file change counter, page count) at header offsets 24 and 28 of `library.sqlite3`, read at P2 and at P4 from the file's contents through `LibraryStore.ReadHeader` (`FileMode.Open`, `FileAccess.Read`, `FileShare.ReadWrite \| FileShare.Delete`, 100 bytes; "absent" when there is no main file). Reads never change it and every committed write advances it ([E-8] 1). It cannot reveal a write transaction rolled back within the window ([E-8] 2); such a write can only come from outside StorageInventory and belongs to the tamper model (CONC-10, D-47). (c) **Not used:** directory-listing metadata (entry names, sizes, times), which NTFS may not keep current for files held open (§8.1, G0-M04), and last-access times anywhere (G0-O08) |
| OBS-05 | "Open Library folder" is disabled while a window is open (LIB-03) |
| OBS-06 | Core, which runs the traversal, has no reference to the Library assembly (A-12), and a type-graph rule proves the spool writer and fan-out cannot reach `LibraryDatabase` (A-16). The interlock (OBS-07) covers the App |
| OBS-07 | **The interlock (D-51).** `LibrarySession` owns one *Library activity interlock* whose state is exactly one of **Idle**, **Mutating(kind, owner)**, **Observing(run)** or **Faulted**. It starts in Mutating(Open) (the start-up open, OBS-14), never in Idle, and Faulted is terminal (OBS-13). **Invariant:** at every instant the process is observing, or mutating, or neither; never both, and never two mutations at once. A window opens only from Idle (a Save-off scan) or by the hand-off of the same capture's Prepare lease; a mutation lease is granted only from Idle or by the hand-off of the same capture's window to its Save lease. Every transition is atomic |
| OBS-08 | **What a Library mutation is (exhaustive).** Any operation that can create, write, truncate, rename or delete a file in the Library directory, or that opens a read-write connection (which can roll a hot journal back and delete it): **Open** (LIB-08, with the lock acquisition, hot-journal rollback and T-RECOVER); **Create** (LIB-07 and T-CREATE: directory, lock file, main file); **Prepare(r)** (P1: creation if needed, or opening a Library that LIB-07 step 4 finds, then T0); **Save(r)** (P5 to P7: V, T-IMPORT, T-OUTCOME, closing the spool); **Delete** (T-DELETE); **SetAside** (LIB-13). A later operation that mutates the Library (migration and its backup, SCH-09; backup, SREQ-05; compaction, SREQ-09; deleting a source, SREQ-06; labels, SREQ-07; any repair) is a new lease kind, added here by the change that introduces it. It cannot be written any other way: every mutating API of `LibraryStore` and `LibraryDatabase`, and the writer connection itself, is reachable only through a `MutationLease` argument (A-25), and the lease must still be current when the work runs (OBS-15) |
| OBS-09 | **Primitive and ownership.** The interlock is an app-lifetime object inside `LibrarySession` (§14.2), created before the first page and present whether or not a Library exists or this process owns it; it is constructed already holding the start-up Open lease (OBS-14). Its state is one field guarded by a private monitor; every operation is a non-blocking *try* that holds the monitor only to test and change the state, never across I/O or an `await`. A grant returns a lease token (`MutationLease`, `ObservationLease`) that only the interlock can construct, carrying a lease id that is never reused (OBS-15); disposing it, always in `finally`, ends the lease: back to Idle only on a clean end, otherwise into Faulted (OBS-13), in the same monitor section. Hand-offs are methods on the lease that change the state in one monitor section. The state, with a reason naming the operation in progress, is published as an observable value that the UI binds to (UI-13). There is no queue and no waiting for a lease. Lock order: interlock (state change only), then the in-process gate (CONC-06), then SQLite |
| OBS-10 | **Capture hand-offs and the next scan.** A capture goes Prepare(r) → Observing(r) at P2 (after the writer has been closed) → Save(r) at P4 → Idle at the end of P7 (on a clean end; otherwise Faulted, OBS-13). Save(r) ends only after T-IMPORT has committed or rolled back, T-OUTCOME has been written or has failed (best effort, bounded by `busy_timeout`), the writer has been closed and the spool handle has been closed. **The next scan becomes eligible to start exactly when the interlock is Idle again.** So the next capture's T0 can never precede the previous capture's recorded outcome, and no scan can start while a capture is saving (G0-B01 failure modes 1 and 2). A Save-off scan goes Idle → Observing(r) → Idle. A capture whose creation or T0 failed, or whose creation found an existing Library in a state other than Available (LIB-07 step 4), continues as a Save-off scan: its Prepare lease hands over to Observing(r), and at P4 the interlock returns to Idle |
| OBS-11 | **Refusal, cancellation and failure.** A refused request is an outcome, not an error: a refused scan starts nothing (no report-folder file, no attempt, O28); a refused mutation changes nothing (O29); each carries the reason of the operation that holds the interlock. Cancellation never skips a transition: **Stop scan** cancels the scan token (the scan returns Cancelled; the P4 hand-off follows; Save(r) records the outcome). **Stop saving** cancels the save token: V or T-IMPORT sees it within CAN-01d's detection budget and rolls back, T-OUTCOME records SaveCancelled and the lease ends normally, all within PERF-16 for the save's workload class (CAN-03). Cancelling a deletion rolls T-DELETE back. An Open or a SetAside cannot be cancelled once started (SQLite's hot-journal rollback; at most four renames), so closing the window waits for it. **If the import fails,** the transaction rolls back, T-OUTCOME records SnapshotFailed (best effort), the spool is closed and the lease ends; the next scan is then eligible, and Save to History is unavailable only if the Library's new state requires it (O24). "Stop saving and scan" is not offered as one action: the user stops saving, then starts the scan once the interlock is Idle |
| OBS-12 | **The writer exists only inside a mutation lease.** The `Mode=ReadWrite` connection is opened after a mutation lease is granted and closed before it ends (CONC-03). So during an observation window no StorageInventory handle with write access is open on any Library member; `library.lock` stays held (it is never written) and reader connections may be open. Rollback mode has no close-time side effect ([E-2]); reopening costs one open, the §5.8 pragmas and the engine assertion |
| OBS-13 | **Faulted (restart required), the one terminal state (G0F-M02).** *Clean end:* ending a lease returns the interlock to Idle only when (a) its operation completed or ended with a class A or B outcome (CAT-01, CAT-02), and (b) every resource the lease opened is released: the writer connection closed (every mutation kind), the spool handle closed (Save), the held root handle closed (an observation). *Any other end enters Faulted*, in the same monitor section, for every lease kind (Open, the start-up open included; Create; Prepare; Save; Delete; SetAside; an observation): a class C exception (CAT-03: `OutOfMemoryException`, `InsufficientExecutionStackException`) thrown inside the lease or reported by `RunCapture` (`CaptureScanOutcome.Catastrophic`); a release step that throws; a non-current lease presented to an operation (OBS-15). A class C exception that an App service observes outside any lease (CAT-04) enters Faulted from whatever state the interlock is in; a lease held at that moment can then no longer end at Idle, and its operation's token is cancelled. **In Faulted:** no lease of either kind is ever granted again in this process, and Faulted never returns to Idle (it has no exit transition; only process exit ends it); ending a lease while Faulted changes nothing. **Best-effort release**, each step in its own guard, never retried, stopping at a second class C exception: cancel the scan and save tokens; dispose any open transaction and the writer connection (an open transaction rolls back, or SQLite rolls it back at the next open, O21); close the spool handle (delete-on-close deletes it; if it cannot be closed, the OS deletes it when the process exits, SPOOL-15); close the held root handle; dispose reader connections through the gate. `library.lock` stays held until the process exits, so no other instance opens the Library while this process may still hold a handle on it. **Nothing is attempted after the fault:** no T-OUTCOME (the attempt becomes Interrupted at the next open, T-RECOVER), no import, no scan, no Library read. **Artefacts:** report files stay exactly as v1 left them (valid if the scan had finished, otherwise listed as incomplete and never deleted, INV-10); the spool does not survive the process except after power loss (SPOOL-17). **User-visible:** the Library state is Unavailable (restart required), every page shows "StorageInventory ran out of resources and must be restarted. Nothing more will be saved to History in this session. <Your reports are complete in <folder> / The reports of the last scan were not completed.> Close StorageInventory and start it again." (UI-08); every command is unavailable except closing the window (UI-13). **Closing** asks nothing and exits at once; a write transaction still open is left to SQLite's hot-journal rollback at the next start, exactly as after a crash (O21) |
| OBS-14 | **Start-up ownership (G0F-M01).** The interlock is constructed in *Mutating(Open)* holding the **start-up Open lease**, before any page exists and before any command's availability is evaluated, so there is no moment at which the App looks Idle before the Library has been examined. Under that lease the App runs LIB-08 once: path validation; if the Library directory exists, the writer lock is acquired before any member is inspected (CONC-01); then the member checks, recovery and state derivation. The lease ends as any lease does (OBS-13): on a clean end the interlock reaches Idle for the first time and **only then** do Start scan, New scan and the Library actions become available (UI-13), whatever state the open derived. While it runs, a scan request is refused with "Opening the Library…" and starts nothing (O28); there is no queue, and the user starts the scan again once the phase ends. If the directory did not exist, the open ends at once with Not created and no lock; a Library that appears later is found under the lock by the next creation attempt (LIB-07 step 4) or by "Retry". A start-up open that fails with a class A error ends with the state it reached (Unavailable, Damaged and so on), and scans then run without saving |
| OBS-15 | **Lease validity at run time (G0F-M03).** A lease authorises work only while it is *current*: it was granted by **this** `LibrarySession`'s interlock (reference identity, so a lease of another `LibrarySession` instance is refused); the interlock's current lease id equals the lease's id (ids are never reused, so a lease from an earlier grant is refused); it has not been disposed; and it has not been handed off (a Prepare lease stops being current at its hand-off to Observing, an observation lease at its hand-off to Save). Each operation also names the kinds that permit it: T-CREATE: Create or Prepare; T0: Prepare; LIB-08 steps 3 to 9: Open, or Prepare or Create through LIB-07 step 4; V, T-IMPORT, T-OUTCOME and the spool's closing: Save; T-DELETE: Delete; renames: SetAside; enumeration: an observation lease. The check runs inside the interlock's monitor, never across I/O, at every point where work could start or commit: when the writer connection is opened; at `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`; every 16,384 written rows; immediately before `COMMIT`; before every `LibraryStore` create, rename or lock operation; before every hand-off; and in `RunCapture` before enumeration starts and again at P4. Core cannot reference the Library assembly (A-12), so `RunCapture` takes Core's internal `IObservationPermit`, whose only implementation is the interlock's observation lease (A-25). A failed check refuses the operation before any I/O and enters Faulted ("internal check failed", OBS-13): a non-current lease can only come from a lifecycle defect, so the process stops trusting its own state. Leases stay internal: no public type takes or returns one (A-13, A-25) |

**Machine-checkable form.** The interlock is a small state machine that performs no I/O itself, so TEST-W2 checks
every (state, request) pair against the table above, including the constructed start-up state and entry into the
terminal Faulted state from every lease kind; runs randomised concurrent request sequences from several threads and
asserts the invariant after every step; and uses test hooks that pause inside every mutation kind to show that an
observation request is refused while the mutation runs and granted only after it ends. TEST-W6 checks that a lease
authorises work only while it is current (OBS-15). A-25 checks statically that every Library mutation and every
observation entry point in every first-party assembly requires a lease; OBS-15 checks at run time that the lease is
still current when the work runs. Neither alone is a proof that no mutation can bypass the interlock; together with
TEST-W2, TEST-W5 and TEST-W6 they are the enforcement.

### 11.7 The observation spool (SPOOL-01 to SPOOL-24, D-35, D-49, D-50)

| ID | Aspect | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| SPOOL-01 | **Location** | Directly inside the validated **report folder** of the run, never elsewhere. This was evaluated, not assumed: (a) v1 validates the report folder to be outside the source literally and by canonical location, with the own-name tripwire as backstop; (b) the user chose it and it already receives the same class of private data (the Files report lists every path); (c) create-new, own-name and three-proof rules already govern it; (d) reports are mandatory in 1.1.0 (D-43), so it always exists; (e) every other candidate is either possibly inside the source (`%TEMP%` lies inside `C:\`) or is the Library, which must not be written. Costs: transient space in the report folder; spool traffic when the report folder is on a network share; possible upload when it is inside OneDrive (both already warned about by v1, and the warnings now name the spool). It is the 1.1 design, not a permanent one: a reviewed change may replace it (for example a user-chosen working folder with SREQ-04 in 1.2). **Inherited residual (G0-O10):** when v1's canonical check is unavailable, v1 only warns (`PathPolicy.cs:189-195`) and its tripwire fires only after a report, or now the spool, already exists inside the source. The spool adds no new class of exposure; TEST-SP2 keeps the alias case |
| SPOOL-02 | **Owner** | The scan's `ReportRun` (Core) creates, holds and deletes it. The capture receives the live in-memory `ObservationSpool` handle, never a path |
| SPOOL-03 | **Name** | `History_<runId>.spool.tmp`. It is added to the run's own names **only when a capture is requested**, so `ReportRun.Prepare`'s existence check and the scan's tripwire cover it; runs without a capture keep v1's six names exactly |
| SPOOL-04 | **Create-new** | `FileMode.CreateNew`, `FileAccess.ReadWrite`, `FileShare.None`, `FileOptions.DeleteOnClose`, by `ReportRun` only, after the report-folder reparse-point re-check, when the run creates its first report file. One file per capture |
| SPOOL-05 | **Privacy** | It holds names, sizes, timestamps and attributes in a machine-readable private binary format, the same class of data as the Files report. It is held exclusively (no other process can open it), deleted when closed, and never uploaded by StorageInventory |
| SPOOL-06 | **Never a snapshot by itself** | (1) No API accepts a spool path; the importer takes only the live handle of the current capture. (2) Its header carries the capture token, which must equal the in-memory token and the attempt's `capture_token`. (3) Its trailer must be present and match the scan result's totals. (4) The SHA-256 in the trailer must match the file's physical byte sequence, verified by the pass V (SPOOL-23) before any record is used. (5) Import happens only after a `Finished` result and P5. (6) A spool left by another process is never opened, because no code finds spools by name. (7) The format is private and versioned, never an export (the INV-11 analogue) |
| SPOOL-07 | **Lifecycle** | Created at scan start; file and error records appended during enumeration; finalised folder records written after aggregation, before v1's Folders report (SINK-03 #4); the run index, trailer and digest written at the seal, `OnScanEnded(Finished)`, after v1's reports; read back through the same handle, first by the verification pass V in physical order at P5 (SPOOL-23), then by the importer in key order (IMP-03); closed after T-IMPORT commits, or at any earlier stop. It is **append-only**: no byte is ever rewritten or patched after it is written |
| SPOOL-08 | Cancellation | Closed, and so deleted, at the end of the cancelled scan or the cancelled import |
| SPOOL-09 | Failed scan | Closed and deleted. Unlike v1's report files, which v1 lists as incomplete artefacts, the spool has no value to the user and its ownership is proven by the exclusive handle held since creation. It never appears in `StorageScanResult.IncompleteArtifacts`: it no longer exists when a Cancelled or Failed result is returned |
| SPOOL-10 | Report failure | The scan is not Finished, so there is no import (LIFE-08); closed and deleted |
| SPOOL-11 | **Spool write failure** | The isolated observer is faulted (class A) and immediately disposes its buffer and closes the handle, which deletes the file and frees its space for the reports; the capture becomes `NotEligible (SpoolWriteFailed)`. The same release happens when the spool gives way to the reports (SPOOL-24), recorded as `ReportVolumeNearlyFull` or `ReportVolumeSpaceUnknown` |
| SPOOL-12 | **Seal and digest** | The writer feeds **every byte it writes, in write order**, to an incremental SHA-256; since the file is append-only, write order is physical order. At `OnScanEnded(Finished)` it writes the run index and the trailer fields (SPOOL-20), then the 32-byte digest of **every byte before the digest** (header, records, folder section, run index and trailer fields; bytes `[0, L − 32)` of a file of length `L`), then flushes. The digest is the file's last 32 bytes; nothing follows it. No `fsync`: the spool is never read after a crash. P5 checks that the trailer's counts and totals equal `StorageScanResult.Totals` (files, folders, bytes, scan errors, reparse points skipped, file reparse points) |
| SPOOL-13 | Database-import failure | T-IMPORT rolls back; the spool is closed and deleted; the reports stay valid |
| SPOOL-14 | **Deletion mechanism** | `DeleteOnClose`: the OS deletes the file when its last handle closes. The three v1 proofs hold by construction (created by this run with `CreateNew`, directly in the validated folder, with the exact own name, held exclusively ever since). `DeleteOnClose` appears only in `ReportRun` (A-02) |
| SPOOL-15 | **App crash, process termination** | The OS closes the handle and deletes the file (documented Win32 behaviour of delete-on-close handles; Q-20 confirms it with antivirus, SMB and OneDrive report folders) |
| SPOOL-16 | Disk full | A write fails → SPOOL-11. Projected size about 80 B per file (PERF-13, measured in C5, Q-18), below the Files report of the same scan (measured about 254 to 266 B per file on the benchmark trees). The spool yields space to the reports before they run out, under the bounds SPOOL-24 states |
| SPOOL-17 | **Stale spool** (power loss or OS crash only) | The file remains as `History_<runId>.spool.tmp`. It is **never imported** and **never deleted automatically**: after a restart nothing proves ownership, and v1 has no delete-by-name. The Interrupted attempt's message names the report folder and file as plain text (never a link, SEC-15) and says it may be deleted |
| SPOOL-18 | Cleanup summary | Automatic deletion happens only by closing the delete-on-close handle this process created. Never by name, never for another run's file |
| SPOOL-19 | Finished but incomplete scans | The spool carries every finalised folder record with its status and every error record; the snapshot is published as Incomplete with its completeness evidence (LIFE-09) |
| SPOOL-20 | **Format 1 (one file)** | Little-endian. **Header:** magic `SISPOOL1`, format `1` (u16), flags (u16), capture token (16 bytes), run ID (u16 length + UTF-16), `scanner_contract` (u16), created UTC ticks (i64). **Records** (tag u8 + payload): `0x01` file: folder index (i32), name (u16 length + exact UTF-16 code units), size (i64), created, modified and accessed ticks (i64 each, `i64.MinValue` = null), attributes (i32); the sequence is the record's position among file records. `0x02` error: type (u8), path (u32 length + UTF-16, as v1 reports it), message (u32 length + UTF-16). `0x03` finalised folder: index and parent index (i32), name (UTF-16), status, status reason (0xFF = none) and `subtree_complete` (u8 each), attributes (i32), created and modified ticks, direct and total bytes, files and subfolders (i64 ×6), largest file bytes (i64 or null). **Run index:** per file run in emission order, (folder index i32, offset i64, count i32). **Trailer:** tag `0x7F`, counts, totals, offsets of the folder section and run index, then the SHA-256 digest (32 bytes) of every preceding byte of the file (SPOOL-12). **Order:** header, then file and error records in emission order, then the folder section, then the run index, then the trailer; nothing is written out of this order |
| SPOOL-21 | **Memory bounds** | Writer: one 1 MiB buffer, the incremental SHA-256 state, and the run index (16 B per folder: about 7.5 MB at 470k folders; INV-13's "grows with the folder count" is unchanged in kind). Reader and importer: IMP-08 |
| SPOOL-22 | **Performance expectations** | Projected, not measured: sequential appends of about 80 B per file add at most 10% to v1's enumeration time (PERF-02a). C5 measures on the 250k tree and on 1M and 2M generated trees (Q-18) |
| SPOOL-23 | **Verification pass V (D-50)** | At P5, before **any** record is used, one sequential read of the whole file from offset 0 through the live handle, in physical order: (1) the length `L` is at least header plus trailer, and SHA-256 of bytes `[0, L − 32)` equals the last 32 bytes; (2) the header: magic, format 1, and the capture token equal to the in-memory token; (3) the record region, parsed record by record up to the folder-section offset: every tag is `0x01` or `0x02`, every length is within bounds, and the counts per tag equal the trailer's; (4) the file runs are rebuilt from the record stream (a maximal sequence of file records with one folder index, errors allowed between them; no folder index may start two runs) and must equal the stored run index entry for entry; (5) the folder section: the trailer's count, ascending indexes, `parentIndex < index`, and totals recomputed from it equal the trailer's and `StorageScanResult.Totals`; (6) the trailer's tag and offsets are consistent with the positions found. Any failure → `NotEligible (SpoolInvalid)` (O10). Only after V passes may the importer seek by the run index and read in key order (IMP-03). The spool is held exclusively and never written after sealing, so those reads return the bytes V verified; silent corruption by the storage stack between the two reads is outside the integrity model, as for every file StorageInventory reads. V costs one extra sequential read and IMP-03's error pass another over the record region, both usually from the OS cache and both part of PERF-14. **What V proves, and what it does not (G0F-M07):** the digest proves byte integrity relative to the seal: any byte changed, removed or added after the seal without recomputing the digest is detected. Steps (3) to (6) prove structural consistency, which holds even against a rewrite that recomputes the digest: tags, lengths, counts, run membership (a folder's run split in two, or a file record moved into another folder's run, changes the rebuilt run list), the run-index order, the folder section and the trailer. Neither proves *semantic* content or order against an outside authority: a rewrite that keeps every structural invariant and recomputes the digest is accepted. Examples: two file records of the **same** folder exchanged within its run (format 1 has no per-record sequence field, and the run index is unchanged); names or timestamps exchanged between records while every folder index stays in place (sizes that no longer add up to a folder's direct totals are then caught by T-IMPORT's §9.5 checks, IMP-05 (c), equal sizes are not). The record order is normative (it is `file_obs.seq`, the tie order of D-48), and it is enforced where it can be: by the writer (append-only, emission order, SPOOL-07; TEST-SP1's exact round trip), by the exclusive handle held from creation to deletion (no other writer can reach the file, SPOOL-04), and end to end by TEST-H2's parity with the CSV report. Only a StorageInventory defect could produce such a rewrite, and those tests are what catch it |
| SPOOL-24 | **Bounded space: the spool gives way to the reports (G0-M07, G0F-M06)** | **Purpose.** The spool must never be the reason the report folder's volume runs out: when a check fails, the spool releases all its space before v1 needs it, and the reports are then byte-identical to a Save-off run. **Inputs at a check** (exact unless marked): `F`, the bytes free to this caller on the report volume (`GetDiskFreeSpaceExW` on the report folder, its caller-available value, so quotas count; already in the native set, §7.8) and `Cap`, the volume's capacity (same call); `T`, in sorted mode, the bytes written so far to the run's temporary Files file, whose rows the sorted Files report copies byte for byte after enumeration (`ReportWriters.WriteSortedFiles`), and 0 in unsorted mode, whose Files report is complete when enumeration ends; `n_d`, the folders recorded so far; `r̄`, the run's mean Files row size so far (Files bytes written ÷ files, 256 B before the first file); `D`, the drop in `F` since the previous check (0 at the first). **Reserve** = `M` + `I` + `P`: **`M`**, the margin (defined) = max(64 MiB, 1% of `Cap`), at most 1 GiB: buffered bytes not yet on disk (the spool's 1 MiB buffer, v1's 1 MiB Files buffer and 64 KiB writers), filesystem metadata and other programs; **`I`**, the interval allowance (defined) = 2 × max(4 MiB, `D`): what the volume may lose before the next check, from the loss actually measured over the last interval (the run's own writes and any other program's), doubled; 0 at the post-enumeration check, after which the spool writes only its tail; **`P`**, what v1 will write after enumeration for what is known so far = `T` + the Files header + `n_d` × (2 × `r̄` + 256 B) (+ the spool's tail, exactly, at the post-enumeration check): the sorted Files report (exact) plus the Folders report (**projected**: a Folders row carries at most one more path than a Files row of its folder plus 13 more numeric, date and status fields, `ReportWriters.WriteFolders`; Q-18 measures it in C5). **Cadence:** (1) before the spool is created; (2) after every 4 MiB written by the run, the spool and v1's report writers together (both write on the scanning thread; `ReportRun` keeps the shared count, so a run whose CSV rows are much longer than its spool records is still checked often); (3) after the last finalised folder, before v1 writes the Folders report and the sorted Files report: the root is finalised first and its `TotalSubfolderCount` gives the index of the last folder (`FolderAggregator.cs:73`). A 2M-file sorted scan makes about 170 checks of one metadata query each. **At P0** the same reserve with `P` = 0 and `D` = 0 is evaluated read-only: below it, or if the query fails, "Save to History" is unavailable for this scan with the reason (O24) and no attempt is recorded. **Outcome:** `F` ≥ reserve → continue. `F` < reserve → the spool releases itself (SPOOL-11: buffers dropped, handle closed, file deleted by delete-on-close) and the capture becomes `NotEligible (ReportVolumeNearlyFull)` ("the report folder's drive is nearly full; saving needed about N MB more"); at check (1) the spool is never created. The query fails → the same release, as `NotEligible (ReportVolumeSpaceUnknown)`: an unknown free space is never treated as enough. The scan continues either way, and the reports are never touched by this rule. **Guarantee, with its bounds:** the spool is released before the volume's free space can fall below `M` while it exists, provided (a) between two checks the volume loses at most `I` (twice the loss measured over the previous interval, at least 8 MiB), and (b) the Folders report is no larger than its projection. Under the same bounds, everything v1 writes after the post-enumeration check fits beside the spool, leaving `M` for buffers, metadata and other programs. Outside them (another program filling the volume faster; a projection Q-18 shows too small) a report write can still fail first: v1's own handling applies (O6, O7), the capture is not published (LIFE-08) and the spool is deleted with the run, as before. **Very small volumes:** with `Cap` = 100 MiB the first check needs 72 MiB free, so a small or nearly full report volume never holds a spool and the scan runs exactly as v1. **Cleanup:** release is the only action. StorageInventory deletes only its own spool, through its own handle (SPOOL-18); it never deletes or moves any other file to make room. **Measured and assumed:** measured, v1's Files report is 63.5 MB for 250,000 files, about 254 to 266 B per file, on the benchmark trees (`docs/benchmarks/native.md`); fact from code, the sorted report is a byte-for-byte copy of the temporary file's rows, so sorted mode briefly needs about twice the Files report; projected and no longer used by the rule, the spool's 80 B per file (PERF-13), which puts the Files report at 3.2 to 3.3 times the spool, above `121358c`'s factor of 3; assumed (defined values, confirmed or revised in C5 by Q-18), `M`, the factor 2 and the 4 MiB cadence in `I`, and the Folders row bound. Preflight also shows the projected spool size ("Saving needs about N MB in the report folder while scanning") when an earlier snapshot of the source gives a file count |

### 11.8 The post-scan import (IMP-01 to IMP-11)

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| IMP-01 | Runs only after P5 holds, inside the capture's Save lease: the scan returned `Finished`, the spool is sealed, passed V (SPOOL-23) and matches, and both tripwires (INV-19, ID-10) passed |
| IMP-02 | One transaction, T-IMPORT: the writer connection is opened inside the Save lease (OBS-12), acquires the in-process gate (CONC-06), then `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`; `synchronous = FULL`; import `cache_size` 64 MiB |
| IMP-03 | **Key order.** (1) Resolve or create the volume and source rows from the P0 decision, re-reading them inside the transaction (`SourceChanged` if a chosen row is gone). (2) Insert the snapshot row (`state = 1`, sealed totals) with `snapshot_id = next_snapshot_id`. (3) Read the folder section; intern folder paths in ascending discovery index (parents first) to build the folder-index → `path_id` map; insert `folder_obs`. (4) Sort the run index by `path_id`; for each run, seek to it, read its file records, intern their names in the source's dictionary (IMP-04, SCH-04), sort the run by `name_id` and insert `file_obs` with `seq`. A run longer than 262,144 files is inserted in spool order unsorted (correct, only slower). (5) Stream error records into `scan_error` with root-relative paths, in one more sequential pass over the record region that skips file records. (6) Insert `snapshot_extension_total` from totals accumulated while reading. Key order was 2.3 to 2.9 times faster than shuffled order in the probe [E-3]. Interning each chunk's names in key order was prototyped by the C4 design review and **not adopted**: it moved the cost into the in-transaction verification (the `name_id`s of one folder no longer lie together) without a net gain |
| IMP-04 | **Name cache per capture and per source** (REV-M06, D-52): created when T-IMPORT begins and discarded when it ends, committed or rolled back, so a rolled-back `name_id` can never be reused. Bounded LRU of 262,144 entries backed by lookups of the source's `name` range inside the transaction |
| IMP-05 | **Verification inside the transaction, before the final statements:** (a) V passed at P5 for this spool handle (SPOOL-23; the digest is never recomputed from key-ordered reads); (b) the numbers of rows inserted equal the trailer and `StorageScanResult.Totals`; (c) the §9.5 invariants 1 to 12 hold for this `snapshot_id` (aggregate SQL); (d) the attempt row exists with this session's token, this capture's token and outcome `InProgress`. Any failure → `ROLLBACK`, `SnapshotFailed (InvariantViolation)`. The verification checks the save token as CAN-01d states; a cancellation there is `SaveCancelled`, never `InvariantViolation` |
| IMP-06 | **Final statements**, each required to change exactly one row: `UPDATE snapshot SET state = 2, published_utc = ? WHERE snapshot_id = ? AND state = 1`; `UPDATE scan_attempt SET outcome = 2, source_id = ?, ended_utc = ? WHERE attempt_id = ? AND session_token = ? AND capture_token = ? AND outcome = 1`; `UPDATE library_info SET next_snapshot_id = next_snapshot_id + 1 WHERE next_snapshot_id = ?`. Then `COMMIT`, the validity point |
| IMP-07 | Cancellation: CAN-01d |
| IMP-08 | **Memory:** page cache 64 MiB; the folder map (8 B per folder); one run buffer (at most 262,144 records); the name cache; the extension totals. Projected peak ≤ v1 peak + 300 MB at 2M files (PERF-11b) |
| IMP-09 | **Performance and journal (measured, C4 design review; D-52).** T-IMPORT is CPU-bound. Beyond its appends, its cost is the dictionary: every name not in the name cache is a probe of the source's name index, and every new name is an insertion that journals, once, the existing leaf it lands in (G0-O03; PERF-15 (a)). With C4's mechanics (a Library-wide dictionary visited in shuffled order) a 1M-file import into a 3M-file Library ran at 36k to 73k rows/s for interleaved, mixed or re-scan names and journalled 39 to 89 MiB, while names sorting past the end of the dictionary journalled 0.2 MiB (the C4 benchmark's case, C4-B01). Per-source dictionaries (SCH-04) are the response: a new source's names go into a new key range, so its import is append-shaped in every B-tree and journals only the fixed pages, and a known source's import touches only its own range. Budgets PERF-01, PERF-14, PERF-15; Q-17; TEST-P1 |
| IMP-10 | After `COMMIT`: release the gate, close the writer, close the spool (deleted), end the Save lease (OBS-10), refresh the UI |
| IMP-11 | **Library space: preflight and guard (D-52; C4 provides the primitive's hook with its pending-growth reading and the footprint query; C5 wires the rule).** **Purpose.** StorageInventory keeps its own conservative reserve on the Library's volume, usually the system drive: T-IMPORT does not start, or rolls back, as soon as its model of what the import may still write no longer fits in the free space above a margin. The import needs room for the snapshot's growth `G` and its rollback journal `J` at the same time; a rollback or a hot-journal recovery needs no new space and returns both (original page images are written back in place, the main file is truncated to its size at `BEGIN`, the journal to 0 bytes). **What it does not promise.** Another program can consume space at any moment, including between a check and the write it covers; no check can prevent that. External consumption is a **residual race**: IMP-11 sees it at its next check and rolls back, or `SQLITE_FULL` ends the import first, with the same rollback, no damage and the same outcome (O13). IMP-11 never claims that the volume cannot cross the margin under arbitrary outside activity. **Inputs** (exact unless marked): `F_L` and `Cap_L`, the bytes free to the caller and the capacity of the Library's volume (`GetDiskFreeSpaceExW` on the Library directory, through Core's existing native method, §7.8; the Library assembly has no P/Invoke); at P5, the spool's file and folder counts `n_f` and `n_d` and the sum `B_n` of the UTF-16 byte lengths of every file and folder name (summed by pass V while it parses each record, SPOOL-23 (3)); the target source's **dictionary footprint**: its name count `E_n`, the sum `E_b` of those names' UTF-16 bytes and its folder-path count `E_p` (one read-only aggregate on a reader connection before T-IMPORT; all zero for a new source); **at every check during T-IMPORT**, `P` = `PRAGMA page_count` on the writer inside the transaction, `S` = the page size and `L` = the main file's length through a handle, giving the **pending main-file growth** `Λ` = max(0, `P` × `S` − `L`): pages of the transaction's database image that the main file does not hold yet (new pages still in the page cache), which a cache spill or `COMMIT` will write (C4DR-M01: 33 to 54 MiB landed after the old last check). **Projections** (defined; confirmed or revised in C5 by Q-24): growth `G_p` = 110 B × `n_f` + 120 B × `n_d` + 2 × `B_n` (a name is stored twice, row and index); journal `J_p` = min(2 × (`E_b` + 16 B × `E_n` + 24 B × `E_p`), 4,104 B × (`n_f` + `n_d`)) + 1 MiB: the twice-the-key-bytes term covers B-tree pages at least half full and one 4,104-byte record per page (PERF-15 (a)); the per-row term is an allowance, not a strict bound (rebalancing can journal more than one page per insertion; the guard covers it). **Margin** `M_L` = max(256 MiB, 1% of `Cap_L`), at most 2 GiB (defined). **Check points.** (1) **P0**, read-only: `F_L` < `M_L` + `G_p` + `J_p`, with the projections from the source's latest published snapshot when there is one (`B_n` taken as 100 B per name) and 0 otherwise, or the query fails → Save to History is unavailable for this scan with the reason and no attempt is recorded (O24). (2) **P5**, before `BEGIN`, with the exact counts: `F_L` < `M_L` + `G_p` + `J_p`, or the query fails → T-IMPORT does not start; `SnapshotFailed (LibraryFull)`, "the drive holding the Library has N MB free; saving needs about M MB" (O13). (3) **Guard during T-IMPORT**: periodic checks at `BEGIN` and after every 4,096 inserted rows of any table, and a **final check** immediately before `COMMIT`, after the in-transaction verification and the final statements (nothing runs between it and `COMMIT`). The free space required at a check k is, at a periodic check, `R_k` = `M_L` + `Λ_k` + `I_L`, with the interval allowance `I_L` = 2 × max(16 MiB, `D_k`) and `D_k` the drop in `F_L` since the previous check; at the final check, `R_f` = `M_L` + `Λ_f` + `R_C`, with the **commit reserve** `R_C` = 1 MiB (defined; Q-24). T-IMPORT continues when `F_L` ≥ the required free space (**equality continues**); `F_L` below it, or any input that cannot be read (the free-space query, `page_count`, the page size, the main file's length), rolls T-IMPORT back: `SnapshotFailed (LibraryFull)`, "the drive holding the Library became nearly full while saving" (O13). **Why these terms.** `Λ` is what the page cache holds and the file does not: `COMMIT` writes exactly that (measured in the C4 design repair: the main file grew at `COMMIT` by the final check's `Λ` to the byte in every instrumented run, 0.9 to 35 MB), and a spill can write part of it between two checks (measured: up to 2.0 MB during a verification), so every check includes it. The final check needs no journal term: every page `COMMIT` writes was journalled when first changed, so `COMMIT` appends no journal record (measured: no journal growth at `COMMIT` in any instrumented run) before truncating the journal; nor a rollback or recovery term, because a rollback writes no new bytes. `R_C` covers what `Λ` cannot see (file-system allocation rounding, metadata). `I_L` is the allowance for one interval's own growth (its rows, two copies of each new name, a journal record for each existing page first changed): about 1.5 MB per 4,096 rows in the repair's representative and stress cells and 11.3 MB in its worst-case existing-source cell, against at least 32 MiB (these are the largest 16,384-row interval's growth scaled by 1/4, so averages over 16,384 rows, not maxima over 4,096; TEST-P1 records every 4,096-row interval, C4DRR-O02); it is **an allowance, not a bound** (4,096 rows of new 255-character names each landing in an untouched leaf, with rebalancing, could in theory exceed it), and an interval that exceeds it is caught at the next check, having used at most the excess of the margin. **The model, stated exactly:** if, between two consecutive checks, the import's own growth (main file plus journal) stays within `Λ_k` + `I_L`, and after the final check within `Λ_f` + `R_C`, then the import alone never takes the volume below `M_L` free. The second condition is exact **for the main file's length** by construction: SQLite's commit phase one extends the file to `page_count` × page size, so `COMMIT` adds exactly `Λ_f` (measured to the byte in 25 committed runs, which are 9 deterministic configurations repeated), given no `SQLITE_FCNTL_CHUNK_SIZE`, no auto-vacuum and clusters of at most 1 MiB. The step from file length to free space (allocation rounding, metadata) is what `R_C` covers; it is not exact and is measured by Q-24 and TEST-L9's C5 case (C4DRR-O03). The first is verified by measurement (TEST-P1, TEST-L9 and Q-24 record every interval's growth against its allowance). A preflight refusal or a guard stop deletes or moves nothing; StorageInventory never frees space by touching any other file. **C4's primitive** consults a guard callback at each check with the check's kind (`BEGIN`, rows, final), `Λ`, the journal's length and the rows so far, and checks the save token there (CAN-01d); a "stop" answer, an exception from the guard or an unreadable input rolls back as `LibraryFull` (class A). **Cost:** about 610 checks at 2M files, each a metadata call, a `page_count` read and a handle length. Tested by TEST-L9 |

### 11.9 Results and API compatibility (SINK-07)

- `IStorageInventoryScanner`, `StorageScanOptions` and `StorageScanResult` are **unchanged and public**. v1 callers
  and tests compile and behave identically; `ScanAsync`/`Scan` run the same internal pipeline without a capture.
- `InventoryScanner` gains two **internal** methods: `PrepareCapture(StorageScanOptions) → PreparedCapture` (validates
  exactly as `Scan` does and names the run; creates nothing) and `RunCapture(IObservationPermit, PreparedCapture, IProgress<StorageScanProgress>?, CancellationToken) → CaptureScanOutcome { StorageScanResult Scan; ObservationSpool? Spool; SpoolFault? Fault; bool Catastrophic; }`.
  `IObservationPermit` is a Core-internal interface whose only implementation is the interlock's observation lease
  (A-25); `RunCapture` checks it is current before enumeration starts and at P4 (OBS-15). `Catastrophic` reports a
  class C exception that the scanner's top-level handler turned into `Failed (Unexpected)` (CAT-03).
- **In `StorageInventory.Library`** (the SQLite adapter, D-41): `LibrarySession`, `LibraryDatabase`, `LibraryStore`,
  `SnapshotImporter`, the catalogue and the row streams, and the `InventoryRun` orchestrator that performs P0 to P7.
  `InventoryRun` takes the capture's Prepare `MutationLease` (Save on) or an `ObservationLease` (Save off) and is the
  only caller of `RunCapture`; every scan the App runs, Save on or off, goes through it (A-25).
  Its result is `InventoryRunResult { StorageScanResult Scan; SnapshotCaptureResult Snapshot; }`, with
  `SnapshotCaptureResult.Outcome` one of `NotRequested | Published | NotEligible | Failed | SaveCancelled | Declined`,
  plus `SnapshotId?`, `SourceId?`, `Failure?` (a `CaptureFailureKind` and message) and `Timings`. The scan result is
  delivered to the App as soon as the scan returns, before saving starts (UI-10).
- **In `StorageInventory.History`** (package-free, D-41): identity matching, the Library path rules, the comparison
  engine and its oracle, history arithmetic, and the row-stream interfaces the Library implements.
- `ScanPhase` gains the appended value `SavingToHistory`, reported by `InventoryRun`, never by the v1 scanner.
  Appending is compatible, and the v1 UI maps unknown phases safely. TEST-O5 (Core's public surface equals v1's) is
  re-baselined once, in C5, to allow exactly this value (G0-O07).
- **The PowerShell reference** keeps its authority over v1 traversal and report semantics. **Persistence is not
  reimplemented in PowerShell** (D-19). Parity tests keep running on the refactored scanner (C1).

---

## 12. Diff semantics and truth table

This section makes INV-16 mechanical. The `c0c031f` definition proved absence by **exact** path, which is unsound on
Windows' case-insensitive directories (REV-B02; [E-6]: 2,482 to 2,728 unsound verdicts per 20,000 generated worlds).
The repaired definition (D-38) aligns folders first, then proves absence with a comparison key only where the
name matching of **both** snapshots is verified to be contained in that key; elsewhere, absence needs a proof that
does not depend on name matching at all, or the result is Unknown (NAME-03, NAME-04, NAME-09). Every proof of absence
also needs listings that show every entry: where that cannot be proven, as on every network share, nothing is ever
reported Added or Missing (DIFF-14, L-DIFF3; G0F-H01).

### 12.1 Scope and preconditions (DIFF-01, DIFF-10)

- **Only published snapshots of the same source are compared:** `Compare(PublishedSnapshotId older, PublishedSnapshotId newer)`,
  with `older.source_id == newer.source_id`. Comparing across sources is refused (ID-11).
- **"Older" and "newer" follow capture order** (`snapshot_id`, SCH-11), never the wall clock (REV-M04). The API
  normalises the order and states it in the result. If the snapshots' `finished_utc` order disagrees with capture
  order, the result carries a **clock anomaly** flag and the UI shows both dates (D-44).
- Comparing a snapshot with itself is valid (DIFF-P5).
- **DIFF-10 (input verification, D-47).** While streaming each snapshot, the engine counts folders and files and sums
  bytes, and compares them with that snapshot's sealed totals. Any mismatch aborts the comparison with "The stored data
  of this snapshot is inconsistent" and no partial result; the Library is flagged for verification. A comparison can
  therefore never present Missing or Added computed from rows that a defeated commit left behind (LIFE-11).
- The identity banner of ID-09 applies. For sources with basis `UserAsserted`, Missing reads "missing at this
  location". Network sources (basis `Location`) report no Missing or Added (L-DIFF3).

### 12.2 Alignment and the case-aware absence proof (DIFF-11, DIFF-12)

**Observations.** For a published snapshot `S`: every observed item has an exact path (a sequence of exact names,
NAME-01) and a type (file or folder); every observed folder `f` has a status. `Listed_S(f)` ⇔ `f` is present in `S`
and `status(S, f) = Ok`: its listing ran to the end. `Partial`, `Unreadable` and `ReparsePointSkipped` are **not**
Listed. Whether a listing that ran to the end showed **every** entry is a separate question, the comparison's
listing visibility `V` (DIFF-14). `K` is the comparison key (NAME-03). `(V, A)` is the comparison's capability:
listing visibility `V`, `Complete` or `Unverified`, and matching assurance `A`, `Verified` or `Unverified`, both
derived from the two snapshots together (DIFF-14). `Dom(x, f)` holds when the name `x` and every child of `f` of `x`'s
type are in the verified domain (NAME-09).

**DIFF-14 Comparison capability (G0F-H01, G0F-M04).** Computed once per comparison from **both** snapshots' own
records (`snapshot.capture_kind`, `snapshot.fs_name`, §9.2), never from the source row, the drive as it is now, or one
snapshot alone, and never stored:

- `V = Complete` ⇔ **both** snapshots were captured through a local volume (`capture_kind = LocalVolume`) whose
  filesystem name is on the release's checked-in *complete-listing list*, which C7 fills from Q-23's evidence (a class
  is listed only if a listing of a folder the account can read is shown to contain every entry, whatever the entries'
  own permissions and attributes). Otherwise `V = Unverified`: every network capture (a UNC path or a mapped letter:
  SMB access-based enumeration, DFS and NAS policies, and account or ACL changes between scans can hide entries from a
  listing that still succeeds), every local filesystem not on the list, and every class before its Q-23 evidence
  passes (the safe default: an empty list). The premise for a listed class is the one v1's own `Complete` already
  rests on: v1 lists with `AttributesToSkip = 0` and `IgnoreInaccessible = false`, so hidden and system entries are
  returned and an unreadable folder is reported, not skipped.
- `A = Verified` ⇔ `V = Complete` **and** both snapshots recorded the **same** filesystem name **and** that class is on
  the verified-class list (NAME-04, Q-14). Otherwise `A = Unverified`: a snapshot of an unverified or unknown class, two
  snapshots of different classes (for example an NTFS source and a UDF capture attached to it by the user's answer,
  ID-04), or any snapshot whose listing visibility is unverified. Neither snapshot's matching is used as authority for
  the other's namespace.
- **Relevant folders.** One filesystem name covers every folder of a snapshot: v1 never enters a reparse point, volume
  mount points included (INV-03, X-7), so every observed folder lies on the volume whose name was recorded. Per-directory
  case sensitivity needs no capability: exact matching is contained in any per-unit fold (§8.3 table).
- The two axes nest: `A = Verified` implies `V = Complete`. Both are symmetric in the two snapshots, so DIFF-P6 holds.
- The comparison result states its capability, and the UI shows the matching banner (§14.4).

**DIFF-11 Alignment** (a relation between folders of O and folders of N, built top-down):

1. The roots are aligned (same source, identity re-verified at capture, ID-10).
2. For each aligned pair `(a_O, a_N)`, children are matched in two steps:
   1. **Exact:** an O child and an N child with exactly the same name and the same type are matched.
   2. **Case-fold (DIFF-07):** only if `V = Complete`, **both** `a_O` and `a_N` are Listed, and `Dom(c, a_O)` and
      `Dom(c', a_N)` hold, whatever `A` is (so the alignment never depends on the assurance level, and the uniqueness
      test below is a claim about complete listings). An unmatched O child `c` and an unmatched N child `c'` of the same type with
      `K(c) = K(c')` are matched if `c` is the **only** child of `a_O` of that type with that key and `c'` is the
      **only** child of `a_N` of that type with that key, **counting matched children**.
3. Matched folder pairs are aligned (and the rule recurses); matched file pairs are compared (§12.5). Nothing else is
   aligned. An aligned pair is **exactly aligned** when every step from the roots to it was an exact match (step 2.1).

**DIFF-12 Proven absence.** Let `p` be an **unmatched** O item. Let `(a_O, a_N)` be the aligned pair of `p`'s deepest
aligned proper ancestor, let `c` be the child of `a_O` on the path to `p` (`c = p` when `p` is a direct child), and let
`T` be `c`'s type (a folder when `p` lies strictly below `c`). Define

- `Cand_N(a_N, c, T)` = the children of `a_N` of type `T` with `K(x) = K(c)`, **matched or not**;
- `Kids_N(a_N, T)` = all children of `a_N` of type `T`, matched or not;
- the **name-independent proof** `NI_N(p)` ⇔ `Listed_N(a_N)` **and** `(a_O, a_N)` is exactly aligned **and**
  `Kids_N(a_N, T) = ∅`. No name comparison is involved: nothing of type `T` exists anywhere in the listing;
- the **key-based proof** `KB_N(p)` ⇔ `Listed_N(a_N)` **and** `Dom(c, a_N)` **and** `Cand_N(a_N, c, T) = ∅`;
- **`ProvenAbsent_N(p)` ⇔ `V = Complete` and (`NI_N(p)` or (`A = Verified` and `KB_N(p)`))**. Under `V = Unverified`
  nothing is ever proven absent, whatever the listing shows. Under `V = Complete` both assurance levels accept the
  name-independent proof, and `Verified` adds the key-based one (G0F-M05: `121358c` gave `Verified` the key-based proof
  only, so lowering the assurance could turn an Unknown into a Missing, T59).

`p` is **Missing** iff `ProvenAbsent_N(p)`; otherwise it is **Unknown** with side `Newer` and the first reason that
applies:

| Condition | Unknown reason |
|---|---|
| `a_N` is Unreadable | `AncestorUnreadable` |
| `a_N` is Partial | `ListingIncomplete` |
| `a_N` is ReparsePointSkipped | `BehindLink` |
| `a_N` is Listed and `V = Unverified` | `VisibilityUnverified` (the listing may not have shown every item here, for example on a network share, so StorageInventory can't prove this item is gone) |
| `a_N` is Listed, `A = Verified`, and `Dom(c, a_N)` fails | `NameMatchingUnverified` (a name uses a character newer than the pinned Unicode version) |
| `a_N` is Listed, `A = Verified`, and `Cand_N` is not empty | `NameVariantPresent` (another child has the same name apart from letter case or Unicode encoding) |
| `a_N` is Listed, `A = Unverified`, and `Kids_N(a_N, T)` is not empty or the pair is not exactly aligned | `NameMatchingUnverified` (this drive's rules for matching names are not verified, so another item here could be the same one) |

Symmetrically, an unmatched N item `q` is **Added** iff `ProvenAbsent_O(q)` (the same definition with O and N
exchanged), otherwise **Unknown** with side `Older` and the reason taken from `a_O`. A Missing item whose `a_N` has a child of the other type with key `K(c)` carries the
informational sub-reason `ReplacedByFolder` / `ReplacedByFile`; Added likewise `ReplacedFile` / `ReplacedFolder`.

**Soundness argument (why Missing is proof).** *Visibility.* Both proofs read an absence from a listing, so both need
the premise that a listing that ran to the end showed every entry of its folder. `V = Complete` is claimed only where
Q-23 has shown that premise for the filesystem class of **both** snapshots; under `V = Unverified` no absence is read
at all, so a server that hides entries (G0F-H01) can make StorageInventory say less, never something false. An exact
match needs no such premise: an entry seen under the same exact name in both listings occupies that name's position
in both. *The name-independent proof.* An exact match is the true position in any directory under any name matching
(below), so an exactly aligned `a_N` is the true position of `a_O`; if `a_N` was listed completely and holds no child
of type `T` at all, nothing of type `T` was at `c`'s position, whatever the filesystem treats as the same name. No
premise about name matching is used, which is why both assurance levels accept it. *The key-based proof.* The premise
is that the name matching of the snapshot being read is contained in `K` for names in the verified domain (NAME-04,
NAME-09); `A = Verified` requires it for **both** snapshots (DIFF-14), because Missing reads the newer listing and
Added the older one, and every step below that relies on `K` also requires `Dom`, so the premise is used only where it
holds. By induction down the alignment, each aligned N folder is either the true position of its O folder, or that O
folder's true position is **absent** in N:

- An exact match is always the true position: in any directory, the entry whose exact name is `c` is the only entry of
  `c`'s equivalence class.
- A case-fold match happens only under two complete listings, and only when `c'` is the sole same-type child of
  `a_N` with key `K(c)`. Any entry at `c`'s position in N would have a key equal to `K(c)` and type `T`, so it is
  either `c'` (then `c'` is the true position) or does not exist (then `c` and everything below it are truly absent,
  and any Missing claimed below it is correct).

Now take a Missing `p` whose aligned `a_N` is the true position of `a_O`. `a_N` was listed completely, and no child of
`a_N` of type `T` has key `K(c)`; any entry at `c`'s position would have one. So nothing of type `T` exists at `c`'s
position, and `p`, which is `c` or lies below it, did not exist in N when `a_N` was listed. **If `a_N` is not Listed,
or a same-type child with key `K(c)` exists (matched or not), absence proves nothing, and the result is Unknown.** The
argument needs no knowledge of per-directory case sensitivity, of changes to it between scans, or of the exact
filesystem fold, only that `K` is at least as wide. (A scan is not an instantaneous snapshot: Missing means "absent
when its parent folder was listed", REV-O12; the help text says so.) [E-6] tests **the rule** on a toy model (a fold of
ASCII letters, a key wider by one character): 0 unsound verdicts in 100,000 generated worlds with a key wider than the
filesystem's and 100,000 with an equal key, against 2,482 to 2,728 per 20,000 worlds for the exact-name key. It is
evidence about the rule, not about `K` (G0-O09); `K`'s width is NAME-04's per-unit condition ([E-9]) and Q-14's census.
[E-11] extends the model with this revision's capability axes: names outside the verified domain, arbitrary folds,
two snapshots of different classes, and servers that hide entries from Ok listings. The corrected rule gave 0 unsound
verdicts in 240,000 world pairs, and the `121358c` rule about 325 per 20,000 mixed-class pairs (G0F-M04) and about
4,600 per 20,000 network pairs (G0F-H01).

**Why not the review's proposed rule.** The review's proposal (its §10.6) treats two listed case variants as proof that the directory
was case-sensitive, and lets a variant already matched to another item fail to block the proof. Both fail when the key
is wider than the filesystem's fold or when case sensitivity changed between scans ([E-6]: 45 to 71 unsound verdicts per
20,000 worlds). An earlier draft of this repair that counted only *unmatched* variants for the case-fold match also
failed on a targeted case ([E-6], T41). Rule 2.2's "counting matched children" closes it.

### 12.3 File classification (DIFF-02)

`O` is the older snapshot, `N` the newer.

| O | N | Class | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| file | file (exact or case-fold match) | **Changed** if the metadata differs (DIFF-04), **Unknown (ChangeIndeterminate)** under DIFF-05, else **Unchanged** | Case-fold matches carry the flag `NameSpelling` |
| file | unmatched, `ProvenAbsent_N` | **Missing** | "Last seen": O (REQ-16) |
| file | unmatched, **not** `ProvenAbsent_N` | **Unknown** (side Newer, reason from §12.2) | **Never Missing** |
| file | a folder at the same exact name, and `ProvenAbsent_N` | **Missing** (`ReplacedByFolder`) | Proven: N observed a folder there and, under §12.2's rule for the comparison's capability, no file that could be the same; otherwise the row above (Unknown) applies |
| unmatched, `ProvenAbsent_O` | file | **Added** | |
| unmatched, **not** `ProvenAbsent_O` | file | **Unknown** (side Older, reason from §12.2) | Present now, but we can't say it is new |
| a folder at the same exact name, and `ProvenAbsent_O` | file | **Added** (`ReplacedFolder`) | Otherwise Unknown (side Older) |

Under `V = Unverified` (DIFF-14) `ProvenAbsent` never holds and no case-fold match is made, so only the first row
and the two Unknown rows apply: a file is Changed, Unchanged or Unknown (ChangeIndeterminate) only when the same exact
path was observed in both snapshots, and every other file is Unknown (`VisibilityUnverified`). Even a type change at
the same exact name is reported as two Unknown items there; the rule is kept a single mechanical condition (DIFF-P13).

### 12.4 Folder classification: two axes (DIFF-03, D-39)

Folders get an **existence class** and, when Present, a separate **content certainty**. Only the existence class takes
part in the headline counts and in the properties; content certainty is reported beside it and is **never counted as
Unknown** (REV-H05).

| O | N | Existence class |
|---|---|---|
| folder | folder (aligned) | **Present** |
| folder | unmatched, `ProvenAbsent_N` | **Missing**. All of its O descendants are Missing too (same anchor, same candidate set) |
| folder | unmatched, not proven | **Unknown** (with the reason); all O descendants Unknown with the same reason |
| unmatched, `ProvenAbsent_O` | folder | **Added** (all N descendants Added) |
| unmatched, not proven | folder | **Unknown** (side Older); its N descendants Unknown |
| folder | a file at the same exact name, and `ProvenAbsent_N` | **Missing** (`ReplacedByFile`); descendants Missing. Otherwise Unknown, as the third row |
| a file at the same exact name, and `ProvenAbsent_O` | folder | the folder is **Added** (`ReplacedFile`); all N descendants Added. Otherwise Unknown (side Older), as the fifth row |

Under `V = Unverified` a folder is Present only when the same exact path was observed in both snapshots; every other
folder, with its whole observed subtree, is Unknown (`VisibilityUnverified` unless a status reason applies first).

The content certainty of a Present folder uses `total_bytes`, `total_files` and `total_subfolders`:

| Certainty | Condition | Shown as |
|---|---|---|
| **Exact** | Both sides have `subtree_complete = 1` and neither side is a link | The delta. Content "changed" if non-zero, "unchanged" if zero |
| **NotComparable** | Either side has `subtree_complete = 0` (and neither is a link) | "Not comparable (incomplete)", with both values. Both are lower bounds, so their difference is not a bound in either direction |
| **NotObserved** | Either side has status `ReparsePointSkipped` | "Link (not followed)"; links are never entered, so there is no content on that side |

**Bytes.** File bytes are exact. The Missing (Added) byte total of a Missing (Added) folder is labelled "at least"
when its subtree was incomplete on its own side (REV-O07). Under `V = Unverified`, totals and Exact content deltas
are those of the entries visible to the scanning account in each scan, as v1's reports of that share are: a delta
can reflect a change in what the server showed, and the L-DIFF3 banner says so.

### 12.5 What "Changed" means for files (DIFF-04, DIFF-05, D-13, D-40)

"Changed" and "Unchanged" compare the metadata **the directory listing reported** (REV-O06); the help text says so.

| Field | Effect |
|---|---|
| `size_bytes` differs | **Changed** (flag `Size`) |
| `modified_utc` differs (`NULL ≠ value`; `NULL = NULL`) | **Changed** (flag `Modified`), except under DIFF-05 |
| `created_utc` differs | Stays **Unchanged**, informational flag `Created` |
| `attributes` differ | Stays **Unchanged**, informational flag `Attributes` (the Archive bit changes on every backup, for example) |
| Only the name's spelling differs (a case-fold match, DIFF-07) | Stays **Unchanged**, informational flag `NameSpelling` |
| `accessed_utc` differs | **Ignored entirely.** Last-access updates are lazy, often disabled, and coarse |

**DIFF-05 (clock shifts on FAT-family volumes, D-40).** FAT and FAT32 store local times, and exFAT written by devices
without its UTC-offset field behaves the same way; a time-zone or daylight-saving change shifts the reported UTC time
without any content change. **Rule:** on sources whose `fs_type` is `FAT`, `FAT32` or `exFAT`, a matched file whose size
is equal and whose `modified_utc` differs by a non-zero whole multiple of 15 minutes within ±14 hours is **Unknown
(ChangeIndeterminate, PossibleClockShift)**: counted separately, never Unchanged, never Changed (REV-M05). A size
difference makes it Changed as usual. exFAT stays in the rule until Q-03 proves otherwise on real media.

### 12.6 Reparse points (DIFF-06)

| Situation | Result |
|---|---|
| Link folder in both O and N | Existence **Present**, content **NotObserved**. Shown as "Link (not followed)". No descendants on either side |
| A normal folder in O is a link folder in N | The folder is Present (aligned), content NotObserved. Its O descendants have `a_N` = the link folder, which is not Listed, so they are **Unknown (Newer, BehindLink)**, **never Missing** |
| A link folder in O is a normal folder in N | Its N descendants are **Unknown (Older, BehindLink)** |
| File reparse points (for example symbolic-link files) | Ordinary file observations (their listed size), compared like any file |

### 12.7 Spelling-only renames (DIFF-07)

A case-fold match (§12.2 rule 2.2) treats two names that differ only in letter case or Unicode encoding as the same
item, **only** under two complete listings of a comparison whose listing visibility is `Complete`, only when both
names and their same-type siblings are in the verified domain, and only when each is the sole same-type member of its
key class on its side. Matched folders align their subtrees. In every other case exact-name identity applies, and the unmatched items are
classified by §12.2 (typically `NameVariantPresent`).

**Limitation L-DIFF1.** A case-fold match is a claim that both names occupy the same namespace position. In a folder
that distinguishes the two spellings (a case-sensitive NTFS folder, or NFC and NFD forms on NTFS), where one name was
deleted and the other created between the scans, StorageInventory reports a spelling change instead of Missing and
Added. This can only under-report, never produce Missing or Added, and the help text says so. Collecting per-directory
case sensitivity (Q-16, 1.2) would narrow it.

**Limitation L-DIFF2.** On comparisons whose listing visibility is `Complete` but whose matching assurance is
`Unverified` (UDF, a class before its Q-14 census passes, or two snapshots of different classes, DIFF-14), a deletion
is reported as Missing only when its folder, reached through exact matches, now holds nothing of that type; otherwise
it is Unknown (`NameMatchingUnverified`), and additions likewise. This under-reports by design (INV-16); the Compare
