banner says so ("This drive's rules for matching names aren't verified; items that may have been deleted are shown as
Unknown").

**Limitation L-DIFF3 (G0F-H01).** On comparisons whose listing visibility is `Unverified` (every network share,
whatever the server; every local filesystem that is not on the complete-listing list; and every class before its Q-23
evidence passes), StorageInventory **never reports Added or Missing**. A server can hide items from an account while
its listing still succeeds (access-based enumeration, DFS and NAS policies, a change of account or permissions between
scans), so an item absent from a listing may still exist, and an item that appears may have existed unseen. Such
transitions are Unknown (`VisibilityUnverified`), in both directions. Items seen at the same exact path in both scans
are still compared: Changed, Unchanged and their sizes are reported, and totals and folder deltas describe what the
scanning account could see. **Product consequence:** network history remains useful for positively observed files,
sizes and changes, but v1.1 makes no authoritative claim about creation or deletion on network sources; that would
need provider or filesystem evidence that a later release may add by reviewed change. The Compare banner says so
("This is a network location. The server can hide items from an account, so StorageInventory doesn't report items as
added or missing here: items that appeared or disappeared are shown as Unknown. Items seen in both scans are
compared."). Case-fold matching is also off there (§12.2 rule 2.2), so a case-only rename on a share shows as two
Unknown items.

### 12.8 Algorithm (DIFF-08, bounded memory)

1. **Folder alignment (O(folders) memory).** Load `(path_id, parent, name_id, discovery_index, status, subtree_complete, totals)`
   for both snapshots (at most about 470k rows each on the reference drive). Exact alignment is equality of `path_id`
   (paths are interned per source with exact names). Derive the comparison capability once (DIFF-14). When `V =
   Complete`, apply the case-fold step to unmatched child folders of aligned Listed pairs, decoding names and computing
   `K` and `Dom` only for folders that have unmatched siblings; record for each aligned pair whether it is exactly
   aligned. Verify folder counts against the sealed totals (DIFF-10).
2. **File merge (streaming).** Read `file_obs` for both snapshots ordered by `(folder_path_id, name_id)` (the primary
   key; measured 3.3 s for 2 × 2M on the proxy engine). Exactly aligned folders share `path_id` and merge in one pass;
   case-fold-aligned folders are read with keyed seeks. Within one folder pair with an unmatched file, when `V =
   Complete`: decode the names of each side that is Listed and compute `K` and `Dom` there (the proving side needs
   them when the other side is Partial or Unreadable, G0F-O04); apply the case-fold step when both are Listed; then
   the proof rule of DIFF-12. When `V = Unverified`, no name is decoded: every unmatched item is Unknown.
   Memory for this step is bounded by the largest single folder (PERF-12).
3. **Unmatched subtrees** stream with the verdict of their topmost unmatched folder (all its descendants share its
   anchor, candidate set and so its verdict).
4. **Output.** A summary (count and bytes per class and reason, flags, folder existence counts, content-certainty
   counts); top-N heaps (largest Added, largest Changed by absolute size delta, largest Missing, largest Unknown); an
   in-memory list of non-Unchanged items capped at PERF-06, beyond which the UI shows the summary and top-N lists; the
   folder-growth list (Exact deltas only). Streamed counts and bytes are checked against the sealed totals (DIFF-10).
   Comparison results are **not persisted** (D-14).

### 12.9 Truth table (DIFF-09)

"Complete" and "Incomplete" describe the whole snapshot; the per-location rules above do the work. Each row is a
required test case (TEST-D1). Unknown is written as Unknown (side, reason). Rows are for a comparison whose
capability is `V = Complete`, `A = Verified` (two local snapshots of one verified class, DIFF-14) unless they say
otherwise; "network" means a capture through a UNC path or mapped letter, so `V = Unverified`.

| # | Scenario | Older | Newer | Expected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | complete → complete, file unchanged | file | same file | Unchanged |
| T2 | size changed | file | size differs | Changed {Size} |
| T3 | only mtime changed | file | mtime differs | Changed {Modified} |
| T4 | attributes only | file | attributes differ | Unchanged {Attributes} |
| T5 | created time only | file | created differs | Unchanged {Created} |
| T6 | last-access only | file | accessed differs | Unchanged (no flag) |
| T7 | invalid timestamp on one side | mtime NULL | mtime value | Changed {Modified} |
| T8 | file deleted, parent fully listed in N, no name variant | file | parent Ok, no child | **Missing** |
| T9 | the file's folder is Unreadable in N | file | parent Unreadable | **Unknown (Newer, AncestorUnreadable)** |
| T10 | the folder listing stopped part-way in N | file | parent Partial, file absent | **Unknown (Newer, ListingIncomplete)** |
| T11 | unreadable **ancestor** in N (grandparent Unreadable, so the parent is absent) | file | anchor = grandparent, Unreadable | **Unknown (Newer, AncestorUnreadable)** |
| T12 | file in a folder that was Unreadable in O | (folder Unreadable) | file present | **Unknown (Older, AncestorUnreadable)** |
| T13 | new file under a folder Listed in O | parent Ok, no child | file | **Added** |
| T14 | different unreadable folders on each side | mixed | mixed | per location: T8 to T13 apply independently |
| T15 | folder disappears from a fully listed parent | folder + files | parent Ok, no folder, no variant | folder **Missing**, all descendants **Missing** |
| T16 | folder disappears, parent Partial in N | folder + files | parent Partial | folder and descendants **Unknown (Newer, ListingIncomplete)** |
| T17 | folder became a junction in N | folder + files | link folder | folder Present (content NotObserved); descendants **Unknown (Newer, BehindLink)** |
| T18 | junction became a real folder | link folder | folder + files | folder Present; descendants **Unknown (Older, BehindLink)** |
| T19 | link folder in both | link folder | link folder | Present, content NotObserved; no descendants on either side |
| T20 | file replaced by a folder with the same name | file | folder | file **Missing (ReplacedByFolder)**; folder **Added (ReplacedFile)** |
| T21 | folder replaced by a file | folder + files | file | folder and descendants **Missing (ReplacedByFile)**; file **Added (ReplacedFolder)** |
| T22 | case-only rename, both parents Listed, singleton key classes | `Report.txt` | `report.txt` | Unchanged {NameSpelling} (Changed if the size or mtime also differs) |
| T23 | a case variant appears beside the old name (case-sensitive folder in N) | `a.txt` | `a.txt`, `A.txt` | `a.txt` Unchanged; `A.txt` **Unknown (Older, NameVariantPresent)** (was "Added" in `c0c031f`: in O, `a.txt` may have been the same position) |
| T24 | source root unavailable at the newer scan | — | no snapshot (the scan Failed: SourceMissing) | Nothing to compare. History shows a ScanFailed attempt |
| T25 | whole volume offline | — | no new snapshot possible | Nothing to compare. Earlier snapshots remain comparable with each other |
| T26 | root Unreadable in N (the scan finished, Incomplete) | files | root Unreadable | **every** O item Unknown (Newer, AncestorUnreadable); no Missing |
| T27 | moved file (path A → path B), both folders Listed | file at A | file at B | A **Missing**, B **Added** (no "Moved" in 1.1, FID-01) |
| T28 | FAT32 source, same size, mtime +1 h | file | +3600 s | **Unknown (ChangeIndeterminate, PossibleClockShift)** (was Unchanged in `c0c031f`) |
| T29 | the same snapshot compared with itself, any completeness, links and unreadable folders included | S | S | all files Unchanged; all folders Present; zero Added, Missing and Unknown; content certainty reported as it is (never counted as Unknown) |
| T30 | folder content delta, both subtrees complete | 10 GB | 12 GB | folder Present, content **Exact**, +2 GB |
| T31 | folder content delta, one side incomplete | 10 GB | 9 GB (incomplete) | folder Present, content **NotComparable**, both values shown; not Unknown |
| T32 | hard link: two paths to the same file | 2 paths | 1 path removed, parent Listed | the removed path Missing; the other Unchanged (FID-03) |
| T33 | case-renamed folder, Unreadable in N (review CE-1) | `A\Photos\x.jpg`, all Ok | `A` Ok lists `photos`; `A\photos` Unreadable | `Photos` Present {NameSpelling}; `x.jpg` **Unknown (Newer, AncestorUnreadable)** |
| T34 | folder Unreadable in O, case-renamed in N (review CE-2) | `A` Ok lists `backup`, Unreadable | `A\Backup\y.txt` | `y.txt` **Unknown (Older, AncestorUnreadable)** |
| T35 | case-renamed folder is a junction in N (review CE-3) | `A\Backup\…` | `A\backup` junction | descendants **Unknown (Newer, BehindLink)** |
| T36 | folder replaced by a file whose name differs only in case | `A\b\x` | `A` Ok lists file `B` only | `A\b` and `x` **Missing (ReplacedByFile)**; `B` **Added (ReplacedFolder)** |
| T37 | self-comparison of a snapshot with a junction and an Unreadable folder | S | S | files all Unchanged; folders all Present; Unknown 0; content NotObserved and NotComparable reported separately |
| T38 | identity evidence differs between the window's opening and closing | — | — | not published (`NotEligible (IdentityChangedDuringScan)`); nothing to compare |
| T39 | `finished_utc` order is the reverse of capture order | — | — | ordered by capture sequence; clock anomaly flagged |
| T40 | case-sensitive folder loses one of two variants | `Makefile`, `makefile` | `makefile` (Listed) | `makefile` Unchanged; `Makefile` **Unknown (Newer, NameVariantPresent)** |
| T41 | case sensitivity changed and a wider-key variant appears ([E-6] targeted case) | case-sensitive `{s\child, S}` | case-insensitive `{S\child, ſ}` | `S` Present (exact); `s` and `s\child` **Unknown (Newer, NameVariantPresent)**; `ſ` **Unknown (Older, NameVariantPresent)**; `S\child` **Added** (O's `S` was listed complete and empty) |
| T42 | case-only rename under a Partial parent | `A` Ok lists `Report.txt` | `A` Partial lists `report.txt` | no case-fold match; `Report.txt` **Unknown (Newer, ListingIncomplete)**; `report.txt` **Unknown (Older, NameVariantPresent)** |
| T43 | NFC name in O, NFD name in N, singleton classes | `café.txt` (NFC) | `café.txt` (NFD) | Unchanged {NameSpelling} (L-DIFF1) |
| T44 | names with different unpaired surrogates, parent Listed | `a\uD800` | `a\uDC00` | different keys: `a\uD800` **Missing**, `a\uDC00` **Added**; each round-trips exactly (NAME-01) |
| T45 | FAT-family offsets | same size on FAT32, mtime +5:30; on exFAT −3:00; on FAT32 +0:07; on FAT32 size differs and +1 h | — | Unknown (ChangeIndeterminate); Unknown (ChangeIndeterminate); Changed {Modified}; Changed {Size, Modified} |
| T46 | a snapshot's rows disagree with its sealed totals | — | — | the comparison is refused ("stored data inconsistent"); no partial result |
| T47 | network source (basis Location, `V = Unverified`): a file deleted from a folder that still holds other files | file | file absent, parent Ok, other files present | **Unknown (Newer, VisibilityUnverified)** (Missing in `456b233`; Unknown (NameMatchingUnverified) in `121358c`), with the location banner and the L-DIFF3 banner |
| T48 | Missing folder whose older subtree was incomplete | folder (subtree incomplete) | parent Ok, no folder | folder Missing; Missing bytes labelled "at least" |
| T49 | combining-mark reordering under a per-unit fold that maps U+0345 to U+0399 (a model fold in the test; not the NTFS or exFAT default tables, [G0 R2]) | `A\<α U+0345 U+0301>\x.jpg`, all Ok | `A` Ok lists `<α U+0399 U+0301>` only; that folder Unreadable | the folders are case-fold matched (equal `K`, NAME-03); `x.jpg` **Unknown (Newer, AncestorUnreadable)**. Under the `456b233` key the folders did not match and `x.jpg` was Missing |
| T50 | network source (`V = Unverified`): the last file of a folder deleted; the folder reached through exact matches | `\\nas\m\A\x.txt` | `A` Ok, holds folders but no file | `x.txt` **Unknown (Newer, VisibilityUnverified)** (was Missing in `121358c`: the server may hide it, G0F-H01); `A` and its listed folders Present |
| T51 | network source (`V = Unverified`): a folder case-renamed and emptied of files | `A\Docs\x.txt` | `A` Ok lists `docs`, Ok, no file | no case-fold match on a network source: `Docs` and `x.txt` **Unknown (Newer, VisibilityUnverified)**; `docs` **Unknown (Older, VisibilityUnverified)** (`121358c` matched `Docs` with `docs`) |
| T52 | `Verified` comparison: a sibling's name uses a character not assigned in Unicode 16.0.0 | `A\x.txt` | `A` Ok lists `<name with an unassigned code point>` only | `x.txt` **Unknown (Newer, NameMatchingUnverified)**; the new name **Unknown (Older, NameMatchingUnverified)** |
| T53 | the same pair of snapshots compared at full capability (`Complete`, `Verified`) and at lower capability: the assurance lowered to `Unverified`, and the visibility lowered to `Unverified` | any | any | in each lowered comparison, every Missing or Added item is Missing or Added in the full one, and every item whose class differs is Unknown (DIFF-P11). Run on T41, T52, T59 and T60 and on generated pairs (TEST-D2). Under `121358c` both halves failed: T59 (Unknown → Missing) and T60 (Unknown → Unchanged) |
| T54 | network source: access-based enumeration hides a folder's contents in the newer scan (ACLs changed, or another account scans) | `\\nas\family\Alice\` lists `a.jpg`, `Trip\b.jpg` | `Alice` Ok, empty (entries hidden) | `a.jpg`, `Trip` and `Trip\b.jpg` **Unknown (Newer, VisibilityUnverified)**; never Missing (G0F-H01's failure case) |
| T55 | network source: items become visible that were hidden before | `Alice` Ok, empty | `Alice` lists `a.jpg` | `a.jpg` **Unknown (Older, VisibilityUnverified)**; never Added |
| T56 | network source: the same exact path in both scans | `A\x.txt` 10 B, `A\y.txt` | `A\x.txt` 12 B, `A\y.txt` unchanged | `x.txt` **Changed {Size}**; `y.txt` **Unchanged**; `A` Present |
| T57 | local source, `V = Complete`, `A = Unverified` (UDF, or NTFS before its Q-14 census): a folder's last file deleted, and a file deleted from a folder that still holds files | `A\x.txt`; `B\y.txt`, `B\z.txt` | `A` Ok, no file; `B` Ok lists `z.txt` | `x.txt` **Missing** (name-independent proof); `y.txt` **Unknown (Newer, NameMatchingUnverified)** |
| T58 | snapshots with different recorded filesystem classes (an NTFS source; a later UDF capture attached by the user's answer, ID-04) | `A\x.txt`, `A\w.txt` (NTFS) | `A` Ok lists `w.txt` only (UDF) | capability `V = Complete` (both classes on the complete-listing list), `A = Unverified` (different classes, DIFF-14): `x.txt` **Unknown (Newer, NameMatchingUnverified)**; under `121358c` (assurance from the source's NTFS volume) it was Missing by the key-based proof (G0F-M04) |
| T59 | a name outside the verified domain in an exactly aligned folder that is now empty of files | `A\<name with a code point unassigned in 16.0.0>` | `A` Ok, exactly aligned, no file | **Missing** (name-independent proof), under `Verified` and `Unverified` alike (was Unknown under `Verified` and Missing under `Unverified` in `121358c`, G0F-M05) |
| T60 | a case-only rename beside a sibling outside the verified domain | `A` lists `Report.txt`, `<x>.txt` (`<x>` unassigned in 16.0.0) | `A` lists `report.txt`, `<x>.txt` | no case-fold match (`Dom` fails, at every assurance level): `Report.txt` **Unknown (Newer, NameMatchingUnverified)**; `report.txt` **Unknown (Older, NameMatchingUnverified)**; `<x>.txt` Unchanged. `121358c` matched them as Unchanged {NameSpelling} when compared as `Unverified`, the second T53 contradiction |

### 12.10 Properties (DIFF-P1 to DIFF-P13)

Checked by property-based tests over generated trees (TEST-D2, TEST-D3, TEST-D5):

- **DIFF-P1 Partition:** every file observed in O or N gets exactly one file class; every folder gets exactly one
  existence class.
- **DIFF-P2 Soundness:** Missing ⇒ `ProvenAbsent_N` and Added ⇒ `ProvenAbsent_O`, exactly as defined in §12.2, checked by
  an independent checker over the engine's output.
- **DIFF-P3 Monotonicity:** if `N'` is `N` made less observable (a folder's status lowered from Ok to Partial or
  Unreadable with its unlisted children removed, or a folder turned into a link), then
  `Missing(O, N') ⊆ Missing(O, N)` and `Added(O, N') ⊆ Added(O, N)`, and every file whose class changes becomes Unknown.
  Symmetrically for `O`. Content certainty is outside this property (it may move from Exact to NotComparable).
- **DIFF-P4 Complete snapshots:** if both snapshots are Complete, every Unknown has reason `BehindLink` (under a folder
  that is a link on exactly one side), `NameVariantPresent`, `NameMatchingUnverified`, `VisibilityUnverified` or
  `ChangeIndeterminate`.
- **DIFF-P5 Self-comparison:** `Compare(S, S)` has all files Unchanged, all folders Present, and zero Added, Missing and
  Unknown, for every S.
- **DIFF-P6 Duality:** `Added(O→N)` equals `Missing(N→O)` as path sets; Changed and Unchanged are symmetric; Unknown
  swaps its side. Tested through an internal entry point that does not normalise the order.
- **DIFF-P7 Conservation:** `|O files| = Unchanged + Changed + Missing + Unknown_O` and
  `|N files| = Unchanged + Changed + Added + Unknown_N`, where `Unknown_O` counts Unknown items observed in O (an
  Unknown (ChangeIndeterminate) item is observed in both).
- **DIFF-P8 Location independence:** changing the mount point or drive letter between the snapshots changes no result.
- **DIFF-P9 Oracle agreement:** on generated trees of up to 2,000 items, the engine equals the reference oracle
  (§12.11) item by item.
- **DIFF-P10 World soundness:** on generated *worlds* (per-directory case sensitivity, changes to it, renames, links,
  partial and unreadable listings, multi-code-point names), the engine emits no Missing whose position is occupied or
  unknowable in the newer world, and no such Added in the older world (§12.11). Hidden entries count as occupying
  their position. For `Verified` worlds the world's fold is a random per-unit fold contained in `R` on the verified
  domain (and free to join characters outside it); for `Unverified` worlds it is an **arbitrary** equivalence on names
  (full case folding, normalisation, random merges), so soundness there must not depend on `K` at all; *mixed* worlds
  give the two snapshots different classes (DIFF-14); *network* worlds also hide a random subset of entries from
  listings that still report Ok, so soundness there must not depend on listings being complete.
- **DIFF-P11 Capability monotonicity (corrected after G0F-M05):** compare the same two snapshots at full capability
  and at a lower one, where the matching assurance is lowered from `Verified` to `Unverified`, or the listing
  visibility from `Complete` to `Unverified`. Then (a) every item that is Missing (Added) in the lowered comparison is
  Missing (Added) in the full one, as item sets, not only as counts; and (b) every item whose class differs between the
  two is Unknown in the lowered one. (a) holds because the name-independent proof is accepted at both assurance levels
  and nothing is proven under unverified visibility; (b) because the alignment does not depend on the assurance (the
  case-fold step needs `Dom` at every level) and dissolves into Unknown when visibility is lowered. Replacing `K` by a
  wider key satisfies (a) only: it can join a Missing and an Added item into one spelling-only match, which is a match,
  not a new proof (L-DIFF1).
- **DIFF-P12 Input verification:** a snapshot whose rows disagree with its sealed totals is never compared (T46).
- **DIFF-P13 Unverified visibility (G0F-H01):** when the comparison's listing visibility is `Unverified`, no file or
  folder is Added or Missing; every file classified Changed, Unchanged or Unknown (ChangeIndeterminate), and every folder
  classified Present, is an exact-path match observed in both snapshots; every other item is Unknown.

### 12.11 Reference oracle and world-model test (DIFF-13)

- **The oracle** is a brute-force implementation written independently of the engine: it shares only the
  observation types, the `K` function and the `Dom` predicate. It enumerates both snapshots as sets of `(exact path, type, status)`,
  computes the alignment of §12.2 literally with nested loops, and classifies every item from the definitions. TEST-D2
  runs it against the engine on at least 10,000 generated cases per property, with trees of up to 2,000 items that
  include: random Partial, Unreadable and link statuses on either side; case variants in case-sensitive folders; case
  renames; NFC and NFD forms; multi-code-point sequences (combining marks in both orders, the U+0345 family, Hangul
  syllables and jamo); unpaired surrogates; characters outside the verified domain; file and folder replacements;
  case-renamed folders that become links or unreadable; both assurance levels and both visibility levels; and snapshot
  pairs whose recorded kinds and filesystem names differ, so that the capability is derived as DIFF-14 states.
- **The world-model test** (TEST-D5) generalises [E-6]: it generates ground-truth worlds with per-directory case
  sensitivity and changes to it, observes them as a v1 scan would, runs the **production engine**, and counts unsound
  Missing and Added verdicts. Acceptance: **zero** in at least 100,000 worlds in the default configuration and 100,000
  in the biased configuration (names restricted to one key class plus a filler, up to 20 changes), each run for every
  world kind DIFF-P10 describes (`Verified`, `Unverified`, mixed and network). It also runs four **controls that must
  fail**: the engine with an exact-name key; the engine with the `456b233` key `R(NFD(n))` in `Verified` worlds whose
  fold maps U+0345 to U+0399; the engine with its capability taken from the source's volume instead of both snapshots
  in mixed worlds (G0F-M04); and the engine with `V` forced to `Complete` in network worlds (G0F-H01). Each must produce
  unsound verdicts, proving the test can detect each defect ([E-11] shows the last two on the model).
- **`K` itself** (TEST-D6) is table-driven and tested three ways: the per-unit condition over every code unit for each
  fold table Q-14 checks; the property "names equal under a random per-unit fold contained in `R` have equal `K`"
  ([E-9] 3); and the Q-14 census data, single code units and multi-code-point sequences, where every set of names the
  filesystem treated as one must lie within one `K` class.

---

## 13. History, analytics and search

Everything in this section is **metadata arithmetic over published snapshots**. There is no content analysis (§4.1).
"Older" and "newer" always mean capture order (SCH-11).

### 13.1 Totals over time (HIST-01, REQ-09, SREQ-11)

- **Series:** bytes, files and folders per published snapshot of one source, from the snapshot's sealed totals, in
  capture order.
- **Table (MUST, REQ-09):** one row per snapshot, with attempts inline as rows with no values.
- **Chart (SHOULD, SREQ-11):** drawn from the same data; the chart never joins complete and incomplete points into one
  line without marking the difference.
- **Incomplete snapshots** are labelled **"at least"** (lower bounds) in both.
- **Capacity and free space** at each scan are a separate column/series. They are the volume's, not the source's (for a
  subfolder source that matters).
- **Accessibility:** every chart has its equivalent table (UI-09).

### 13.2 Growth (HIST-02, REQ-10, SREQ-02)

- **Folder growth** between two snapshots comes from the §12.4 content axis of Present folders.
  - Top-N folders by absolute **Exact** delta, split into grew and shrank.
  - A separate count of folders whose content is **Not comparable (incomplete)** or **Not observed (link)**, with a
    way to list them.
  - **Never** rank a folder on a non-Exact delta.
- **"Fastest-growing over a period"** is the Exact delta between the first and last published snapshots in the period.
  A per-day figure may be shown as delta ÷ elapsed time, labelled "average between two scans", not a measured rate.
- **File-count growth** works the same way, using `total_files`.
- **Extension and category growth** (SREQ-02) uses `snapshot_extension_total`, with categories from v1's
  `FileTypeCatalog`. An incomplete snapshot's per-extension totals are lower bounds and are marked as such.
- **Bytes added between scans** use the comparison summary (Added and Changed bytes; "at least" where §12.4 says so).
- **Largest additions and changes** use the comparison's top-N lists (REQ-07).
- **Network and other visibility-unverified sources** (L-DIFF3) have no Added or Missing items: the summary and the
  Added and Missing lists say "Not reported for this location" with the banner's reason, never an empty "0"; Changed
  and Unknown are shown as usual.

### 13.3 Last seen (HIST-03, REQ-16)

- **MUST:** in a comparison, every **Missing** item shows "last seen: <older snapshot date>". That is exact: the older
  snapshot observed it.
- "Last seen anywhere in history" (SREQ-10) is 1.1.x. Its rule is fixed now: it searches older published snapshots of
  the source, newest first, stops at the first observation, and when it passes a snapshot where the path could not be
  proven absent it says "possibly present later (not observable on <date>)" rather than claiming a gap.

### 13.4 Offline browsing (HIST-04, REQ-08, D-48)

`LibraryExplorer` serves the v1 results tabs from a published snapshot, with the same rows in the same order as v1's
`ReportExplorer` over the CSV reports of the same run. The stored discovery ordinals reproduce v1's tie order exactly
(REV-M09):

| Tab | Query | v1 rule it reproduces |
|---|---|---|
| Overview (largest top-level folders) | `folder_obs` at depth 1, `ORDER BY total_bytes DESC, discovery_index ASC LIMIT 100` | `ReportWriters.FolderOrder` then depth 1 |
| Largest folders | `ORDER BY total_bytes DESC, discovery_index ASC LIMIT 2000` (`ExplorationViewModel.FolderLimit`) | Folders report order |
| Largest files | `ORDER BY size_bytes DESC, seq ASC LIMIT 1000`, a bounded top-N sort (A-24); measured 0.43 s for 2M files without an extra index | Sorted Files report order; the unsorted-report heap also breaks ties by sequence |
| File types | `snapshot_extension_total` plus `FileTypeCatalog` | Files report aggregation |
| Errors | `scan_error` in `seq` order, with counts by type | ScanErrors report order |

**Parity test (TEST-H2):** for every fixture scan with Save to History on, in both sorted and unsorted Files mode,
`ReportExplorer` over the CSVs and `LibraryExplorer` over the snapshot return the same rows in the same order, including
fixtures with many equal-size files and empty folders. The only allowed differences are documented presentation
differences (local-time rendering; the CSV's U+FFFD rendering of an unpaired surrogate, which the Library keeps exact).

**When the source is offline,** browsing works from the Library alone. v1.1 has one action that needs the live
filesystem, opening the run's report files if they still exist, through the v1 allow-list; it is unavailable with its
reason otherwise.

### 13.5 Search (SRCH-01, SRCH-02, SREQ-01): a filter model, not a language

**SRCH-01 (semantics):**

| Filter | Semantics |
|---|---|
| Source; snapshot (default: latest published) | Exactly one snapshot per search in v1.1. Cross-history search is deferred |
| Name contains | Case-insensitive (`OrdinalIgnoreCase`) substring match on the decoded exact name (NAME-06) |
| Path contains | Case-insensitive substring match on the rebuilt folder path |
| Extension | Exact match on `ExtensionOf(name)`, case-insensitive |
| Size range | Inclusive, in bytes. The UI accepts KB, MB and GB and converts with 1024 units, as v1 does |
| Modified between | UTC bounds converted from local dates. Items with `NULL` modified times are excluded, with the count shown |
| Status relative to a reference snapshot | **Deferred** (it needs a stored comparison) |

**SRCH-02 (execution):**
1. Match names in .NET over the source's name dictionary (SCH-04; about 250k distinct names for 2M files; Unicode-correct).
2. Match folder paths in .NET over the loaded folder table.
3. Pass the matching IDs as one bound parameter read with `json_each(?)` (SQLite's built-in JSON table function), so the
   read-only connection needs no temporary table.
4. Filter `file_obs` for the snapshot by those IDs and the numeric bounds.

Results are paged, and each page is bounded (PERF-04). There is **no free-text query language and no SQL typed by
users** (SEC-04).

### 13.6 Age cohorts (SREQ-03)

- **Cohorts:** "Not modified in 1 / 2 / 5 / 10 years, according to the file's modified date", counting files and
  bytes.
- **Mandatory wording:** "Modified dates are set by the programs that write files. Copying, restoring or syncing can
  keep old dates or set new ones, so a file's modified date does not prove when it was last used."
- **Created and last-access dates are never used for cohorts.**

### 13.7 When a volume is offline (HIST-05)

- All history, comparison, browsing and search features work from the Library alone.
- The source card shows **Offline** (after an on-demand check) or **Not checked**, and "last seen <date> as <mount
  point>".
- **Scan is unavailable**, with the reason.
- **Online state is never cached as truth:** it is re-checked on demand (ID-07).

---

## 14. WPF information architecture

### 14.1 Shell and navigation (UI-01)

v1's single window with three stages becomes a **shell window with a navigation list** (keyboard-accessible, with
automation names) and a content region. One view per page, one view model per page, and a small navigation service.
There are no UI packages (A-11).

| Page | Purpose | Main content | Empty state |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Home** | Where am I? | Library status and path (LIB-03); the most recent snapshot per source; attempts needing attention (interrupted captures, attempts of sources never created, stale-spool notes); a "Scan a drive or folder" button | "No history yet. Scan a drive or folder and save it to start a history." |
| **Sources** | Every drive and folder ever saved | A card per source: name, filesystem, identity confidence and "How is this drive recognised?", **Online / Offline / Not checked**, last seen, latest totals and completeness, number of snapshots, Library space used; a "Check drives" action | "No sources yet." |
| **Scan** | The v1 flow, unchanged | Setup (with preflight), Progress, Results, reusing v1's views. The preflight gains one line: "Save to History: <source> in <Library path>", or the reason it can't. Progress gains the "Saving to History" phase, and the Results view shows it until saving ends (UI-10, UI-13) | — |
| **History** | One source over time | The timeline table (REQ-09) and chart (SREQ-11); attempts inline; per-snapshot actions: Browse, Compare with…, Delete (with confirmation; available only while the interlock is Idle, UI-13) | "No saved scans for this source." |
| **Compare** | What changed? | Selectors (§14.4); a summary by class and reason; tabs for Added, Changed, Missing, Unknown and folder growth; top-N lists; flags | "Pick a source with at least two saved scans." |
| **Browse** | A snapshot, offline or online | The v1 results tabs, served from the Library (§13.4) | — |
| **Search** (if SREQ-01 ships) | Find items in one snapshot | The filter form (§13.5) and paged results | "Enter at least one filter." |
| **Settings & About** | Transparency | Library path, size and state; version; schema version; SQLite version and source id; licences; privacy wording (deleted snapshots stay in free space until reused; how to copy the Library while the app is closed) | — |

### 14.2 App-lifetime state and services (UI-12, REV-M10)

Long-lived state is owned by services created once per app session, not by page view models:

| Service | Owns | Lifetime and rules |
|---|---|---|
| `ScanSession` | The running scan and capture: options, cancellation (scan and save tokens, CAN-02), progress, the `StorageScanResult` (delivered as soon as the scan returns), the save outcome, the class C boundary (CAT-04). It starts a scan only with an `ObservationLease` or a Prepare lease from the interlock, every scan (Save on or off) goes through `InventoryRun`, and it is the App's only caller of it (A-25); a scan request before the start-up open has ended, or in Faulted, is refused (OBS-14, OBS-13) | App lifetime. **C2 moves the scan out of `MainViewModel` into `ScanSession`**, so C8 adds pages without rework |
| `LibrarySession` | The writer lock, connections (the writer only inside mutation leases), the in-process gate, the **Library activity interlock** (OBS-07 to OBS-15) with its state and reason, and the Library **state as an observable value** (§6.8) | App lifetime, created before the first page, present even when no Library exists; its interlock is constructed holding the start-up Open lease (OBS-14). Every page reacts to state changes (for example Available → Damaged mid-session) and to interlock changes (UI-13) |
| `ICatalogue` | Published snapshots and attempts; the only constructor of `PublishedSnapshotId` | Stateless over `LibrarySession` |
| `ICompareService` | A running comparison and its result (up to PERF-06's cap) | Owned by the Compare page; cancelled and released when the user navigates away |
| `ISourceStatus` | On-demand online checks (ID-07) | Stateless; never probes without a user action |

Pages depend on these interfaces, so their view models are unit-tested with fakes; a test factory creates
`PublishedSnapshotId` values for page tests.

### 14.3 Scanning, saving and Library operations: phases and commands (UI-02, UI-10, UI-13)

- **UI-02:** a running scan keeps going when the user moves to another page. The navigation list shows "Scanning…"
  or "Saving to History…" while either runs.
- **UI-10 (saving phase):** after the scan returns, the results are shown at once (the reports are valid). Until the
  capture's Save lease ends, the Results view shows a **"Saving to History"** status band with the saving progress and
  its own **Stop saving** button (CAN-02), and **New scan is unavailable** with the words "Available when saving to
  History has finished (or stop saving)". **Stop saving** turns the band at once into **"Stopping…"** and disables the
  button (a second request changes nothing); New scan stays unavailable until the Save lease has ended. A representative
  save stops within 2 s, a stress or worst-case save within 5 s (CAN-03, PERF-16); if stopping still runs after 2 s the
  band adds "undoing the partial save" with an indeterminate progress indicator. After Stop the band reports the save's
  outcome, not the moment of the click: if the save published anyway, because Stop came after its last token check
  (IMP-11's final check, CAN-01e), it reports "Saved to History (it had finished before Stop took effect)"; if it rolled
  back, it never shows "Saved to History". The Results view never looks idle while saving runs: the band stays until
  the outcome is shown ("Saved to History", or the reason it was not saved). History pages that need the Library
  during saving show "Saving to History…" and refresh when it ends.
- **Closing the window** during a scan uses v1's confirm-and-wait flow (`MainWindow.OnClosing`). During saving it asks:
  "Saving to History. **Stop saving and close** / **Keep saving**." Stopping rolls back (O4, O27). While "Stopping…"
  runs, closing asks nothing more and waits for the stop to finish (at most PERF-16's budget for the save's class),
  showing "Stopping…"; ending the process instead would only defer the rollback to the next start (O21). During a deletion
  it asks "Deleting a snapshot. **Stop and close** / **Keep deleting**" (stopping rolls T-DELETE back). During an
  Open (the start-up open included) or a set-aside it waits, showing the operation's name (OBS-11). When Faulted it
  asks nothing and the process exits at once; an open write transaction is left to SQLite's rollback at the next
  start (OBS-13, O21).
- **UI-13 (phases and command availability).** The App's workflow phase is derived from observable values only: the
  interlock state (OBS-07), whether `ScanSession` has a scan or its v1 workbook step running, and, during a save, whether
  its save token has been cancelled (UI-10's "Stopping…"). Every command's
  availability, and the reason shown when it is unavailable, comes from this table (TEST-U2 checks each cell):

| Phase (shown as) | Interlock | Steps | Start or New scan | Stop scan | Stop saving | Delete snapshot | Set aside, Create new Library, Retry open | Browse, Compare, Search, History | Open Library folder |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Opening the Library** (also the **start-up** phase: the App starts here, OBS-14) | Mutating(Open) | LIB-08 | No: "Opening the Library…" | — | — | No | No | Wait ("Opening the Library…") | No |
| **Ready** (Idle) | Idle | — | **Yes** (Save to History per §6.8 and ID-13) | — | — | Yes | Yes, per Library state | Yes | Yes |
| **Starting scan** | Mutating(Prepare r) | P1 | No: "A scan is starting" | Yes (the scan returns Cancelled at P2, O3) | — | No | No | Yes (reads wait briefly for T0) | No |
| **Scanning** (then "Writing reports") | Observing(r) | P2 to P4 | No: "A scan is running" | Yes | — | No: "Unavailable while scanning" | No: "Unavailable while scanning" | Yes (reads only) | No (OBS-05) |
| **Results, saving to History** | Mutating(Save r) | P5 to P7 | **No**: "Available when saving to History has finished (or stop saving)" | — | **Yes** | No | No | Library pages wait ("Saving to History…"); the Results tabs (CSV reports) work | Yes |
| **Stopping…** (Stop saving pressed; until the Save lease ends) | Mutating(Save r), save token cancelled | P5 to P7 | **No**: "Available when saving to History has stopped" | — | No: "Stopping…" (a second request changes nothing) | No | No | Library pages wait ("Saving to History…"); the Results tabs (CSV reports) work | Yes |
| **Results** (saved or not; Idle) | Idle | after P7, or a Save-off scan | **Yes** | — | — | Yes | Yes, per Library state | Yes | Yes |
| **Deleting a snapshot** | Mutating(Delete) | T-DELETE | No: "Deleting a snapshot…" | — | — | No (Cancel deletion: Yes) | No | Library pages wait | Yes |
| **Creating or setting aside the Library** | Mutating(Create) or Mutating(SetAside) | LIB-07, LIB-13 | No: "Setting up the Library…" | — | — | No | No | No | No |
| **Restart required** (terminal) | Faulted | — | No: "Restart StorageInventory" | — | — | No | No | No | No |

  A command that is enabled can still be refused if the state changes between the click and the request; the
  refusal carries the same words and nothing starts (OBS-11, O28, O29). The v1 workbook step, which runs after a scan
  in the report folder, keeps v1's own availability rule and is neither a Library mutation nor an observation, except
  in Faulted, where it is unavailable too: there, closing the window is the only command, and the message names the
  report folder as plain text when the reports are valid (OBS-13). The phase is never Ready before the start-up open
  has ended, and never Ready again after Faulted.

### 14.4 Compare selectors (UI-03)

- The source picker lists only sources with at least two published snapshots.
- The **Older** and **Newer** pickers list only that source's **published** snapshots in capture order, never attempts.
  The default is the latest two, and there is a **Swap** button.
- Each option shows its date, completeness (in words and with an icon, never colour alone), total size and identity
  confidence and basis.
- If either snapshot is Incomplete, a banner explains: "Some locations could not be read in one of these scans. Items
  there are shown as **Unknown**, never as Missing."
- Below Strong confidence, or with basis Location or UserAsserted, the ID-09 banner is shown.
- The comparison's capability (DIFF-14) chooses one more banner: listing visibility `Unverified` (every network
  source) shows the L-DIFF3 banner, and the Added and Missing tabs say "Not reported for this location"; visibility
  `Complete` with matching `Unverified` shows the L-DIFF2 banner. When the two snapshots recorded different filesystem
  classes, the banner says so ("These two scans came from different kinds of drive").
- A clock anomaly (§12.1) is shown with both dates.

### 14.5 Presentation rules (UI-04 to UI-09, UI-11)

- **UI-04:** each class has a word, an icon and a colour; the Missing help says "not found when its folder was
  listed during the newer scan" (a scan is not instantaneous). Unknown always shows its reason in plain words:
  - AncestorUnreadable: "Couldn't read this folder in the newer (older) scan";
  - ListingIncomplete: "The folder's listing stopped part-way";
  - BehindLink: "Behind a link (not followed)";
  - NameVariantPresent: "Another item here has the same name apart from letter case or accents, so StorageInventory
    can't tell whether this is the same item";
  - NameMatchingUnverified: "This drive's rules for matching names (letter case, accents) aren't verified, so
    StorageInventory can't prove this item is gone" (or "is new");
  - VisibilityUnverified: "This location can hide items from your account (for example a network share), so
    StorageInventory can't prove this item is gone" (or "is new");
  - ChangeIndeterminate: "Same size, time moved by whole hours on a FAT/exFAT drive: possibly a clock change".
- **UI-05:** incomplete snapshots are always labelled Incomplete, and their totals say "at least".
- **UI-06:** offline sources show when and where they were last seen; there is never a spinner for "checking" network
  drives.
- **UI-07:** the Library's own folder is labelled "StorageInventory Library (app data)" wherever it appears inside a
  scanned source, with help text about the one-snapshot lag and about listing sizes of open files (§6.5 rule 4).
- **UI-08:** every Library state (§6.8) and every `CaptureFailureKind` (§9.4) has a fixed message with a "What you can
  do" line; a test asserts that the table covers every value (TEST-U3).
- **UI-09 (accessibility):** everything v1 has is carried forward (word + icon + colour; access keys, visible labels and
  automation names; polite live regions for progress; `ThemeMode="System"` light and dark; PerMonitorV2 DPI). New for
  v1.1: every chart has a data table; diff tabs and lists are virtualised and keyboard navigable.
- **UI-11:** a Library state banner appears on every page whenever the state is not Available, and actions that need
  the Library are disabled with the state's reason.

### 14.6 Behaviour-preserving decomposition first (C2)

`MainWindow.xaml` (388 lines) is split into `SetupView`, `ScanProgressView` and `ResultsView` user controls, bound to
the **existing** view models and hosted inside the new shell's Scan page; the scan moves into `ScanSession` (UI-12).
- **Acceptance:** every v1 UI test passes unchanged; rendered screenshots of the three stages (light and dark, on the
  synthetic fixture) differ from v1 **only in the shell chrome** (the navigation list and frame), confirmed by an
  image diff masked to the content region; the smoke test passes.
- **No new pages in C2.**

---

## 15. Performance and capacity plan

Every figure below is labelled **measured** (with where and how) or **projected** (with its basis). No projected figure
may appear in user documentation until the named gate has measured it (REV-O08). Units in the text of the C4 design
review and its repair: MiB = 2^20 bytes, MB = 10^6 bytes.

### 15.1 Measured

**(a) The `c0c031f` probe (Windows).** Machine: 12th Gen Intel Core i7-1270P, 32 GB, internal SSD, Windows 11 26200 (the
machine of `docs/benchmarks/native.md`). A disposable harness using **Windows' `winsqlite3.dll` 3.51.1 as a proxy
engine**; synthetic trees at about 4.3 files per folder (the reference drive's ratio: 2,009,918 files in 467,481
folders); the §9 schema in a TEXT-name variant; WAL, `synchronous=NORMAL` for 50,000-row batches and `FULL` for
publish; no foreign keys on observation tables; the snapshot written **alone**, not during a scan; **insertion order
not recorded**; one and two snapshots only.

| Files per snapshot | Write snapshot 1 | Write snapshot 2 | DB size after 1 / after 2 | Bytes per file observation (marginal) | Ordered-merge compare (2 snapshots) | Search: SQL LIKE on all rows / names first | Top-1000 by size | `sum(size)` |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 250,000 | 1.38 s (181k rows/s) | 0.98 s | 17.4 / 32.1 MB | 61 B | 0.19 s | 0.19 s / — | 0.04 s | 0.04 s |
| 1,000,000 | 5.42 s (185k rows/s) | 4.18 s | 72.9 / 133.5 MB | 63 B | 1.09 s | 1.42 s / — | 0.31 s | 0.18 s |
| 2,000,000 | 10.33 s (194k rows/s) | 12.82 s | 147.0 / 268.9 MB | 64 B | 3.33 s | 4.92 s / **0.56 s** | 0.43 s | 0.65 s |

Maintenance at 2M files (same probe): `quick_check` 2.51 s; `integrity_check` 4.43 s; deleting one snapshot in one
transaction 3.11 s; `VACUUM` afterwards 4.82 s (268.9 → 134.2 MB). The merge reproduced the injected ground truth
exactly at every size (at 2M: 20,157 changed, 10,063 removed, 10,000 added).

**(b) The repair probe (Linux, relative only) [E-3].** Python `sqlite3` (SQLite 3.45.1), a `file_obs`-shaped table
only, 1M rows per snapshot, each import in **one** transaction:

| Mode | Order | Import 1 / import 2 | Peak side file during import | Delete one snapshot (peak side file) | Re-import into freed pages (peak side file) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| rollback `TRUNCATE` | key | 3.48 s / 3.22 s | journal 0.0 MB | 0.41 s (journal 31.5 MB) | 2.61 s (journal 0.1 MB) |
| rollback `TRUNCATE` | shuffled | 8.02 s / 8.24 s | journal 0.0 MB | 0.74 s (journal 30.7 MB) | 7.28 s (journal 0.1 MB) |
| WAL | key | 3.25 s / 3.28 s | WAL 31.6 / 32.7 MB | 0.38 s (WAL 31.6 MB) | 3.18 s (WAL 32.7 MB) |
| WAL | shuffled | 9.39 s / 8.67 s | WAL 30.8 / 31.6 MB | 0.39 s (WAL 30.8 MB) | 9.06 s (WAL 31.7 MB) |

Readings: key order is 2.3 to 2.9 times faster than shuffled order in both modes; rollback mode needs almost no journal
for an appending import; the two modes are equally fast. Absolute numbers do not transfer to Windows.

**Not measured anywhere yet:** the shipped engine (3.53.3) on Windows; UTF-16 `BLOB` names; the `seq` and
`discovery_index` columns; traversal-order capture through the spool; capture concurrent with a real scan; more than two
snapshots; comparison with folder alignment and case-fold checks; memory.

### 15.2 Capacity projection (full snapshots, D-07)

**Projected** linearly from the measured marginal cost (61 to 64 B per file observation) plus about 3 to 4 B for `seq`
(projected); not measured beyond two snapshots.

| Source size | First snapshot | Each further snapshot | 10 snapshots | 50 snapshots | 100 snapshots |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 250k files | ~17 MB | ~15 MB | ~0.15 GB | ~0.8 GB | ~1.5 GB |
| 2M files (the reference system drive) | ~150 MB | ~135 MB | ~1.4 GB | ~6.8 GB | ~13.5 GB |

- **Index overhead.** `file_obs` and `folder_obs` are clustered `WITHOUT ROWID` tables with no secondary index, so
  index overhead grows with distinct names and paths (the `name` table stores each distinct name of a source twice: row
  and `UNIQUE` index; a name seen by several sources is stored once for each, SCH-04), not with the number of
  snapshots.
- **Fill and fragmentation.** `snapshot_id` leads each key, so a new snapshot appends at the right edge; key-ordered
  import (IMP-03) keeps its pages dense. After deletions, later imports reuse freed pages [E-3], scattering a
  snapshot's pages: harmless on SSD, measured on HDD by CAP-03's churn test.
- **Deletion cost.** Measured 3.11 s at 2M on the proxy; in rollback mode deletion journals about the deleted
  snapshot's size [E-3], so it needs that much free space transiently. The file does not shrink until Compact (1.1.x).
- **Transient disk needs.** Import: the snapshot's own growth plus a rollback journal of one 4,104-byte record per
  existing page of the source's dictionary range that receives a new name, about one per new name until the range is
  saturated, plus 30 to 50 fixed pages (measured, C4 design review; PERF-15): about 0.24 MiB for a new source's first
  snapshot, about 29 MiB for a representative 2M-file re-scan (C4 design repair), up to the size of the source's
  dictionary range for a snapshot of mostly new names; IMP-11 checks the space before and during the import, including
  the main-file growth that the page cache writes at `COMMIT`. Deletion: a journal of about one snapshot. Capture: the spool in the report folder,
  about 80 B per file (projected).
- **Backup implications.** StorageInventory does not back up the Library in 1.1.0 (SREQ-05 is 1.1.x). History is lost
  if the drive holding `%LOCALAPPDATA%` fails. A user copy of the closed Library's `library.sqlite3` is complete (§6.3)
  and is the size of the Library.
- **Scale claims.** Every figure in this table is a projection. Before release, C10 measures a **separate series**
  of Libraries with 1, 10 and 50 snapshots of a **1M-file** source (TEST-P3); CAP-05 compares that series with this
  projection scaled to 1M files. The table's own rows (250k and 2M files) and its 100-snapshot column stay projections
  and are never documented as characteristics (G0-O04).

### 15.3 Budgets (each confirmed or revised with measured justification at its gate, by §15.4's method)

A budget is a **performance acceptance target**. Where a gate also has an **architectural stop threshold**, it is a separate,
weaker number in its own column: missing the target and crossing the stop threshold are two different outcomes (§15.4,
"Outcomes"), and a target that is missed is never reported as met because its stop threshold was not crossed.

| ID | Budget (acceptance target) | Basis | Stop threshold (§15.4) | Confirmed in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PERF-01 | **T-IMPORT throughput ≥ 100,000 file rows/s**: file rows ÷ the time from `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` to the return of `COMMIT` (in-transaction verification included), the **median of five valid runs** in each **representative** cell of §15.4 | Derived: PERF-02b leaves 2.5 to 3.2 s for pass V and the import of 250k files, so the import needs 80k to 100k rows/s (C4 design review §12). The representative cells are fixed by §15.4's input parameters (D-53): first saves of 1M and 2M files at 25% distinct names on the system-drive name model, and 1% re-scans of such a source. Measured on the reference machine **under load, informational only** (C4 design repair, whole-machine other load 34% to 68%): 2M first save 66,270 to 107,116 rows/s over five runs (median of the three timing runs 84,795), 2M re-scan 66,841 to 93,242 rows/s over five runs (median 88,225); every run invalid by §15.4's load rule. **NOT MEASURED** until a valid quiet session | a representative cell's median **below 50,000** rows/s | C4 (engine), C5 (end to end) |
| PERF-02a | Scan with Save on, warm-cache 250k-file tree, sorted mode: time until the reports are ready **≤ 1.10×** v1 (v1: 6.36 s, `docs/benchmarks/native.md`) | projected: the only addition during the scan is sequential spool appends | — | C5 |
| PERF-02b | Same tree: time until "Saved to History" **≤ 1.5×** v1 | projected: v1 time plus about 1.5 s of import at the measured rate | — | C5 |
| PERF-03 | Library growth **≤ 90 B per file observation**, folder rows, interning and `seq` included | measured 61–64 B without `seq`; projected +3–4 B | — | C5 |
| PERF-04 | Search over the latest snapshot of a 2M-file source: **≤ 2 s** to the first page | measured 0.56 s (proxy) | — | C9 |
| PERF-05 | Compare two 2M-file snapshots: **≤ 10 s** to the summary, including folder alignment, case-fold checks and input verification | measured merge 3.3 s (proxy) | — | C7 (synthetic), C10 (Library) |
| PERF-06 | The comparison's in-memory list of non-Unchanged items holds at most **1,000,000 entries and ≤ 200 MB**; beyond that, summary and top-N only (§12.8) | design limit | — | C7 |
| PERF-07 | History timeline of a source with 50 snapshots: **≤ 0.5 s** | projected | — | C10 (50 × 1M Library) |
| PERF-08 | Opening the Library at start-up with 50 snapshots of 1M files: **≤ 1 s**, with no integrity check at open. This is a clean open; an open that rolls a hot journal back after a crash is budgeted by PERF-15 (c) | projected | — | C10 |
| PERF-10 | Deleting a 2M-file snapshot: **≤ 10 s** | measured 3.1 s (proxy, WAL) | — | C6, C10 |
| PERF-11a | Peak working set during a scan with capture: **≤ v1 peak + 64 MB** at 2M files (spool buffer and run index) | projected | — | C5 |
| PERF-11b | Peak working set during the import: **≤ v1 peak + 300 MB** at 2M files (IMP-08) | projected | — | C5 |
| PERF-12 | Comparison memory: folder alignment **≤ 300 MB** at 2 × 470k folders, plus **≤ 120 B per file** of the largest single folder pair | projected | — | C7, C10 |
| PERF-13 | Spool size **≤ 120 B per file** observation | projected about 80 B | — | C5 |
| PERF-14 | **Saving phase (P5 to P7) at 2M files, end to end: ≤ 60 s** (the C5 target), in every representative and stress cell of §15.4 and on TEST-P2's real drive. Three numbers, never one (D-53): **(1) the C5 end-to-end target**, 60 s, covering V, the spool-reading T-IMPORT, IMP-11's checks and the transitions, measured end to end in C5 on spools written from §15.4's generator; **(2) the C4 engine target**, T-IMPORT alone **≤ 45 s** at 2M in every representative and stress cell: 60 s less 15 s reserved for C5's part, which is projected at 3 to 8 s (two sequential reads of a 160 to 240 MB spool and its decoding inside T-IMPORT) with a factor of about two for what is not measured yet (anti-malware scanning the spool, a cold spool cache); **(3) the C4 architecture stop threshold** (next column). Worst-case cells have no target: they are bounded by the stop threshold, and C5 reports their end-to-end time for information | Projected 10–15 s (IMP-09) plus two sequential reads of the spool (V; the error pass of IMP-03). Measured T-IMPORT at 2M, under load and informational only: representative cells 18.6 to 30.1 s (the slowest an analysed run with its journal copy), the stress first save (60% distinct, data model) 43.9 s (C4 design repair); the design review's worst-case cells 45 to 53 s. The 45 s are not chosen to fit these runs: they follow from the 60 s requirement and C5's reserved share | T-IMPORT **above 67.5 s** (1.5 × the engine target; the prepared constant-factor alternatives recover about 1.5×) in a representative or stress cell, or **above 90 s** (1.5 × the whole saving budget) in a worst-case cell | C4 (engine target and stop, TEST-P1), C5 (end to end, TEST-P2; if C5's own part exceeds 15 s, the split is revised by reviewed change before C5 is accepted) |
| PERF-15 | **The transient cost of T-IMPORT.** Replaces "peak journal ≤ 5% of the snapshot's size", which measured an append-only special case and is the wrong denominator: the journal grows with the dictionary pages that new names land in, not with the snapshot (C4-B01, D-52). **(a) Key-range confinement of the journal** (the property D-52 rests on: importing one source never causes rollback-journal churn across other sources' dictionary key ranges), checked against the database as it was at `BEGIN` (copied after T0) by §15.4's key-range attribution, which is valid only when the checker validates its evidence completely (INVALID evidence is never a pass; C4DRR-M02): in each source-prefixed dictionary index (`name (source_id, utf16)`, `folder_path_child`, `folder_path_root`), at every B-tree level, every journalled page lies within **W = 2 positions** of the run of pages whose key interval (from the dividers above them) intersects the target source's keys (for a new source, whose keys sort past every existing key, the rightmost page of the level), and at most **2 × (W + 1) = 6** journalled pages of a level hold an entry of another source (the cap follows from W and key order, not from an independent result: keys sort by `source_id` first, so only the two end pages of a level's IN run and the at most 2W ADJACENT pages can hold another source's keys; in the per-source layout it is implied by W, and it decides only the negative control, where IN spans the whole index); in each append-shaped tree (`file_obs`, `folder_obs`, `scan_error`, `snapshot_extension_total`, and the `name` and `folder_path` tables), only the rightmost page of each level and at most W pages to its left; otherwise only the catalogue trees' pages (`library_info`, `volume`, `source`, `scan_attempt`, `snapshot` and their indexes), page 1 and free-list pages. **Status of W:** for one rebalancing step it is a property of SQLite's source (`balance_nonroot` in btree.c of 3.53.3 redistributes the page with at most two siblings under one parent); several steps at a range's border could in principle reach further, which no analysed journal shows (largest distance observed: 2, at a new source's right edge; 1 beside an existing source's range). A page beyond it is reported, never waived; changing W needs a reviewed change with the journal evidence. **(b) Free space:** IMP-11. **(c) Engine stop and recovery cost** (phases 1 and 2 of CAN-03): from cancelling the save token to `ImportSnapshotAsync` returning rolled back, at the worst of four cancel points (immediately after a token check during row insertion; at the start and at the middle of the in-transaction verification; and during the final statements: after the token check that follows the verification and before IMP-11's final check, which is the last token check before `COMMIT` and detects it; a cancellation after the final check is not observed and the save publishes, CAN-01e), within **1.75 s** in every representative cell and **4.75 s** in every stress and worst-case cell; and the next start-up open after a process kill at the journal's peak within **5 s** in every cell | Measured (C4 design review §5, §8; C4 design repair): one 4,104-byte journal record per existing dictionary leaf that receives a new name (about one per new name until the region is saturated) plus 30 to 50 other pages; a new source's import under per-source dictionaries journalled 60 pages (0.24 MiB); a representative 2M re-scan 28.9 MiB; under the rule of (a), clean per-source journals pass and the Library-wide dictionary fails (5,662 pages beyond the window for a new source; 5,569 leaves shared with another source for an existing one); cancelling at the end of row insertion rolled back 28.9 MiB in 0.38 s, 22 MiB in 0.6 to 0.8 s, 89 MiB in 1.2 to 1.8 s, 120 MiB in 2.0 to 3.1 s; recovery at the next open took 0.9 s for 28.9 MiB, 0.7 s for 89 MiB, 1.4 s for 120 MiB and 3.3 s for 177 MiB. The in-transaction verification itself took 6.5 to 10.6 s at 2M, which is why CAN-01d checks the token inside it | (a) none: a page outside (a) is a defect and the outcome MISSED; (c) above **1.5×** its budget | C4 (TEST-P1: a, c), C5 (b) |
| PERF-16 | **Stop Saving at 2M files, total** (CAN-03: from the save token's cancellation to the Save lease's clean end): **≤ 2 s** in every representative cell and **≤ 5 s** in every stress and worst-case cell of §15.4, at the worst cancel point (during V, during row insertion, during the in-transaction verification, during the final statements before IMP-11's final check; PERF-15 (c)) | The engine's part is PERF-15 (c), measured in C4 (1.75 s; 4.75 s); C5 adds T-OUTCOME, the writer and spool closes and the lease's end, for which 0.25 s is reserved (projected: one one-row transaction and three closes); C5 measures the whole and reports the split | — | C5 |

`c0c031f`'s PERF-09 ("a real-world Library grows past 20 GB") could not be observed without telemetry (INV-05); it is
replaced by the local triggers of §15.5.

### 15.4 Benchmark plan, method and gates

Units in this section: MiB = 2^20 bytes, MB = 10^6 bytes.

- **C4, engine (TEST-P1).** One-transaction imports with the shipped engine on Windows, using the **full schema with
  dictionary interning**, into a Library that **already holds other snapshots and sources** (G0-O03). **Every cell is
  defined by numeric input parameters fixed before it runs** (D-53, C4 design repair): a run's workload class follows from
  its cell's parameters alone and **never from its result**. No outcome moves a cell to another class; a slow but valid
  run of a representative cell stays a representative result.
  - **The objective generator** (deterministic: every machine produces the same names; its reference implementation is
    `ObjSnapshot` in `docs/evidence/c4-design-review/harness.patch`):
    - **Tree:** the C4 generator's folders, file counts, sizes and times (about 0.25 folders per file, branching 6), with
      folder names `dir<i mod 997>`.
    - **First save F(n, d, m)**, into a **new source**, whose dictionary is empty, so that every distinct name is new to it
      (the new-name ratio equals the distinct-name ratio `d`): a vocabulary of V = round(d × n) entries; every entry occurs
      once, at the file slots that an affine permutation of the slot index maps below V; each of the other n − V slots
      repeats an entry of rank floor(V × u^β), u uniform in [0, 1), never twice in one folder.
    - **Name model m**, from the aggregate name censuses in `docs/evidence/c4-design-review/census/` (counts and length
      histograms only, never a name):
      - **system**: the reference machine's system drive, 2,583,859 files, 22.9% distinct, mean name length 20.8 UTF-16
        code units (median 13, 95th percentile 65); β = 13.5 reproduces the 55% of files that carry its most frequent 1%
        of names;
      - **data**: a data volume of the same machine, 449,727 files, 59.1% distinct, mean 34.6 (median 37); β = 2.56,
        7.3% of files in its most frequent 1%.
      An entry's length follows the model's length quantiles, assigned to ranks by a golden-ratio sequence so that the few
      most frequent names take spread lengths; an entry starts with five letters that encode a bijective scramble of its
      rank, so entries are distinct and spread uniformly over the key space, followed by filler and an extension.
    - **Re-scan R(n, d, m; ρ, δ, α)**, into the **existing** source A, whose first snapshot p1 is F(n, d, m) and whose
      second, p3, is p1 re-scanned with the routine churn (ρ = 1%, δ = α = 0.5%): the target is p1 again, with a fraction
      **ρ** of its files renamed to names new to the source (spread over the key space), a fraction **δ** deleted, and,
      beside a fraction **α** of them, one added file whose name is already in the vocabulary. The source's dictionary then
      holds about (d + 0.01) × n names (about 520,000 at 2M files with d = 25%); the retained-name ratio is 1 − ρ − δ.
    - **The Library before the import:** **L2**, three snapshots of the target's size in two sources, from the same model:
      p1 and p3 of source A as above, and p2 of source B with its own vocabulary, which shares ω = 25% of its entries with
      the target first save's vocabulary (under D-52 this touches no dictionary range of the target; it sets the duplicated
      names across sources and gives the negative control shared names); **L1**, three snapshots of 250k files.
    - Every run records the generator's realised statistics before the import (files, distinct names, mean length, names
      seen once, share of files in the top 1% of names, churn counts) and the importer's count of names new to the source.
  - **Workload classes: a total function of the cell's input parameters** (D-53; C4 design final repair, C4DRR-M01). Each
    parameter of a generator cell lies in exactly one band of its row. A row's bands are disjoint intervals that together cover
    the parameter's whole range: `d` in (0, 100%], each churn ratio in [0, 100%], with ρ + δ ≤ 100%.

    | Parameter of the cell | Representative band | Stress band | Worst-case band |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | `d`, the source's distinct-name ratio, name model **system** | 0 < d ≤ 25% | 25% < d ≤ 60% | 60% < d ≤ 100% |
    | `d`, name model **data** | none | 0 < d ≤ 60% | 60% < d ≤ 100% |
    | `ρ`, re-scan: files renamed to names new to the source | 0 ≤ ρ ≤ 1% | 1% < ρ ≤ 10% | 10% < ρ ≤ 100% |
    | `δ`, re-scan: files deleted | 0 ≤ δ ≤ 0.5% | 0.5% < δ ≤ 5% | 5% < δ ≤ 100% |
    | `α`, re-scan: files added with names already in the source | 0 ≤ α ≤ 0.5% | 0.5% < α ≤ 5% | 5% < α ≤ 100% |

    A cell's class is decided in this order, and every valid cell has exactly one:
    1. **Informational** if it belongs to a named informational family: **N1** append (C4's control, the best case) at any
       size, or any cell of more than 2M files (the 10M cells). No budget is judged on it.
    2. Otherwise **worst-case** if it belongs to a named adversarial family: **N2** interleaved or **N3** mixed names (the C4
       design review's families) into an existing source, or a re-scan of an N2 source.
    3. Otherwise (a generator cell), the **hardest band among its parameters**, in the order representative < stress <
       worst-case:
       - a first save F(n, d, m) has one parameter: its `d`, on the row of its model m;
       - a re-scan R(n, d, m; ρ, δ, α) has four: the `d` of its source, on the row of m (the dictionary it imports into), and its
         three churn ratios.

    So a generator cell is **representative** exactly when every one of its parameters is in its representative band,
    **worst-case** when any parameter is in its worst-case band, and **stress** otherwise.
    - **Parameters, not results.** The ratios are the cell's parameters, never its realised statistics. The churn draws are
      per-file Bernoulli trials, so realised ρ, δ and α only approximate the parameters, and a realised distinct share can fall
      just above a bound (C4DRR-O10); `ObjSnapshot` is the reference.
    - **Size.** The classes apply at any size. Budgets are judged only on the matrix's cells below (1M and 2M files): a cell
      outside the matrix is classified but gates nothing.
    - **Checked mechanically.** `workload_class.py` (evidence directory) evaluates this function from this section's two tables.
      It checks that the bands partition each range, and that each case below maps to exactly one class equal to the intended
      one: each boundary and the value just above it, low-churn re-scans of 25%, 60% and 100%-distinct sources, and mixed
      cases. It also checks that the matrix's Class column equals the function, with all four representative cells
      Representative. It fails on the design repair's nested-bound rule.
  - **Re-scans of high-novelty sources** (D-53). A re-scan's class includes its source's band, because the dictionary it
    imports into is part of its workload: every file name of the re-scan is looked up in the source's name range, and its new
    names land in that range's leaves.
    - A representative 2M-file source holds about 520,000 names, about half of the 15,232 name-index pages measured for L2's
      two sources, so about 30 MiB: it fits the 64 MiB import cache.
    - A 60%-distinct data-model source holds about 1.2M names, each about 1.7 times as long (inferred: about 3.5 times the pages,
      more than the cache).
    - So a routine re-scan of a high-novelty source is a **stress** save, held to PERF-14's engine target and the 5 s Stop
      budget like its first save, and a routine re-scan of a source above 60% distinct is a worst-case save.
    - This is the intended product behaviour, not a label the source inherits from its first save: the class follows the
      parameters of the dictionary the save imports into. PERF-01 is not extended to high-novelty sources.
  - **Why these boundaries** (D-53). The representative bound d ≤ 25% covers every whole source of 0.5M to 2.6M files
    measured on the reference drive (18.0% to 22.9% distinct; SRCH-02's projection for the reference drive is 12.5%).
    Smaller trees are more distinct (48% to 70% for subtrees of 27k to 200k files of the system drive; 74% to 100% on the
    data volume, which is 59% distinct as a whole), so a 2M-file source at up to 60% distinct names is a stress case and
    beyond it an adversarial one. Re-scan churn is a defined product parameter (1% renamed to new names for a routine
    re-scan), not a measurement: the census's creation-time proxy was unusable (most creation times on the system drive
    had been renewed by an operating-system upgrade), and TEST-P2 measures the real rate (C5). One workstation does not
    represent every user: the classes are the bounds the product is accepted against, not a claim about every machine.
  - **The matrix (TEST-P1).** Each cell's class is the function's value (checked by `workload_class.py`):

    | Cell | Kind | Files | Source (model, d) | ρ | δ | α | Library before | Class |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | F(1M, 25%, system) | first save | 1M | system, 25% | – | – | – | L2 | Representative |
    | F(2M, 25%, system) | first save | 2M | system, 25% | – | – | – | L2 | Representative |
    | R(1M, 25%, system; 1%, 0.5%, 0.5%) | re-scan | 1M | system, 25% | 1% | 0.5% | 0.5% | L2 | Representative |
    | R(2M, 25%, system; 1%, 0.5%, 0.5%) | re-scan | 2M | system, 25% | 1% | 0.5% | 0.5% | L2 | Representative |
    | F(2M, 60%, data) | first save | 2M | data, 60% | – | – | – | L2 | Stress |
    | R(2M, 25%, system; 5%, 2.5%, 2.5%) | re-scan | 2M | system, 25% | 5% | 2.5% | 2.5% | L2 | Stress |
    | R(2M, 25%, system; 10%, 5%, 5%) | re-scan | 2M | system, 25% | 10% | 5% | 5% | L2 | Stress |
    | R(2M, 60%, data; 1%, 0.5%, 0.5%): the routine re-scan of a high-novelty source | re-scan | 2M | data, 60% | 1% | 0.5% | 0.5% | L2 | Stress |
    | F(2M, 100%, data) | first save | 2M | data, 100% | – | – | – | L2 | Worst-case |
    | N2 into the existing source | family N2 | 2M | – | – | – | – | L2 | Worst-case |
    | N3 into the existing source | family N3 | 2M | – | – | – | – | L2 | Worst-case |
    | 1% re-scan of an N2 source | family N2 re-scan | 2M | – | – | – | – | L2 | Worst-case |
    | N1 append | family N1 | 1M; 2M; 10M | – | – | – | – | L1 | Informational |
    | N2 at 10M | family N2 | 10M | – | – | – | – | three 2M snapshots | Informational |

    The re-scan of a high-novelty source (added by the C4 design final repair) is expressed by `ObjSnapshot`'s parameters
    (`d=0.60;model=data;rho=0.01;delta=0.005;alpha=0.005`) with no generator change; its L2 holds p1 and p3 of that source and
    p2 of a second source of the same model.
  - **Budgets by class:** PERF-01 in the representative cells; PERF-14's engine target in the representative and stress
    cells, its stop threshold in every 2M cell; PERF-15 (a) in every cell; PERF-15 (c) in every representative, stress and
    worst-case cell, with its class's budget.
  - **Recorded per run:** the cell's parameters and the generator's realised statistics; the Library's size before
    (through a handle) and its growth; the journal's length through a live handle (`FileShare.ReadWrite |
    FileShare.Delete`) **immediately before `COMMIT`**, which is the reported peak (a `TRUNCATE` journal is at its largest
    there), and the sampled series with its actual largest sampling interval as a cross-check; IMP-11's quantities at
    every check (`page_count`, the main file's and the journal's lengths, `Λ`, the drop in free space) and the main file's
    growth during `COMMIT` against the final check's `Λ`; the largest interval between two save-token checks; rows/s,
    duration and phase times, peak working set and the load record (per collection: the whole machine, own and `U`, and the
    diagnostics); for the attribution, the target and the copy's length and SHA-256.
  - **PERF-15 (a), key-range attribution** (one run of every cell; journals are deterministic): the database is copied
    after T0 commits and before T-IMPORT's `BEGIN` (so that every journal record's original image equals the copy's page),
    the journal immediately before `COMMIT`.
    - **Binding.** The run's record holds the target (its source id, or a new source), the journal's length read through the
      live handle at that moment, and the copy's length and SHA-256 when it was taken. The checker takes them from the record,
      never from the analyst.
    - **Validity (C4 design final repair, C4DRR-M02).** A PERF-15 (a) attribution is valid only when the checker validates its
      evidence completely. `journal_ranges.py` in the evidence directory is the reference; a port keeps its outcomes and passes
      its negative self-tests (`journal_ranges_selftest.py`). The checker has three outcomes, with distinct exit codes:
      **PASS** (0), complete and valid evidence that meets PERF-15 (a); **FAIL** (1), complete and valid evidence that does not;
      **INVALID** (2), evidence that cannot be trusted or interpreted, and any usage or internal error. The evidence is INVALID
      when any of the following holds:
      - the journal's length differs from the recorded one;
      - the journal is empty or holds no record. No TEST-P1 cell journals nothing: every T-IMPORT of the matrix allocates pages,
        and SQLite journals page 1 before its first allocation. A 0-byte journal is what a copy taken after `COMMIT` gives, never
        pre-`COMMIT` evidence; a run declared separately to journal nothing would accept only a 0-byte journal;
      - a header is truncated, its magic is neither SQLite's nor the zero form of a segment not synced yet, or its sector size or
        page size is not valid;
      - the journal's page size, or its original database size in any segment, differs from the copy's;
      - a segment ends inside a record;
      - a record's page number is 0, beyond the original size, or repeated;
      - any checksum fails (SQLite's algorithm: the segment's nonce plus every 200th byte of the image from the end);
      - any original image differs from the copy's page, or page 1 is not journalled (its image ties the copy's change counter,
        size and free list to the journal);
      - the copy is not StorageInventory's Library: its header, `application_id`, `user_version`, quick check, its 21 trees, or a
        name index keyed otherwise than the run declares; or its SHA-256 differs from the record;
      - a page needed for placement, or a key, cannot be decoded, or a journalled page cannot be placed;
      - the target is not an existing source of the copy, or, for a new source, a key already sorts at or past it.

      **An INVALID attribution is never PASS.** That cell's PERF-15 (a) has no result (NOT MEASURED, which counts as MISSED)
      until the run is captured again and its attribution is valid; INVALID evidence is an objective invalidation of the run.
    - **Procedure.** Every record's checksum is verified and its original image compared byte for byte with the copy; every
      journalled page is placed by tree, level (the root is level 0) and position within its level (left to right), and for
      the dictionary indexes the first key column (`source_id`) of each entry and the key interval the page covers, from the
      dividers of its ancestors, are decoded; each journalled page is then **IN** (its interval intersects the target
      source's keys; for a new source, the rightmost page of its level), **ADJACENT** (outside IN, at most W positions from
      the nearest IN page of its level), **REMOTE** (further: a defect) and, separately counted, **SHARED** (it holds an
      entry of another source); PERF-15 (a) states the limits.
    - **Negative control** (required, once per TEST-P1 session): the same check on an import into a Library whose name index
      is keyed by the name alone (C4's blocked schema, or a test-only build of it), where a page's sources are those whose
      snapshots or folder paths reference its names: a new source then has no insertion point, so every journalled
      name-index page is REMOTE, and an existing source's journal holds thousands of pages shared with another source at a
      level. The check **must FAIL** on it; if it passes, the checker is defective and TEST-P1 fails; if it is INVALID, the
      control is captured again. (Measured in the C4 design repair: the per-source journals pass; the Library-wide control
      fails with 5,662 REMOTE pages for a new source and 5,569 shared leaves for an existing one. Measured again in the C4
      design final repair with the fail-closed checker: the same six verdicts and counts, and the negative self-tests below.)
  - **Cancellation and recovery (PERF-15 (c)):** in every representative, stress and worst-case cell, a cancellation at each
    of four points, each a point where a later token check still sees it and the transaction rolls back: (1) immediately after
    a token check during row insertion; (2) at the start and (3) at the middle of the in-transaction verification; (4) during
    the final statements, after the token check that follows the verification and before IMP-11's final check, which is the
    last token check before `COMMIT` and detects it (C4 design final repair, C4DRR-O04: the design repair's "immediately
    before `COMMIT` (after … IMP-11's final check)" could never roll back). Each is timed from the cancellation to
    `ImportSnapshotAsync` returning rolled back, and the cell's figure is the largest. A cancellation after the final check
    is not observed: the save publishes (CAN-01e). Also a process kill at
    the journal's peak (the start of the verification), with the next start-up open timed.
  - Also: deletion of a 2M snapshot; `SQLITE_FULL` on a really full volume (rollback, no damage); IMP-11's guard stopping an
    import (TEST-L9). Answers Q-17.
- **C5, capture (TEST-P2):** the existing 10k, 60k and 250k synthetic trees, and generated 1M and 2M trees: scan without
  saving; scan with saving (reports-ready time, saving time, spool size, bytes per observation, peak memory); traversal
  order through the spool; one real system drive, reporting aggregates only (never names). **PERF-14 end to end** (P5 to
  P7: V, the spool-reading T-IMPORT, IMP-11, the transitions) on spools written from this section's generator for every
  representative and stress cell at 2M, and for information for the worst-case cells; **PERF-16 in total**, at the worst
  cancel point (during V, during row insertion, during the verification, during the final statements before IMP-11's final
  check), by class. **Re-scan
  churn of a real drive:** two captures of the reference drive at least 7 days apart report the second's ratios of names
  new to the source, deleted files and added files (aggregates only); if the new-name ratio exceeds the representative
  ρ (1%), the representative re-scan cell is raised to the measured rate by reviewed change before C5 is accepted.
- **C10, scale (TEST-P3):** Libraries with 1, 10 and 50 snapshots of a 1M-file source; comparing latest with previous
  and latest with oldest; timeline, search, top-N; deletion; CAP-03's churn test; opening the Library.
- **Method (every benchmark of this section).**
  - **Reference machine:** §15.1's (12th Gen Intel Core i7-1270P, 32 GB, internal NVMe SSD, Windows 11), on AC power. A
    figure from another machine is labelled with that machine and is never an acceptance figure.
