| **D-33** | **Persistence ADR (§5):** SQLite retained, through `Microsoft.Data.Sqlite.Core` 10.0.12 and SQLitePCLRaw 2.1.12 `core`, `provider.e_sqlite3`, `lib.e_sqlite3` (SQLite 3.53.3), provider set explicitly; **no custom native resolver**: the runtime loads `e_sqlite3.dll` from the single-file extraction directory; the loaded module is verified at release and the engine version at every open; the pin rule of BLD-15 | DECIDED | Supersedes D-01; [E-1] |
| **D-34** | **Library location:** fixed `%LOCALAPPDATA%\StorageInventory\Library\` in 1.1; the app-data root can be neither a source nor a report folder; the Library may lie inside other sources **only under D-35** | DECIDED | Supersedes D-08 |
| **D-35** | **No Library mutation while observing:** within the process, no Library mutation overlaps an observation window, in either order (every scan; enforced by D-51); capture data goes to an out-of-source spool in the report folder; the snapshot is imported in one transaction only after the scan has finished; the Library folder is observed, not excluded, and labelled; a Library-mutation tripwire based on the interlock's mutation epoch and the database's commit generation, never on directory-listing metadata; another process's scans are limitation L-OBS1; the spool yields space to the reports under a bounded-space policy built from the run's own write counters, the measured free-space drop and a defined margin (SPOOL-24) | DECIDED (revised after G0: G0-B01, G0-M01, G0-M04, G0-M07; after the acceptance check: G0F-M06, the reserve rule only) | Supersedes D-09; REV-B01, review §12.4; re-review §5 ACCEPT WITH REVISION; [E-8] |
| **D-36** | **Concurrency:** an exclusively held lock file (cross-session), acquired before any member is inspected, created or renamed and held until the process exits; creation and set-aside only under it, with the Library's state re-derived under the lock before anything is created, so a Library that appeared since an earlier look is opened, never treated as debris or recreated over; one writer connection, open only inside a mutation lease; import, verification and publication in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction; recovery only under the lock; an in-process reader/writer gate; no named kernel object | DECIDED (revised after G0: G0-M06; window ordering by D-51; after the acceptance check: G0F-M01, re-derivation under the lock) | Supersedes D-23; REV-H03; re-review §5 ACCEPT WITH REVISION |
| **D-37** | **Engine configuration (§5.8):** rollback journal `TRUNCATE`, `synchronous=FULL` for every write, `temp_store=MEMORY`, no `VACUUM`, `VACUUM INTO` or `ATTACH` in 1.1.0, `foreign_keys=ON`, `trusted_schema=OFF`, extensions never enabled, `busy_timeout` as a backstop | DECIDED | Supersedes D-03; [E-2], [E-3], [E-5] |
| **D-38** | **Names and absence:** exact stored names; a table-driven candidate key `K(n) = R(NFD(R(n)))` from Unicode 16.0.0 data, with no OS or runtime casing or normalisation API (not a canonical-equivalence key, and never relied on as one); a **comparison capability derived from both snapshots** (DIFF-14): listing visibility `Complete` only when both snapshots are local captures of classes whose Q-23 evidence passed (every network source `Unverified`), and matching assurance `Verified` only when, in addition, both recorded the same class and its Q-14 evidence passed; a verified domain; case-fold matching only under `Complete` visibility, two Listed parents, singleton key classes and names in the verified domain, at every assurance level; Missing (Added) only under `Complete` visibility, by the name-independent proof (the exactly aligned Listed counterpart holds nothing of that type) or, on `Verified` comparisons, also by the key-based proof (no same-type child with the same key, every name concerned in the verified domain); under `Unverified` visibility never Added or Missing, and only exact-path continuity (L-DIFF3); otherwise Unknown | DECIDED (revised after G0: G0-M02; after the acceptance check: G0F-H01, G0F-M04, G0F-M05) | Supersedes D-18; REV-B02, [E-6] (rule), [E-9] (key), [E-11] (capability); re-review §5 ACCEPT WITH REVISION |
| **D-39** | **Folders on two axes:** an existence class (Added, Missing, Present, Unknown) and a separate content certainty (Exact, NotComparable, NotObserved); only Exact deltas are ranked | DECIDED | Supersedes D-20; REV-H05 |
| **D-40** | **FAT-family clock shifts:** on FAT, FAT32 and exFAT, equal size and a non-zero whole quarter-hour offset within ±14 h is Unknown (ChangeIndeterminate), never Unchanged | DECIDED | Revises D-13; REV-M05 |
| **D-41** | **Assembly boundaries:** Core (scan, observers, spool codec, identity evidence; package-free), History (identity matching, path rules, comparison, history arithmetic, oracle; package-free), Library (the SQLite adapter only, packages with `PrivateAssets="compile"`), App (UI, no compile-time SQLite reference) | DECIDED | Supersedes D-02, D-10; REV-M13 |
| **D-42** | **Identity model (§7):** six separate concepts; confidence and basis fixed per source; exact-case root key with case-variant suggestions only; clones indistinguishable unless co-mounted (documented); folder-mounted volumes are not sources; end-of-capture re-verification through a root handle opened on **the enumerated path** and held (E1, E2) and a fresh open of **the enumerated path** (E3), never of the canonical path; a minimum evidence set per source type, without which the source cannot be saved (ID-13); away-and-back re-pointing documented (L-ID2) | DECIDED (revised after G0: G0-M03) | Supersedes D-21; REV-H04, REV-M11; re-review §5 ACCEPT WITH REVISION |
| **D-43** | **Scope moves:** SREQ-04 and SREQ-08 to 1.2; SREQ-05, SREQ-06, SREQ-07, SREQ-09 and SREQ-10 to 1.1.x; the totals chart is SHOULD (SREQ-11); "Keep a copy" dropped; the automatic online sweep to 1.1.x | DECIDED | Supersedes D-24; review §16B |
| **D-44** | **Order:** snapshots are ordered by capture sequence; `snapshot_id` is never reused; clock anomalies are flagged | DECIDED | REV-M04 |
| **D-45** | **Exception classes** A to D (§11.4) | DECIDED | REV-M03, X-6 |
| **D-46** | **Visibility:** the observer interface, fan-out and spool types stay internal until the 1.2 integration design; no discovery callback in 1.1 | DECIDED | REV-M13 |
| **D-47** | **Sealed totals:** verified inside T-IMPORT and at every comparison; the committed-state check at every open | DECIDED | [E-4] |
| **D-48** | **Discovery ordinals** (`seq`, `discovery_index`) are stored so that browsing matches v1's tie order | DECIDED | REV-M09 |
| **D-49** | **Spool deletion** only through the delete-on-close handle of the creating run; stale spools are never imported or deleted automatically | DECIDED | REV-B01 (follow-up) |
| **D-50** | **Spool integrity:** the spool is append-only; one SHA-256 covers every byte before the digest, in physical order; a sequential verification pass V (digest, structure, rebuilt run index, totals) runs at P5 before any record is used, and key-ordered reads happen only after it; format 1 is unchanged; per-section digests are not adopted. V proves byte integrity relative to the seal and structural consistency, not the order of records inside one folder's run against an outside authority; that order is enforced by the writer and the exclusive handle (SPOOL-23) | DECIDED (scope of V stated after the acceptance check: G0F-M07) | G0-M05; decided before C1 builds the codec |
| **D-51** | **Library activity interlock:** one app-lifetime state machine in `LibrarySession` (Idle, Mutating, Observing, Faulted) whose leases every Library mutation and every observation window must hold; the two kinds exclude each other in both directions; it is constructed holding the start-up Open lease, so nothing that needs it starts before the Library has been examined; a capture's leases are joined by atomic hand-offs, so the next scan becomes eligible only when the previous capture's outcome is recorded; refusals rather than queues; the writer connection exists only inside mutation leases; a lease authorises work only while it is current, checked statically and at run time; Faulted is terminal and entered by every class C failure and every unclean lease end; command availability is derived from the interlock state | DECIDED (tightened after the acceptance check: G0F-M01, G0F-M02, G0F-M03) | G0-B01, G0-M04; re-review RR-01 |
| **D-52** | **The one-transaction import is kept; its dictionary cost is bounded by the source:** T-IMPORT stays one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction in rollback-journal mode, with atomic publication and no committed intermediate state. Names are interned **per source** (`UNIQUE (source_id, utf16)`), so a new source's names form a new key range and its import journals no existing dictionary page, and a known source's import touches only its own range. PERF-15 (≤ 5% of the snapshot) is replaced by the key-range confinement of the journal (PERF-15 (a)), IMP-11's space preflight and guard (with the pending main-file growth at every check, C4 design repair), and a stop and recovery budget; PERF-01 and §15.4 get an explicit method, whose workload classes and validity rules are D-53. Rejected: key-ordered interning of each chunk's names (prototyped: it moved the cost into the in-transaction verification without a net gain); keeping C4's mechanics with revised budgets (as fast on realistic names, but every import's journal, rollback and recovery, a new source's first included, grow with every source's dictionary: 88.7 MiB at a 3M-file Library, 177 MiB at 6M); batched import into a hidden staging state (the prepared alternative: it does not reduce the dictionary cost, brings back `c0c031f`'s recovery and purge rules, and leaves a failed save's space in the file); WAL, `ATTACH` or a separate temporary database (D-37) | DECIDED | C4-B01, C4-H02; C4 design review; repaired by the C4 design repair (C4DR-M01, C4DR-M02) |
| **D-53** | **Performance acceptance by objective workload classes, three saving-time numbers and per-class Stop Saving** (C4 design repair): every TEST-P1 and TEST-P2 cell is defined by numeric generator parameters fixed before it runs, calibrated to aggregate name censuses, and its class (representative, stress, worst-case, informational) follows from those parameters alone, by a **total function** (C4 design final repair, C4DRR-M01): each parameter lies in exactly one band of disjoint intervals, and a cell's class is the hardest band among its parameters, after the named informational and adversarial families; no result moves a cell between classes (§15.4). PERF-01 is judged on the representative cells: first saves at ≤ 25% distinct names on the system-drive model, and re-scans of such sources with ρ ≤ 1%, δ ≤ 0.5% and α ≤ 0.5%. High-novelty real sources (above 25% and up to 60% distinct names, or the data name model) are **stress** sources: their first saves **and their routine re-scans** are stress cells, because the dictionary a re-scan imports into is part of its workload. Re-scans of representative sources with up to 10% new names and up to 5% deleted or added files are stress cells too. Stress cells are bounded by PERF-14's engine target and the 5 s Stop budget, not by PERF-01. A re-scan's class follows the parameters of the dictionary it imports into, not a label inherited from the first save. Sources above 60% distinct, churn beyond the stress bands and adversarial distributions are worst-case cells, bounded by the stop thresholds only. PERF-14 is three numbers: C5's end-to-end 60 s, C4's engine target 45 s and C4's stop thresholds (67.5 s; 90 s in worst-case cells). Stop Saving takes at most 2 s in representative saves and 5 s otherwise, with detection within 0.5 s everywhere, verification included (CAN-01d, CAN-03, PERF-15 (c), PERF-16, UI-10). Benchmark validity rests on one whole-machine load source: the PDH processor counter, unelevated, less the benchmark's own processor time from a job object (C4DRR-M03). Per-process counters serve only for diagnosis. Quiet checks run before the session and after every round, and sessions are predeclared, with no valid run discarded. Rejected: classes chosen after the results; a cycled harvested vocabulary as the acceptance workload (its distinct share was an artefact, C4DR-H01); class rows of nested upper bounds where "the hardest row" wins (it emptied the representative class and left re-scans of high-novelty sources without a class, C4DRR-M01); one 60 s figure for the engine and the whole saving phase (C4DR-M04); a universal 2 s Stop promise that stress and worst-case saves cannot keep (C4DR-M03); an API-dependent "readable processes" load rule (C4DR-M05); summed per-process counters as the validity source (blind to short-lived processes, C4DRR-M03) | DECIDED | C4DR-H01, C4DR-M03, C4DR-M04, C4DR-M05; C4 design repair; C4DRR-M01, C4DRR-M03; C4 design final repair |

## 21. Open questions

Every question the review answered with evidence is closed here, citing it. The rest stay `OPEN` with a safe default,
the exact experiment that answers them, and the earliest gate that must resolve them. **None lets a gate choose an
architectural contract:** each default is already the specified behaviour, and each experiment can only confirm it or
trigger a stop condition that sends the question back to review.

| ID | Question | Status | Evidence or reason | Safe default | Exact experiment | Resolve by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| Q-02 | Do `GetVolumeInformationByHandleW`, `FileIdInfo` and `GetFinalPathNameByHandleW` work with 0-access handles on FAT32, exFAT, UDF, SMB (a Windows server and a Samba server) and a mapped drive, and what do they return when they succeed (for example, is the 64-bit serial the 32-bit one zero-extended on FAT)? | **PARTIALLY ANSWERED (C3)** | Measured in C3, through the product's own capture code, on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26200 and a hosted Windows Server 2025 10.0.26100 runner (`docs/v1.1-c3-implementation-evidence.md` §9). The open, `GetFinalPathNameByHandleW`, `GetVolumeInformationByHandleW`, `GetVolumePathNameW` and `GetDiskFreeSpaceExW` work with a **0-access** handle (granted mask `0x00100080`) on NTFS, ReFS, FAT32, exFAT, UDF (a mounted image and a virtual optical drive), SMB shares served by Windows from NTFS and from FAT32 (by UNC and through mapped letters) and SUBST letters. `FileIdInfo` works with 0 access on NTFS and ReFS and answers **Win32 87, not provided,** on FAT32, exFAT, UDF and a share served from FAT32, identically for access 0, `FILE_READ_ATTRIBUTES` and `GENERIC_READ`: there is no 64-bit serial on FAT, so the zero-extension question does not arise; FAT-family volumes are Moderate, UDF is PathOnly, and a source without it stays saveable because ID-13 requires it only when it succeeded at E0. A Windows SMB server reports the **underlying** volume's filesystem name and serials (recorded, never used to match a network source). **Not measured, and still open:** a physical FAT32 or exFAT stick, physical optical media, a Samba server, a Windows server on another machine, a NAS, Windows 10 | A failed call gives `PathOnly` with the reason; confidence is keyed by filesystem name, so FAT never reaches Strong; a source lacking an ID-13 item cannot be saved (reports only) | On Windows 10 and 11: a USB stick or VHDX formatted FAT32 and exFAT, a mounted ISO (UDF; Explorer's Mount needs no admin), SMB shares and a mapped letter. No real volume has been measured | **Every class `Unverified`** (NAME-04): Missing and Added need a name-independent proof (DIFF-12), so nothing unsound can be reported while the question is open | Per class, all four parts must pass. **(1) Tables:** the per-unit condition of NAME-04, checked for every code unit against the published NTFS up-case tables (XP, Vista, 7, 8 and later) and the exFAT default table, recorded as evidence (the tables are not checked into the repository). **(2) Single-unit census:** in an empty case-insensitive folder on a real volume, for every code unit legal in a name, create `x<u>` with `CreateNew`; on "already exists", open the existing file and read its real name with `GetFinalPathNameByHandle`; the collision classes are the volume's fold and must satisfy the per-unit condition. **(3) Sequence census (multi-code-point):** the same collision test for a generated list of name **pairs**: canonical reorderings of combining marks; the U+0345/U+0399/U+03B9/U+1FBE family with following marks; NFC against NFD forms; Hangul syllables against jamo; supplementary case pairs (U+10400/U+10428) as surrogate pairs; full-case-folding pairs (`ß`/`ss`, `ﬁ`/`fi`, `İ`/`i̇`); compatibility pairs (U+212A, U+212B); default-ignorable insertions (U+200B, U+00AD); unpaired surrogates. Every pair the volume treats as one name must have equal `K`. **(4) Case-sensitive directories:** repeat (2) and (3) in a directory marked case-sensitive where the runner permits; every name must be distinct. NTFS in CI (no admin); exFAT, FAT32 and ReFS (Dev Drive) manually. A class that fails any part stays `Unverified`; widening `K` instead is a reviewed change. Measured again in the C4 design review on the unmodified importer, with five name families and Library sizes of 0.75M to 6M files: the journal is one 4,104-byte record per existing dictionary leaf that receives a new name (22 MiB into a 0.75M-file Library and 89 MiB into a 3M one for interleaved names, 39 MiB for a 1% re-scan, 2.4 MiB for real-vocabulary names, 0.2 MiB for the C4 control); 36k to 164k rows/s on a loaded reference machine; cancellation rolled back 89 MiB in 1.2 to 1.8 s and 177 MiB in 3.3 to 4.0 s; a crash's hot journal of 89 MiB was recovered at the next open in 0.7 s; `SQLITE_FULL` on a really full volume (C4 hosted run 37006608313) rolled back to the original size with intact older snapshots. The §15.4 stop condition fired; D-52 is the reviewed design. A prototype of D-52's per-source dictionary on the same loaded machine: a new source's import journalled 0.24 MiB whatever the Library held (instead of 88.7 MiB at the 3M-file Library); a known source's journal was bounded by its own dictionary (120 MiB instead of 177 MiB at 2M into the 6M-file Library, same 39k rows/s); real-vocabulary first snapshots and 1% re-scans ran at 115k to 146k rows/s with 0.2 to 1.6 MiB of journal. **Corrected by the C4 design repair:** that vocabulary was a list cycled into the slots, so its distinct share was an artefact (C4DR-H01); the design review's per-source figures for an existing source were measured on prefills built with key-ordered interning (C4DR-M06, `docs/evidence/c4-design-review/ERRATUM.md`); clean reruns under load (informational): the existing-source cell (1M into 3 × 1M) journalled 62,927,656 B at 48,846 to 67,670 rows/s, and §15.4's representative cells at 2M journalled 0.24 MiB (first save) and 28.9 MiB (re-scan) at 66k to 107k rows/s | D-52 as specified | TEST-P1 (§15.4) on the repaired C4: every representative, stress and worst-case cell, with the key-range attribution of PERF-15 (a) and its negative control; the §15.4 stop condition applies | C4 |

If the Folders report exceeds its projection, or a run's drop between checks exceeds `I`, the constants are revised by reviewed change before C5 is accepted | C5 |
| Q-19 | Side effects of holding a 0-access root handle for a whole scan (removable media, network shares, SUBST and mapped letters) | **PARTIALLY ANSWERED (C3)** | Measured in C3 on Windows 11 Pro 10.0.26200 and a hosted Windows Server 2025 runner, on virtual disks (VHDX), a mounted UDF image and a vendor's virtual CD drive (no physical disc), SUBST letters, mapped letters and SMB shares served by Windows on the same machine (loopback) (`docs/v1.1-c3-implementation-evidence.md` §11; `docs/native-security-review.md`). While the handle is held: the volume lock that "Safely remove" and Eject begin with is **refused** (Win32 5), and an ordinary open folder listing refuses it too (the control), so the handle adds no new kind of effect; a mapped drive's non-forced disconnect asks the usual "open files" confirmation, as an open listing also does (the control); renaming, deleting, listing and scanning the source folder itself are not blocked and a scan's reports are byte-identical, while renaming or removing a parent or ancestor of the source folder is refused (Access denied), and an open listing refuses it identically (the control). Detected: a detached virtual disk and a dismounted image (E2 Win32 21, E3 cannot open), another medium at the same letter (E3), a re-pointed SUBST or mapped letter (E3; E2 Win32 59 after a forced disconnect), a renamed or deleted source folder and a share's backing folder renamed on the server's own filesystem, loopback, not another client (E2 and E3). Not detected, as documented: a letter re-pointed away and back, and a source folder renamed away, replaced and restored (L-ID2). **Not observed:** the physical "Safely Remove Hardware" dialog on a USB stick, a physical removal or swap, a Samba share, a remote Windows server, antivirus or indexer interplay, a handle held over a long scan. The stop condition was not met: each user-visible effect of the held handle (the refused volume lock, the mapped-drive confirmation, the refused rename of a parent folder) is produced equally by an ordinary open folder listing, which a scan always has, so the handle adds duration and no new kind of effect (independent C3 review, `docs/v1.1-c3-review.md` §15) | Hold it (opened on the enumerated path), sharing read, write and delete | Scan a USB stick while attempting "Safely remove" and while swapping it; scan a network share while another client renames the root; scan a SUBST letter while re-pointing it; record the behaviour and that E2 or E3 detects each change | C3 |
| Q-20 | Delete-on-close spools in SMB and OneDrive report folders, with antivirus, after a process kill | OPEN | Documented Win32 behaviour, not exercised here | As specified | TEST-SP3 manual runs: kill the process mid-scan; check the folder, the server and OneDrive's web view | C5 |

No stop condition | `TRUNCATE` mode; the writer closed during windows (OBS-12); a violation by a read is not detected at run time, so this is a **stop condition** for C4 | TEST-W1 on Windows. Whether directory-listing sizes and times stay current is recorded as information only: no detector uses them any more (OBS-04) | C4 |
| Q-22 | Is a managed assembly named `SQLitePCLRaw.batteries_v2.dll` placed beside the single-file exe loaded by Microsoft.Data.Sqlite's by-name lookup ([E-1])? | **RESOLVED (C4)** | The planted `SQLitePCLRaw.batteries_v2.dll` beside the single-file exe is not loaded (no assembly, no module, no marker), and the control shows the decoy is a working assembly (loaded by explicit path it writes its marker); a by-name load of it is also refused beside a normal build, because the runtime resolves assemblies from the dependency list (docs/v1.1-c4-implementation-evidence.md) | Not loaded; the provider is set explicitly (§5.4) | TEST-R2's planted-assembly case: a dummy assembly with that name beside the copied exe; list the loaded assemblies from the smoke script. If it is loaded, **stop** C4 and review the loading model | C4 |
| Q-23 | For which local filesystem classes does a listing of a folder the scanning account can read show **every** entry, whatever the entries' own permissions and attributes, so that the class may have `Complete` listing visibility (DIFF-14)? (Raised by G0F-H01's correction: every proof of absence rests on it.) | OPEN | Access-based enumeration is a feature of SMB servers, and v1 lists with `AttributesToSkip = 0` and `IgnoreInaccessible = false`, so hidden and system entries are returned and an unreadable folder is reported rather than skipped (`ScanEngine.cs:22-26`, [G0F] §2); v1's own `Complete` rests on the same premise. Not measured on any volume | **Every class `Unverified`** (an empty complete-listing list): no comparison reports Added or Missing until a class's evidence passes, and network sources stay `Unverified` whatever this question finds | Per class, on a real volume, with v1's enumerator as the scan uses it: in one folder create a plain file, a hidden and system file, a file and a subfolder whose ACL denies the scanning account every access right (NTFS and ReFS; the owner can set such a DACL without admin), and a subfolder the account cannot list; list the folder; every entry must appear (the unlistable subfolder as an entry, then Unreadable when entered). NTFS in CI; ReFS (Dev Drive), FAT32, exFAT and UDF (a mounted ISO with hidden entries) manually. A class with any missing entry stays `Unverified`; NTFS failing **stops** C7 (a product question for review) | C7 |
| Q-24 | The constants of IMP-11's Library space rule: the growth projection (110 B per file, 120 B per folder, twice the name bytes), the journal projection (twice the source's dictionary key bytes plus 16 B per name and 24 B per folder path), the margin (max(256 MiB, 1% of capacity), at most 2 GiB), the 4,096-row cadence, the 16 MiB floor of the interval allowance and the 1 MiB commit reserve | OPEN | Defined from the C4 design review's measurements (growth 70 to 139 B per file, about 2 × name bytes + 25 B per new name, 30 to 50 fixed journal pages) and the C4 design repair's instrumented runs (the main file's growth at `COMMIT` equal to the final check's pending growth `Λ_f` to the byte in every run; largest growth per 4,096 rows about 1.5 MB in representative and stress cells, 11.3 MB in a worst-case cell, as averages over the largest 16,384-row interval scaled by 1/4, not 4,096-row maxima), on synthetic trees with 0.25 folders per file; not measured on real captures | The defined values; the guard (IMP-11 (3)) and `SQLITE_FULL` keep a projection that is too small safe | C5: for every TEST-P1 cell and every TEST-P2 capture, the measured growth and peak journal must not exceed `G_p` and `J_p`, every interval's growth must stay within `Λ_k` + `I_L` and `COMMIT`'s within `Λ_f` + `R_C`; also an all-unique long-name cell (F(2M, 100%, data)), a small snapshot of all-new names into a large source (50k files into the representative 2M source) and the rollback time after a guard stop; any excess revises the constant, by reviewed change | C5 |

## 22. Review repair dispositions

§22.1 and §22.2 record the repair of `456b233`; §22.3 records the corrections of `121358c` after the G0 re-review;
§22.4 records this revision's corrections after the acceptance check.
The review (`docs/v1.1-preproduction-review.md` at `d7f91bf`) was treated as evidence, not authority. Each finding was
located in the `c0c031f` text, checked against the repository and primary sources ([E-1] to [E-7]), and repaired in the
authoritative sections listed. Where the repair departed from the review's proposal, the reason and the evidence are
given. No finding is left open and none was rejected. Where a later correction (§22.3) changed a repaired rule, the
tables below still describe the repair of `456b233`; the current rule is the one in the authoritative section.

### 22.1 Findings

| Finding | Confirmed? | Disposition | Where it is repaired | Departure from the review's proposal, with evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| REV-B01 D-09 writes inside the tree being enumerated | Yes (spec text; `ScanEngine.cs:190`; review P4, P5) | **Repaired, different mechanism** | D-35; §2.2 (RX-01, INV-17 to INV-21); §6.5; §10; §11.6 to §11.8; §16.1 | (1) Rollback journal instead of WAL plus an anchor connection: reads write nothing [E-2], so no anchor is needed; (2) the window extends to the scan's return, a superset; (3) the tripwire fingerprints every Library entry, not only the main file; (4) the spool is deleted on close rather than left like v1's temporary file; (5) no in-memory spool in 1.1.0 |
| REV-B02 `ProvenAbsent` unsound on case-insensitive directories | Yes, and larger than reported ([E-6]: 2,482 to 2,728 unsound verdicts per 20,000 worlds) | **Repaired, stricter than proposed** | D-38; §8.3 (NAME-01 to NAME-08); §12.2; §12.7; T33 to T48; DIFF-P2, DIFF-P9, DIFF-P10; §12.11; Q-14 | The review's §10.6 clause (ii) and its "unmatched" qualifier are unsound when the key is wider than the filesystem's fold or case sensitivity changed ([E-6]: 45 to 71 unsound per 20,000 worlds). This revision requires an empty same-type candidate set, singleton key classes for case-fold matching, and two Listed parents; 0 unsound in 300,000 worlds |
| REV-H01 SEC-13 pins the wrong directory | Yes (`docs/release.md`; single-file docs; provider has no resolver [E-1]) | Repaired as proposed | §5.6; SEC-13; A-10; BLD-07; BLD-10; TEST-R2; Q-11 | — |
| REV-H02 a WAL Library treated as one file | Yes; the rollback-mode analogue confirmed too ([E-4], [E-5]) | Repaired as proposed, adapted to the rollback journal | §6.3; §6.6 to §6.8; LIB-02; LIB-07; LIB-08; LIB-13; D-28; SEC-08 | — |
| REV-H03 per-session exclusion; verification outside the transaction | Yes (`Local\` namespace; spec §10.2) | Repaired as proposed | D-36; §6.10 (CONC-01 to CONC-10); §10.3; IMP-05; IMP-06 | The import is one transaction, so there is no committed staging state left to purge |
| REV-H04 identity never re-verified | Yes (`InventoryScanner.cs:50`, `ScanEngine.cs:80`) | Repaired as proposed | ID-10; §10.2 (P2, P4); O11; T38; TEST-I5 | — |

### 22.3 G0 re-review dispositions (G0-B01 to G0-O10, RR-01 to RR-10)

The re-review (`docs/v1.1-preproduction-rereview.md` at `35c841d`, not in this tree, G0-O01) was treated as evidence,
not authority, and each finding was re-derived against this text and v1's code before it was repaired. Its §15
numbering (RR-01 to RR-10) is used throughout this document. The correction brief for this revision listed the same
work under a different numbering; the last column of the RR table maps it, so that nothing is skipped under either
numbering.

**How G0-B01 is closed.** The failure needed a scan's window to open while a Library write was already running
(G0-B01 modes 1 and 3), or a capture's import to be refused because a later scan's window had opened (mode 2). Now:
(1) every Library mutation, StorageInventory's or SQLite's on its behalf, happens only inside a mutation lease
(OBS-08), and the type rule A-25 makes a mutation without a lease impossible to write; (2) a window opens only from
Idle or by a capture's own atomic hand-off, and a mutation lease is granted only from Idle or by a capture's own
hand-off (OBS-07), both decided in one monitor section (OBS-09), so neither order of overlap can occur; (3) a capture
holds the interlock from T0 to its recorded outcome through hand-offs (OBS-10), so no scan can start while a capture
saves, and no capture can lose its import or outcome to a later scan; (4) the UI derives every command from the same
state (UI-13), and a request that races a state change is refused with nothing started (OBS-11, O28, O29); (5) a
defect in any of this would still be caught before publication by the mutation epoch and the commit generation
(INV-19, OBS-04). The failing sequence of the re-review (import running, New scan of `C:\`) is TEST-W5, which also
pauses a deletion, an open-time recovery and a set-aside. The cross-instance case is out of one process's control and
is stated as L-OBS1 (G0-M01).

**Findings.**

| Finding | Confirmed? | Disposition | Where it is repaired | Departure from the re-review's proposal, with reason |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| G0-B01 A window can open while a Library write runs | Yes (OBS-02 and CONC-09 constrained writes only; `MainViewModel.Scanning.cs:36`) | **Closed** (see above) | D-51; INV-17; OBS-01 to OBS-13; CONC-07; CONC-09; LIFE-07; §10.1 to §10.4 (O28 to O30); UI-10; UI-13; A-16; A-25; TEST-W2; TEST-W5; TEST-U2 | Refusal instead of queuing: a queued scan would start at a moment the user did not choose, and a refusal is simpler to prove. No "stop saving and scan" action, so no extra O-row is needed for it |
| G0-M01 INV-17 enforced per process, stated for every scan | Yes | **Closed** | INV-17 and OBS-01 scoped to the process; CONC-02 and the In use message state L-OBS1; TEST-K1 | The optional change note in the non-owner's results is **not adopted**: it could rest only on listing metadata, which G0-M04 shows is unreliable while the owner holds files open |
| G0-M02 The key is not width-preserving by construction, nor pinned | Yes; reproduced on Unicode 16.0.0 ([E-9]: 147 of 100,000 fold-equal pairs split by the old key, 0 by the new) | **Closed, stricter than proposed** | NAME-03 (`R(NFD(R(n)))`, Unicode 16.0.0 tables, no OS or runtime API, key version); NAME-04 (per-unit condition; matching assurance); NAME-09 (verified domain); DIFF-11; DIFF-12; L-DIFF2; T47; T49 to T53; DIFF-P4; DIFF-P10; DIFF-P11; Q-14 (tables, single units, multi-code-point sequences, case-sensitive directories); TEST-D1; TEST-D5; TEST-D6; [E-9] | The proposal kept `K` as the proof for every source and documented SMB folds as a residual. This revision proves absence through `K` only where the filesystem class is verified (`Verified`), and elsewhere only by a name-independent proof (an exactly aligned listing holding nothing of that type), so uncertain matching can never produce Missing. The U+0345 case is described as a proof gap, not an NTFS defect ([G0 R2]) |
| G0-M03 ID-10 re-verifies the canonical root, not the enumerated path | Yes (`InventoryScanner.cs:50`) | **Closed** | §7.2 (handle on the enumerated path); ID-10 (E1, E2, E3 roles; the question answered); ID-13 (minimum evidence; no saving without it); §7.6 rows; L-ID2; D-42; TEST-I5; Q-02; Q-19 | Mid-scan checks between top-level folders are **not adopted**: they would narrow L-ID2 without closing it and would add periodic I/O on network sources; L-ID2 is documented instead |
| G0-M04 The tripwire relies on listing metadata | Yes (the spec's own §8.1 citation; [G0 R4]) | **Closed, by a different mechanism** | OBS-04 (mutation epoch, authoritative; commit generation, secondary; listing metadata not used); OBS-12 (writer only inside leases); INV-19; §6.5 rules 4 and 6; OBS-03 reworded; TEST-W1; TEST-W3; Q-21; [E-8] | The proposal compared listing and handle metadata. Evidence tied to StorageInventory's own operations is exact for its own mutations and needs no filesystem metadata; the header generation adds commits by any connection. The writer is closed during windows, as proposed |
| G0-M05 The spool digest cannot be computed while reading in key order | Yes | **Closed** | D-50; SPOOL-06; SPOOL-07; SPOOL-12 (exact coverage); SPOOL-20 (order and trailer); SPOOL-23 (pass V); IMP-05; CAN-01d; PERF-14; TEST-SP1; TEST-SP4; TEST-SP7 (C1) | As proposed (one sequential pass; format 1 unchanged); V also rebuilds and compares the run index, so a run split across folders, records moved between folders and reordered run-index entries are rejected even with a recomputed digest. *Corrected after G0F-M07:* `121358c` said "reordered records" without qualification; two records of the same folder exchanged within its run, with the digest recomputed, are not detectable by V (SPOOL-23) |

### 22.4 Acceptance-check dispositions (G0F-H01, G0F-M01 to G0F-M07)

The acceptance check (`docs/v1.1-preproduction-final-check.md` at `509eccf`) was treated as evidence, not authority.
Each of its eight findings was re-derived against the `121358c` text, v1's code and SQLite's source, and reproduced
where a probe could do so ([E-9] addendum, [E-10], [E-11]) before it was repaired. The scope is exactly those eight;
the accepted architecture (SQLite, rollback `TRUNCATE`, the `%LOCALAPPDATA%` Library, the out-of-source spool, the
post-observation atomic import, exact names, strict Missing and Unknown, single-writer ownership, the internal
observer API, full snapshots, the C1 to C13 structure) is not reopened, and no decision is added: D-35, D-36, D-38,
D-50 and D-51 are revised in place.

| Finding | Confirmed? | Disposition | Where it is repaired | Departure from the check's proposal, with reason |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **G0F-H01** Network listings are not proof of absence (access-based enumeration) | Yes; on the model, `121358c`'s rule gave about 4,600 unsound verdicts per 20,000 network world pairs ([E-11]; T54) | **Closed** | DIFF-14 (listing visibility from both snapshots); DIFF-11 step 2.2; DIFF-12 (`ProvenAbsent` needs `V = Complete`; reason `VisibilityUnverified`); §12.3 and §12.4 notes; L-DIFF3; INV-16; REQ-06; ID-09; §12.1; §13.2; §14.4; UI-04; T47, T50, T51, T54 to T56; DIFF-P4, DIFF-P10, DIFF-P13; TEST-D5 (network worlds and their control), TEST-D9, TEST-U3; Q-23; D-38 | (1) Symmetric, as proposed: no Added either. (2) Case-fold matching is also off under unverified visibility, because its uniqueness test is a claim about a complete listing; continuity is claimed only for exact paths seen in both scans. (3) Visibility is a capability of the comparison, not a flag for network sources: local classes get it only from evidence (Q-23, default none), and local filesystems outside the in-box classes, which may be backed by a server, never. (4) The reason is named `VisibilityUnverified`. (5) A type change at the same exact name, which no hidden entry could contradict, is still Unknown, to keep one mechanical rule |
| **G0F-M01** Creation can treat an existing Library as debris; scans can start before the start-up open | Yes (LIB-07 steps 4 and 8 at `121358c`; nothing held the interlock before the start-up open) | **Closed** | OBS-14 (start-up Open lease held from construction); OBS-07; OBS-09; LIB-07 steps 1, 3 to 5 and 8 (re-derivation under the lock); LIB-08 introduction and steps 2 and 9; §6.8 Not created; §10.1; P1; OBS-08 (Prepare); OBS-10; UI-13; TEST-K1 (sequential case), TEST-W2, TEST-U2; D-36; D-51 | As proposed (an existing main file is opened, not created over; the App holds the Open lease before any command). Added: an implicit first save never recreates a Library that existed (Missing), which the sequential case also reaches. The start-up phase is the interlock's existing Mutating(Open) state, entered at construction; no state is added |
| **G0F-M02** Faulted entry undefined; leases return to Idle | Yes (OBS-09 and §10.1 at `121358c` ended every lease at Idle) | **Closed** | OBS-13 (rewritten: clean end, terminal Faulted, best-effort release, no further write, commands, artefacts, message, closing); OBS-07; OBS-09; OBS-10; CAT-03; CAT-04; §10.1; O18, O19, O30; §6.8 Unavailable; UI-13; §14.3; §9.4 (207 reserved); TEST-W2, TEST-T1, TEST-T5, TEST-U2; D-51 | Wider than proposed: one authoritative outcome for every class C failure, during an observation and in App services too, because `121358c` advised a restart after a class C scan failure while returning to Idle. No T-OUTCOME after the fault: the attempt becomes Interrupted at the next open, so no recovery is attempted in a suspect process |

**Observations of the acceptance check.** G0F-O01 is recorded as the housekeeping note in §1.1. G0F-O02 is adopted
(NAME-03, the capability table and [E-9] finding 5 corrected, with the reproduced 34 code points in the [E-9]
addendum; the key is unchanged). G0F-O04 is adopted (§12.8 step 2), because the corrected proof rule depends on it.
G0F-O08's trust premise for the reported filesystem name is stated (NAME-04); its other two parts are not addressed.
G0F-O03 (C1 codec layout), G0F-O05 (L-ID2 wording), G0F-O06 (the In use notice when no lock is held; after OBS-14 and
LIB-07 step 4 a second instance's first save finds In use under the lock) and G0F-O07 (a failed header read; the
order of M2 and the epoch increment) are outside this revision's scope and stay with C1, C3, C4 and C5 respectively.

**G0-B01 is still closed.** Re-checked after these corrections: (1) a scan cannot start during a Library mutation: a
window opens only from Idle or a capture's own hand-off (OBS-07), the interlock is never Idle before the start-up
open has ended (OBS-14) and never again after Faulted (OBS-13); (2) a Library mutation cannot begin during a
conflicting observation: a mutation lease is granted only from Idle or by hand-off (OBS-07), and a lease that is no
longer current authorises nothing (OBS-15); (3) save, delete, open and recovery, creation (including the new
open-instead-of-create path, which runs inside the same Prepare or Create lease), set-aside and every later kind are
mutation leases (OBS-08); (4) command state cannot bypass the service invariant: UI-13 only derives availability, and
a racing request is refused (OBS-11); (5) the run-time checks cannot bypass it either: OBS-15 can only refuse work,
never grant a lease, and a failed check makes the interlock more restrictive (Faulted). No transition was added that
reaches Idle, Observing or Mutating from a state the `121358c` table did not allow; the only new transitions lead
into Faulted, which grants nothing.

**Totals.** Findings: 8 dispositioned (G0F-H01 and G0F-M01 to G0F-M07 closed); 0 open; 0 rejected. Observations: 3
adopted (O01 as a note, O02, O04), 1 in part (O08), 4 left to their gates. New open question: Q-23, with a
conservative default.

### 22.5 C4 design-review, re-review and repair dispositions (C4-B01, C4-H02, C4DR-H01, C4DR-M01 to C4DR-M06)

The C4 review (`docs/v1.1-c4-review.md` at `035dc90`, verdict `C4 BLOCKED`) found that the §15.4 C4 stop condition
had fired (C4-B01) and that the PERF-01 acceptance statement had no stated criterion (C4-H02). The design review
(`docs/v1.1-c4-design-review.md`) reproduced C4-B01, explained it and took D-52. The other C4 findings (C4-H01, C4-M01
to C4-M17) are implementation findings, unchanged by this review and still required as the review states them.

| **C4-H02** "meets PERF-01" not supported | Yes | **Method decided** (its methodological part): what is measured, target and stop threshold, the cells (now objective classes, D-53), run count, warm-up, fresh process, cache state, machine record, one load source, quiet checks, predeclared sessions, the median, the validity and replacement rules, hosted CI's role, outcome classes | PERF-01; §15.3 (stop-threshold column); §15.4 (method, outcomes) | A measurement by that method in a declared gate session on a quiet reference machine (D-R4, D-R5); until then PERF-01 is NOT MEASURED |

### Gate re-evaluation after the corrections

Only the gates the brief names are re-evaluated; C2, C6 and C8 to C13 are unchanged from §19.1.

|---|---|---|---|
| **C1** | READY, with the TEST-SP7 erratum before its acceptance | **READY** | None. TEST-SP7 now tests what V proves (byte integrity and structure) and asserts the same-folder boundary; SPOOL-23 and D-50 say what V does not prove; no codec or format change, so no C1 contract is left to invent |
| **C3** | READY WITH EXPLICIT PLATFORM GATE (Q-02, Q-13, Q-19) | **READY WITH EXPLICIT PLATFORM GATE** | Q-02, Q-13, Q-19, answered inside C3. Untouched by the eight corrections, except that `snapshot.fs_name` (DIFF-14) records the filesystem name ID-13 already reads |
| **C4** | BLOCKED until G0F-M01, G0F-M02, G0F-M03 and TEST-L8 | **READY WITH EXPLICIT PLATFORM GATE** | The start-up Open lease and lock-first re-derivation (OBS-14, LIB-07 step 4), the terminal Faulted state (OBS-13), the run-time lease check and A-25 (a) and (c) (OBS-15), and TEST-L8 are specified. Platform probes inside C4: Q-05, Q-07, Q-08, Q-11, Q-12, Q-17, Q-21, Q-22 |
| **C5** | BLOCKED until G0F-M06 and the capture-path parts of G0F-M02 and G0F-M03 | **READY WITH EXPLICIT PLATFORM GATE** (after C1, C3 and C4, as §19.1) | The capture-path failure lifecycle (O18, O19, CAT-04, `CaptureScanOutcome.Catastrophic`), lease enforcement on the capture path (`RunCapture`'s permit, `InventoryRun`'s lease, A-25 (b), OBS-15) and the bounded-space policy (SPOOL-24, TEST-SP8) are specified. Platform probes inside C5: Q-18 (sizes and the reserve's constants), Q-20 (delete-on-close) |
| **C7** | READY WITH EXPLICIT PLATFORM GATE for the key and engine; the DIFF-12 tiering waited for G0F-H01, G0F-M04, G0F-M05 | **READY WITH EXPLICIT PLATFORM GATE**, in parallel with C1 to C3 | The core engine (alignment, `K`, `Dom`, the oracle, the world model) needs no open question. The final Added and Missing semantics are fixed: the network visibility rule (L-DIFF3, DIFF-P13), the capability from both snapshots (DIFF-14), the corrected proof rule and DIFF-P11 (T53, T59, T60). Which comparisons may assert Added or Missing is decided by evidence inside C7: Q-03, Q-14 and Q-23, each defaulting to the conservative answer |

### Verdict

Against the conditions set for this correction:

| Condition | Met? |
|---|---|
| G0F-H01 is fully closed | Yes: no comparison whose listing visibility is unverified (every network source) can report Added or Missing, in either direction (DIFF-12, DIFF-14, DIFF-P13, L-DIFF3); on the model, 0 in 60,000 network world pairs ([E-11]) |
| All seven medium findings are incorporated | Yes: §22.4, each into its authoritative sections, with no errata section |
| G0-B01 remains closed | Yes: §22.4 re-check; the only new transitions lead into Faulted, which grants nothing |
| No new architectural decision | Yes: no decision added; D-35, D-36, D-38, D-50 and D-51 revised in place; the accepted architecture is unchanged. Two schema columns (`snapshot.capture_kind`, `snapshot.fs_name`), two failure codes (105, 106), four new rule IDs in existing families (OBS-14, OBS-15, DIFF-14, DIFF-P13), one limitation (L-DIFF3) and one open question with a conservative default (Q-23) carry the corrections |
| C1 needs no architecture invention | Yes |
| C4 and C5 require at most explicit platform gates | Yes: Q-05, Q-07, Q-08, Q-11, Q-12, Q-17, Q-21, Q-22 (C4); Q-18, Q-20 (C5) |
| C7 cannot produce unsound Added or Missing claims | Yes, under the stated premises (Q-14's containment for `Verified` classes; Q-23's listing completeness for `Complete` classes, both empty by default): DIFF-12 and DIFF-14; [E-11] found 0 unsound verdicts in 240,000 world pairs, and TEST-D5 with its four controls is C7's gate |

## `READY FOR FINAL EIGHT-ITEM RE-CHECK`

**Conditions attached to the verdict:**

1. This is a readiness verdict for the **final re-check of G0F-H01 and G0F-M01 to G0F-M07** that the acceptance check
   names, not acceptance of the specification and not implementation authority. No gate starts before that re-check
   accepts this revision (C1 and C2 may be authorised earlier by the owner, as before).
2. Before this specification becomes implementation authority, the canonical pre-production branch must contain the
   review documents of `v1.1/preproduction-review`, `v1.1/preproduction-rereview` and
   `v1.1/preproduction-final-check` (repository housekeeping, §1.1; this revision merges nothing).
3. After G0, C1, C2, C3 and C7 may start in parallel; the others follow §19.1.
4. No v1.1 implementation exists. This revision changes no code, test, build file, package reference, existing v1
   document or review document; it changes only this file and appends to the non-normative
   `docs/v1.1-preproduction-repair-evidence.md` (an addendum to E-9, and E-10 and E-11).
